# Dimension-four homogeneous dependence: Lean formalization

Source: Jie Wang and Dan Yan, *Linear dependence and tameness for homogeneous
nilpotent-Jacobian maps in dimension four*, dated November 10, 2026,
`dimension_four_dependence.pdf` (32 pages).

Lean proofs cover Theorems 1.1, 1.2, and 6.1 over every characteristic-zero
field, the numbered auxiliary results, and both examples in Section 5.6.
Import `DimensionFourDependence` to use the public statements.
Lemma 6.2 is now checked in its full arbitrary-polynomial-parameter form,
including affine coordinate changes, by explicit tame factorizations over
the original characteristic-zero field. Propositions 6.3, 6.4, 6.6,
6.7 and 6.11, Lemmas 6.8 and 6.9, and Corollary 6.10 are checked.
The original-field ternary classification in Theorem 6.1 supplies the
last input to Theorem 1.2. The checked `theorem_1_2 : TamenessTheorem K`
constructs an actual polynomial automorphism, proves one-stable tameness
in every positive degree, and proves ordinary tameness in the independent
normalized ternary branch and through degree five.
The latest local validation counts and axiom dependencies are in
[`validation/audit.json`](validation/audit.json).
All exported theorem declarations have complete proofs. No `sorry`, new axioms,
or unproved classification results are used. The checked theorem
`theorem_1_1 : DependenceTheorem K` proves the exact original-field
assertion in every positive homogeneous degree.

Lemma 2.2 is now checked, including coefficient constancy and the degree bound
for every geometric generic-fiber component over the full output field `K(H)`
in the original four source coordinates. Generic fibers are represented by
the prime of relations of the source coordinates over `K(H)`; components are
minimal primes after extending to algebraically closed algebraic constants.

Proposition 2.3 is checked. Its fixed-plane case uses geometric comparison,
and its kernel-direction-image case follows from the stronger Theorem 1.1.
Corollary 5.3 is checked in both the fixed-coordinate chart and the
original coordinates. Each geometric generic component is a line or a
plane conic, and multiplication by the roots of unity acts freely, so
the homogeneous degree divides the number of components. Section 5.6's
homogeneous example has rank three and the displayed generic parabola.
The positive-characteristic example has independent components and a
nilpotent Jacobian of rank three.

The original hypersurface has a Zariski parameter space of all its lines,
with an open skewness relation. The developable classification identifies
the entire image ideal with the ideal of the ruling family and constructs
either a fixed vertex or a nonplanar characteristic curve with the stated
tangent rulings. The actual projective boundary has a proper closed image
closure generated by finitely many boundary primes.

For Lemma 4.4, `lemma_4_4` starts with the resolved pencil and constructs
an original prime divisor of the numerator over the special value. The
proper birational map lifts that divisor with isomorphic stalk, and the
residue of the projective map retains the original image ideal. The proof
specializes its Hilbert function to bound the special image degree by the
generic image degree. The generic hyperplane degree is expressed as the
projective image degree times the degree of the function-field extension.

The four standard projective coordinate ratios define the hyperplane
line bundle and its pullback on the normalized pencil.
`extendedClosedGaussHyperplaneCategoricalPullbackIso` identifies the
frame construction with the categorical pullback.
`extended_closed_gauss_pencil_pullback_hyperplane_affine_cover` supplies
global coordinate sections whose nonvanishing loci are the four affine
charts, proving ampleness by the affine cover criterion.
[Stacks Project, Definition 28.27.1](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/01PS).

`extended_closed_gauss_pencil_hyperplane_square_degree` computes the
hyperplane self-intersection by the projective pushforward degree. The
normalization contributes generic degree one and the image hypersurface
has Hilbert degree equal to the degree of the pencil equation.
`actual_categorical_pencil_global_section_multiplicity_bound` bounds the
order of every nonzero global section at each prime divisor by that same
degree, using the regular chart coefficients.

Proposition 3.1 is now checked over arbitrary characteristic-zero coefficient fields:
the original Gauss-dimension-two and nonlinear geometric-fiber hypotheses
produce a quadratic defining equation, an exact determinant identity in
invertible linear coordinates over an algebraic closure, and nonzero gradient
at every nonzero vector there and over the original field. The proof constructs
all auxiliary data from the original map and an actual nonlinear geometric fiber.

Section 4.1 is also checked over the original characteristic-zero field.
The actual Gauss curve supplies a source-line function-field embedding, a
rational parameter, and a primitive polynomial covector. Zero radial pairing
then forces component dependence. Proposition 4.1 is now checked over the
algebraically closed original field used in the nonlinear-fiber argument.
An actual nonlinear geometric generic component with Gauss-image dimension
at most one forces both a constant component relation and a linear image
equation. The proof constructs its finite characteristic-root coefficients,
its own root divisor, the same-branch characteristic containment, and the
contradictory source and characteristic degree bounds.

Lemma 5.2 is now checked for both rational ruling parameters in the
paper's Segre coordinates. Actual projective dominance supplies the unit
primitive output and nonzero Wronskian; the proof constructs its entire
original divisor chart and differential contradiction internally.
The original nonlinear geometric fiber now also constructs two distinct
nonradial points at infinity, one on each of the two rulings through its
actual generic output. Section 5.2 is now checked: each selected point has its normalized original
coordinates over a finite extension of its own ruling parameter alone.
Both parameter fields are explicitly isomorphic to `K(T)`. All coordinate
functions, coefficients, poles, field embeddings and algebraicity are derived
from the original hypotheses.
Section 5.3 is now checked over closed original constants: the nonzero
radial-pairing branch is impossible, so the actual first fiber invariant
vanishes. Section 5.4 and Proposition 5.1 are also checked. The original
nonlinear geometric generic-fiber branch of Theorem 1.1 is now complete
over algebraically closed original constants: every such component forces
component dependence. The entire general-degree rank-at-most-two branch is
also now checked over every original characteristic-zero field. The
constant-direction rank-three case is now closed by the proved homogeneous
ternary theorem, and the full Theorem 1.1 is checked over the original
field.
For Proposition 2.1, the positive-degree classified-direction argument is
now checked over every original characteristic-zero field, including
both vertical-derivative cases and inverse source-target coordinate
transport. The entire positive-degree primitive-kernel case is now checked
over closed original constants, including the primitive four-variable
Gordan-Noether span bound and construction of the binary two-axis normal
form. The original independent rank-three map therefore constructs a
nonzero constant right-kernel direction. Its homogeneous three-variable
coordinate reduction is now checked: the actual inverse matrices,
all four ternary forms, and their leading nilpotent Jacobian block are
constructed over the original field. A remaining independent rank-three
map constructs an independent ternary nilpotent map of rank exactly two
and degree at least two. The homogeneous three-variable dependence
theorem is now proved over every original characteristic-zero field,
excluding that remaining counterexample and closing the rank-three case.
The general homogeneous rank-two map now constructs a polynomial
factorization `H_i = g h_i(p,q)`: the coprime source forms `p,q`, their
common positive degree, the homogeneous binary forms `h_i`, the nonzero
homogeneous factor `g`, and the exact source degree equation are derived.
The nilpotent rank-two dependence conclusion is now checked in arbitrary
positive degree over every original characteristic-zero field. A
hypothetical independent original rank-two
nilpotent map now also constructs its own source point, inverse
coordinates with two normalized Jacobian columns, and a point-normalized
primitive pencil. Its first-order data give the inactive double
divisors, and the actual nilpotent traces force the active numerator
minor. A simple homogeneous prime numerator factor is constructed;
its base ideal with the denominator contains all transformed outputs,
and it does not divide the active output. The actual prime quotient now
constructs a nonzero Hamiltonian derivation and a projection relation.
Its nonzero constant term gives a homogeneous equation in the two fixed
coordinates inside the actual base ideal. An original height-two minimal
base prime containing a nonzero fixed-plane linear form is constructed.
The finite original base components now construct their entire
constant covector spaces, each of dimension at most two. A single actual
normalized frame avoids every nonzero original linear form in every
component. Two actual primitive factorizations of the same map differ
by a polynomial unit, so their primitive bases agree. This comparison
survives the actual inverse coordinates and closes the full rank-two
contradiction. Coefficient extension and descent give the entire
rank-at-most-two dependence branch over the original field. The full homogeneous four-variable
quasi-translation span bound is now checked even without primitivity;
its component-dependence conclusion descends to every original
characteristic-zero field. Thus the `(JH)^2 X=0` branch is complete.
The preceding geometric reduction is also checked: a primitive direction
has no zero anywhere on the actual geometric generic fiber, an affine-line
component forces `(Ju)u=0`, and this constructs the polynomial translations
`X+t u` with inverses `X-t u`. Over closed original constants, the checked
nonlinear branch supplies the line hypothesis from the original rank-three
map and independence alone.

The actual closed Gauss cover now embeds in each actual graph coefficient
field, preserving its entire original-source realization. Equations (4.1)
and (4.2), the modified cubic boundary equation, and the Euler weight of
`n c` are constructed from the original homogeneous map. For an actual
parameter-algebraic residue boundary direction, the original quadratic and
cubic fiber equations derive ruling membership, direction constancy and
`D_b^2(n c)=0`. The original map now also constructs its own nonzero
projective boundary point and normalized residue direction. Its residue
field is algebraic over the entire coefficient field. Its normalized
boundary coordinates now generate a finite extension of the smaller
original Gauss parameter field inside that residue field. The original
map constructs this finite ruling branch and `D_b^2(n c)=0` together,
without a supplied boundary point or parameter-algebraicity assumption.
The actual algebraic boundary coefficient extension now also contains
finite parameter-coefficient forms n,m,s and a nonzero root rho satisfying
`n(Y)c=m(Y)rho`, `rho^d=s(Y)`, `s(b)=0` and
`rho=n(Y)c/m(Y)`. Its dimension, directional and Euler derivations,
commutator, nonzero transverse coordinate and weights are all derived
from the original map. These identities now transfer to the actual
normalized pencil function field, retaining the exact second-graph-
derivative numerator coefficients. The second remaining root case
constructs a factorization whose distinguished vector spans the actual
chosen characteristic line.

For a specified fourth-axis vertex over an algebraically closed
characteristic-zero field, the original map now constructs its own finite
coefficients, nonzero affine root and complete cone formula (4.7), including
the nonzero last coefficient. The compatible boundary contradiction and
all original invariant values are instantiated together. On every actual
geometric generic component the same compatible source realization now
gives the full cone formula with its nonzero root in the geometric
constants. Actual nonlinear vertex-parabolic components are excluded in
both reduced-pencil cases, with the pencil and cone data constructed
internally. An actual parabola component containing the fixed fourth-axis
point at infinity is now excluded without an explicit parametrization.
The same original-map hypotheses now construct a full-fiber Hilbert
degree at most two over the constructed coefficient field. A nonlinear
original fiber is a parabola, and each actual nonlinear geometric
generic-fiber component is also a parabola. The original fixed vertex
now belongs to the same full-fiber closure. Compatible embeddings give
vertex-parabola coordinates on an actual nonlinear geometric component,
and the checked vertex-parabola exclusion rules it out. A specified
geometric component now transports through arbitrary invertible linear
source-target conjugacy over the same geometric constants. Consequently
every nonzero constant common vertex is excluded when a nonlinear
geometric component is present. Such a component now constructs the
moving nonplanar characteristic branch with its tangent ruling.

Lemma 4.3 is now checked over the original characteristic-zero field.
Dominance is expressed as equality of the actual projective image
closures in Mathlib's projective spectrum. This constructs the cone
field pullback. Preservation of the original birational Gauss parameter
then derives the common normal and common tangent ruling kernel.
The original Jacobian supplies its simple-pole action on the actual
tangent lattice, and differential transport contradicts nilpotence.
Every homogeneous parameter quotient, denominator, tangent differential,
local unit and common kernel is derived. The subsequent Gauss-parameter
algebraicity is now checked by a direct projective-ruling argument.

The boundary is now also constructed in all four original source
coordinates. Its actual projective closure equations give the primitive
map's target cross equations over its residue field. Wherever the
primitive part is defined, its boundary value is projectively the actual
fiber target. That target retains the original projective generic point.
The first and quadratic invariant equations now put every full boundary
direction at infinity on the original Gauss ruling. Polynomial density
carries the original Gauss-parameter quotient relation to arbitrary
residue-field points of that ruling. A generic boundary source direction
would therefore construct a dominant primitive restriction preserving
that parameter, contradicting Lemma 4.3. The original map constructs the
chart and all auxiliary data for this generic-point exclusion.
A normalized nonalgebraic ruling point would have the same homogeneous
relations as the generic output, contradicting that exclusion. Thus the
actual boundary coordinates are algebraic over the original Gauss field
and supply a finite parameter branch. The global proper closed image
closure described above also retains the full projected boundary.

Lemma 4.2 is now checked in its local-trivialization formulation.
The intrinsic normalization is formed after the actual categorical
coefficient base change. A prime curve's actual reduced integral closure
in the source-parameter product gives its spread-out family. Density of
that family's source image in a divisor supplies the original curve's
source generic point and the full regular, rational and integer-order
comparison. Every regular frame transition preserves the coefficient
order. Actual integer orders of all original homogeneous sections give
`Delta^a | g` and `a <= deg(g)`, including zero sections. The parameter
model may be arbitrary with its specified extension-field point over the
original constants; a normalized affine model is also constructed.

At a codimension-one actual source-image point the actual source stalk is a DVR, and every locally regular source function has exactly the same maximal-ideal power bounds after actual global pullback. This includes source-stalk elements beyond the sections on the original full coordinate open |
| Lemma 4.2, rational valuations and regular local frames on the actual global normalization | `actual_projective_given_prime_canonical_rational_orders`, `actual_global_normalization_source_canonical_rational_orders`, `actual_global_projective_source_rational_stalk_orders`, `actual_global_projective_source_rational_section_orders` | The given normalized prime constructs the canonical rational source comparison. Transport through the checked global stalk isomorphism constructs a DVR on the actual global normalization and a rational pullback preserving every normalized source valuation. It extends every value of the entire actual geometric source stalk map. Such an extension is uniquely determined by those values. Every regular transition unit in the actual source stalk preserves the rational coefficient order, so the comparison holds in every compatible regular local frame. The hypotheses use the actual projective source-image point and its codimension one; no auxiliary local model, rational map, or order comparison is assumed. Arbitrary prime subvarieties on the constructed global normalization are treated below; normalization transport across the categorical base-change isomorphism is checked below, and the spread-out-family source-image bridge is checked below |
| Lemma 4.2, arbitrary global points and prime subvarieties | `extendedClosedGaussNormalizationPoint_exists_tensor_prime`, `actual_global_irreducible_source_divisor_tensor_prime`, `actual_global_point_source_divisor_order_comparison`, `actual_global_irreducible_source_divisor_order_comparison` | Every point of the actual normalization is represented by an original tensor-normalization prime. Intrinsic source codimension one constructs the source height-one prime and DVR. A prime closed subvariety with source image dense in a divisor maps its generic point to that divisor's generic point, so it constructs the required affine prime without an assumed contraction. The entire actual source stalk map has unchanged regular and rational orders in its own fraction field and every compatible regular frame. This applies to arbitrary prime curves on the constructed global normalization. Identification with the intrinsic normalization of the categorical base change is checked below; the spread-out-family source-image bridge is checked below |
| Lemma 4.2, actual scheme function fields and integer orders | `polynomialProjective_isIntegral`, `intrinsic_divisor_pullback_scheme_orders`, `actual_global_irreducible_source_divisor_scheme_orders`, `scheme_source_rational_section_frame_order` | The original projective source is integral and locally Noetherian. The comparison constructs a map between the schemes' actual function fields, extending the entire actual geometric source stalk map, and proves equality of Mathlib's integer orders of vanishing. The upstairs point is proved to have codimension one. The rational pullback is unique from its source-stalk values, and every regular source-stalk frame unit preserves the integer coefficient order. | Lemma 4.2, an actual etale parameter open for finite covers | `normalized_curve_cover_etale_base_localization`, `actual_closed_gauss_finite_cover_etale_parameter_open` | The integral closure of the parameter polynomial ring in each finite extension is a finite affine curve model. This is constructed from the original dimension-one Gauss image and every finite extension of its actual closed field. The actual generic-curve stalks and the spread-out source-image bridge are checked below. The proof of Lemma 4.2 uses these generic normalization stalks rather than identifying the local rings of the entire spread-out surface |
| Lemma 4.2, local order comparison and actual source common-factor degree | `unramified_divisor_rational_valuation`, `principal_prime_localization_power_mem_iff`, `unramified_source_orders_common_factor_degree` | The source ring localized at an irreducible divisor is a DVR. An unramified extension preserves its normalized rational-function valuation. Powers of the source maximal ideal correspond exactly to prime-power divisibility in the original polynomial ring, so the assumed upstairs orders of all `H_i` imply `delta^a` divides the actual common factor and `a * deg(delta) ≤ deg(g)`, hence `a ≤ deg(g)`. The actual generic-curve stalk comparison and the spread-out source-image bridge are checked below |

| Lemma 4.2, local normalization and intermediate coordinate rings | `relativelyClosedDvrBaseChangeLocalNormalizationEquiv`, `intermediate_algebra_source_localization` | Canonical normalization changes neither the horizontal source base change nor its divisor local rings. This constructs the coefficient-field embedding into the source ring, agreeing with both source and target maps and the original coefficient action. | Lemma 4.2, actual contracted pencil primes without horizontal assumptions | `actual_projective_pencil_source_isFractionRing`, `actual_projective_pencil_contracted_prime_local_map_bijective`, `actual_projective_pencil_normalization_contracted_prime_local_map_bijective` | The actual projective pencil chart embeds in the original projective source field and has that fraction field before any divisor is chosen. A pencil prime, or a prime of its normalization, lying over an original source principal prime divisor has exactly the original source DVR. | Lemma 4.2, horizontal parameter and finite source models from prime contraction | `projectivePencilContractedCoefficientHom`, `projectivePencilContractedCoefficientHom_source`, `actual_projective_contracted_prime_horizontal_parameter`, `actual_projective_contracted_prime_base_change_dedekind` | The inverse actual local isomorphism embeds the closed Gauss field in the original source DVR, preserving its original coefficients and its upstairs and source values. | Lemma 4.2, a given normalization prime in a normal source model | `local_equiv_coordinate_isLocalization`, `integralClosure_normal_denominator_localization`, `normalized_prime_over_source_local_equiv` | The inverse local isomorphism recovers coordinate localization at its original denominators. If a source-denominator localization is normal, it is also the localization of coordinate normalization at those denominators. | Lemma 4.2, canonical finite-extension normalization primes | `coefficient_canonical_normalization_prime_model`, `actual_projective_finite_normalization_prime_model`, `actual_projective_given_normalization_prime_local_model` | Starting with a given prime of the actual canonical projective pencil normalization after finite coefficient extension, its contraction to the original pencil supplies all horizontal data. Original source denominators give the finite etale Dedekind model inside the canonical coordinate fraction field. The given prime extends there, lies over the source local maximal ideal, and its local ring is explicitly identified with an unramified DVR |
| Lemma 4.2, actual given-prime rational source orders and local frames | `actual_projective_given_normalization_prime_source_orders`, `localEquivSourceFractionHom_eq_of_original_source`, `rational_section_pullback_order_change_frame` | The original source DVR maps into the given normalization local ring, which is itself an unramified DVR extension. Every regular source-local function has the same maximal-ideal power bounds and every rational source function has the same normalized divisor valuation. The constructed rational pullback preserves the original source ring and is uniquely characterized by those values. Multiplication or division by a regular frame unit leaves the order unchanged. Arbitrary prime subvarieties on the constructed global normalization are treated below; normalization transport across the categorical base-change isomorphism is checked below, and the spread-out-family source-image bridge is checked below; the actual global rational and compatible-frame comparison is checked above |
| Lemma 4.2, original homogeneous common factor from projective normalization orders | `isPrimitiveVec_projectiveDehomogenize`, `projectiveSourceSectionHom_homogeneous_value`, `actual_projective_given_normalization_prime_common_factor_degree` | Homogeneous forms are reconstructed from their chart polynomials, so chart divisors lift and homogeneous primitive vectors remain primitive on each chart. The actual section coefficient is `H_i / X_a^d`. Starting with a given prime of the actual finite-coefficient canonical normalization over a source chart divisor, section orders at least `a` force the original homogeneous divisor power `delta0^a` into the original common factor `g`. Its degree gives `a * deg(delta0) ≤ deg(g)` and `a ≤ deg(g)`. The divisor equation is irreducible in the original coordinate order and has its actual chart degree. All horizontal data and local order comparisons are constructed from the given prime; arbitrary prime curves on the constructed global normalization now supply that prime, and the spread-out-family source-image bridge is checked below |
| Lemma 4.2, divisor source image and a constructed homogeneous equation | `projective_source_height_one_equation`, `projective_source_image_divisor_iff_height_one`, `actual_projective_divisor_image_common_factor_degree` | Height one on the actual original source chart constructs its irreducible equation and DVR. The closure of the actual spectrum image of an upstairs prime is defined by its contraction; being an irreducible hypersurface is equivalent to that contraction having height one. On the actual finite-coefficient pencil normalization, an image dense in a source divisor therefore constructs an irreducible homogeneous equation `Delta` with that same source image, `Delta^a` dividing the original `g`, and `a * deg(Delta) ≤ deg(g)`. A chosen divisor equation and a prime-contraction equality are no longer hypotheses. Arbitrary prime curves on the constructed global scheme now supply these affine primes; the spread-out-family source-image bridge is checked below |
| Lemma 4.2, canonical source-local maps and rational orders | `localEquivSourceAlgHom_eq_canonical_localRingHom`, `normalization_local_canonical_regular_orders`, `normalization_local_canonical_rational_orders` | The map recovered through a normal source model is proved equal to the canonical localization map induced by the original source inclusion. The constructed normalization model yields exact regular order bounds for that canonical map and a rational pullback extending it with equality of every normalized source valuation. The conclusions retain the actual given normalization prime and do not require choosing a comparison map independently |
| Lemma 4.2, direct canonical orders after finite coefficient extension | `coefficient_canonical_normalization_regular_orders`, `coefficient_canonical_normalization_rational_orders`, `actual_projective_given_prime_canonical_regular_orders` | The finite-coefficient normalization has exact regular source-local order bounds through its canonical map and a rational pullback extending that map with unchanged normalized valuations. For an actual projective normalization prime over a height-one source prime, every regular source-local function has the same maximal-ideal power bounds under the canonical map. The horizontal parameter, source coordinate map, and unramified model are constructed from the given prime |
| §3.2, fixed tangent-vertex Gauss bound | `gauss_dimension_le_one_of_constant_image_normal_vertex` | For the original homogeneous positive-degree nilpotent rank-three map, a nonzero constant vector annihilating the actual image normal forces Gauss-image dimension at most one. The graph chart, Euler weight and second independent coefficient-Hessian kernel direction are derived |
| §3.2, actual generic vertex line | `generic_vertex_line_gauss_dimension_le_one`, `gauss_dimension_two_no_generic_vertex_line` | A polynomial line on the actual image equation through its actual generic output value in a nonzero constant vertex direction gives the original polynomial normal relation and Gauss dimension at most one; Gauss dimension two excludes such a line. The uniform covering-family vertex exclusion is also proved above |
| §3.2, direction differentiation | `rank_two_isotropic_orthogonal_plane`, `normalized_coordinate_hessian_direction_constant` | A rank-two Hessian's isotropic orthogonal plane is spanned by the radial vector and direction; differentiating quadratic isotropy and using cubic isotropy gives `DZ=0` under the stated coordinate normalization |
| §3.2, integration to actual image lines | `relation_vanishes_on_zero_acceleration_line`, `ImageRelationWitness.normalized_isotropic_line` | Every polynomial relation of a zero-acceleration graph point vanishes on its entire tangent line; applied to the actual coefficient Hessian over algebraic extensions, the original image equation vanishes identically on the line in the original output order, given rank two, normalization and (3.4) |
| §3.2, genuine distinct lines | `normalized_graph_line_vectors_independent`, `normalized_graph_line_spans_distinct` | The graph point and normalized tangent vector are independent; two distinct directions with a common normalization give distinct two-dimensional line spans |
| §3.2, poles and points at infinity | `nonscalar_has_valuation_pole`, `nonscalar_coordinate_has_projective_boundary` | Nonscalar functions over algebraically closed constants have valuation poles; integral normalization and residue reduction give actual points at infinity on Mathlib's projective closure over the original constants, without identifying the residue field |
| §3.2, leading boundary equations | `quadratic_fiber_residue_direction_isotropic`, `cubic_fiber_residue_direction_isotropic` | The actual homogenized quadratic and cubic fiber forms reduce to their leading homogeneous parts at infinity; their residue directions satisfy the Hessian and third-tensor self-contraction equations, including vanishing top-degree parts |
| §3.2, removing radial shifts | `coefficientCubicTensor_radial_shift_over` | Euler identities and Hessian isotropy remove every radial cross term from the cubic self-contraction, over arbitrary extension or residue fields |
| §3.2, simultaneous-pole exclusion in (3.3) | `generic_fiber_quadratic_no_simultaneous_poles` | Given the paper's full-source coordinate decomposition and quadratic normal form with nonzero radial/mixed coefficients, simultaneous isotropic-coordinate poles force a dominant radial residue; the actual generic-fiber radial exclusion gives a contradiction |
| §3.2, hyperbola pole comparison | `hyperbola_poles_separated` | On `q=κst` with nonzero constant `q,κ`, a valuation pole of `s` forces the value of `t` to be less than one |
| §3.2, single-pole cubic extraction | `generic_fiber_single_pole_bounds`, `single_pole_forces_constant_cubic_isotropy` | At a pole of one isotropic parameter, the actual radial exclusion bounds the radial parameter; normalization gives `Z+rY` over the residue field, and the cubic boundary equation, radial shift identity and injectivity give the constant cubic equation for `Z` |
| §3.2, nonzero-radial normal-form branch | `generic_fiber_nonlinear_nonzero_radial_cubic_directions` | Given (3.3), the full-source decomposition, cubic fiber equation and Euler coefficient identities, an actual nonlinear generic component supplies both cubic self-equations over the original constants; both nonscalar parameters and their valuation poles are derived, while the initial isotropic directions and normal-form data remain explicit |
| §3.2, the case `c=q=0` | `homogeneous_zero_invariants_imply_actual_fiber_line`, `nonlinear_geometric_component_invariants_nonzero` | The two actual fiber covectors annihilate the radial output; their independent common kernel forces a radial tangent, and the exact constant field integrates it to an affine line. Thus a nonlinear full-output geometric generic component has `c≠0` or `q≠0`, directly from the homogeneous nilpotent rank-three hypotheses and the constructed invariant equations |
| §3.2, the `c=0,q≠0` normal-form branch | `generic_fiber_nonlinear_hyperbola_cubic_directions` | On the actual generic component, the hyperbola equation and nonlinearity supply both nonscalar parameters and their poles. The other parameter is finite at each pole, the radial exclusion supplies the radial bound, and the cubic residue equation gives both constant cubic directions. The full coordinate, cubic and Euler coefficient data remain explicit |
| §§2.2, 4.5, 5.3 | `eigenvalue_zero_of_nilpotent` | A nonzero left covector for a nilpotent matrix has eigenvalue zero |
| §5.4, equation (5.5) | `laplacian_radialPairing`, `divergence_laplacian`, `laplacian_half_degree_zero`, `laplacian_pow_sumSquares_pow`, `no_radial_power_identity` | The complete polynomial Laplacian contradiction, starting from the radial pairing, divergence and power-identity hypotheses |
| Proposition 6.4, (6.10) | `Shear.automorphism`, `Shear.automorphism_inverse` | Polynomial algebra automorphism with explicit inverse over every commutative coefficient ring; specialize the coefficient ring to `K[z,t]` |
| Proposition 6.4, (6.11) | `Shear.stable_commutator` | Exact point-map commutator formula over every commutative ring; rightmost factor acts first |
| Proposition 6.4, general assertion | `Shear.proposition_6_4_general` | Actual four-variable automorphism, explicit inverse, cube-zero perturbation Jacobian, and one-stable tameness in five variables over the original field; arbitrary polynomial `a,b,f` |

The general assertion of Proposition 6.4 now has a proof in the polynomial
automorphism group itself. `Tame.subgroup` is generated by affine maps and
elementary coordinate additions whose added polynomial excludes the moved
variable. `Shear.stableAutomorphism5_extends` identifies the constructed
five-variable tame automorphism with the extension of the four-variable shear
and proves that the auxiliary variable is fixed. The final assertion of
Proposition 6.4 about ordinary tameness for coefficient degree at most one
still requires the separate Lemma 6.2.

Additional checked foundations include primitive homogeneous kernel generators,
content factorization, the rank-one homogeneous proportionality theorem, and
the polynomial chain rule. The exact lists and all proof-axiom dependencies
are recorded in `validation/audit.json` after running the audit.

## Files

* `Defs.lean`: polynomial Jacobian, rational-function rank, component dependence,
  homogeneity and primitive kernel vectors.
* `Homogeneous.lean`, `Basic.lean`, `LinearSubst.lean`: general-degree algebraic
  foundations adapted from the neighboring quartic project. Their docstrings
  retain their original attribution and numbering where appropriate.
* `Nilpotent.lean`: the target specification and the proved elementary cases.
* `Flag.lean`: the matrix-algebra part of Lemma 2.2.
* `NilpotentKernel.lean`: the kernel-image inclusion for a corank-one
  nilpotent matrix and the resulting left/right kernel pairing identity.
* `NilpotentFlag.lean`: higher nilpotent images, including containment of
  the rank-three kernel direction in the image of the square and vanishing
  of its pairing with the second covector in a nilpotent chain.
* `NilpotentCubicFlag.lean`: the third-image inclusion, the third covector's
  vanishing on the kernel direction, and independence in a three-step chain.
* `ConstantField.lean`: the field of constants of a nonzero derivation in
  relative transcendence degree at most one.
* `AlgebraicDerivation.lean`, `AlgebraicDifferential.lean`: uniqueness,
  extension and commutation of derivations through algebraic extensions;
  the construction on arbitrary algebraic extensions glues finite subfields.
* `RationalDerivative.lean`, `RationalPartial.lean`, `AlgebraicPartial.lean`:
  rational coordinate derivatives and their unique algebraic extensions.
* `OutputCoordinates.lean`, `CoefficientField.lean`: the actual output field,
  its relative algebraic closure, and the coordinate derivations on it.
* `AmbientDerivation.lean`, `CoefficientChainRule.lean`: uniqueness for
  ambient-valued algebraic derivations and the coefficient-field chain rule.
* `SourceTranscendence.lean`, `VerticalDerivation.lean`: the relative
  transcendence-degree bound and the nonzero tangent derivation `u · ∇`.
* `CoefficientCurveField.lean`: the exact relative transcendence degree one
  over the actual coefficient field, excluding degree zero using the nonzero
  vertical derivation.
* `FiberJacobian.lean`, `JacobianRank.lean`, `OutputSelection.lean`: the actual
  factorization `N = EB`, the square Jacobian criterion, and selection of the
  independent output triple from the rank-three hypothesis.
* `FirstInvariant.lean`, `FirstInvariantChart.lean`: the tangent-frame identity
  and membership of the first invariant, including the result for an arbitrary
  polynomial map with a nilpotent rank-three Jacobian. The independent outputs,
  algebraic last output and nonzero tangent vector are constructed in the proof.
* `QuadraticDifferentiation.lean`, `SecondInvariant.lean`: the differentiated
  first equation, the actual covector `b = ∇c + (D²h)x`, its nonvanishing, and
  membership of the quadratic expression `q` in the coefficient field. The
  final theorem constructs both invariants for the original polynomial setup.
* `CubicDifferentiation.lean`, `ThirdCovector.lean`, `ThirdInvariant.lean`:
  varying-coefficient quadratic differentiation, symmetric cubic
  differentiation, the third covector, independence of the two fiber gradients,
  and construction of all three invariants from the original polynomial setup.
* `FiberPolynomialForms.lean`, `FiberPolynomialGradients.lean`: the actual
  polynomial forms, their degree bounds and their formal polynomial gradients.
* `FiberEquations.lean`, `FiberEquationSystem.lean`: specialization to the
  actual coefficient field and construction of all three equations from the
  polynomial Jacobian hypotheses, with a unit last derivative for the hyperplane.
* `FiberCommonFactors.lean`, `FiberCoprimeEquations.lean`: common-factor exclusion
  at the generic point and relatively prime replacements retaining the degrees,
  vanishing and gradient independence.
* `MultivariateHomogenize.lean`: multivariate homogenization, recovery on the
  affine chart, exact total degree and absence of an extra factor at infinity.
* `HomogenizeChart.lean`, `HomogenizeCoprime.lean`: preservation of affine
  values, affine gradient components and relative primality at actual degrees.
* `ProjectiveFiberEquations.lean`: construction of the homogeneous projective
  equation witness from the original polynomial hypotheses. The equation-degree
  product is at most six; subsequent modules prove the projected components'
  Hilbert-degree bound. The final comparison with the paper's full source-space
  geometric generic fibers remains open.
* `AffineHyperplane.lean`, `FiberCoordinateField.lean`: solving the hyperplane
  equation for the last source coordinate and proving that the first three
  chart coordinates generate the entire source field over the coefficient field.
* `FiberCoordinateRing.lean`: the actual prime relation ideal, the quotient
  presentation of the coordinate algebra, and the identification of its fraction
  field with the original source field.
* `FiberPrimeDimension.lean`, `FiberPrimeHeight.lean`: nonmaximality by Zariski's
  lemma, height two, and the affine minimal-prime assertion for the two equations.
* `ProjectiveCone.lean`, `ProjectiveFiberPrime.lean`: the homogeneous prime
  obtained by evaluation at `t(1,x)`, exact recovery of the affine ideal on the
  standard chart, height two, and the homogeneous minimal-prime assertion.
* `ProjectiveComponents.lean`: the generic point in Mathlib's projective spectrum
  and the maximal irreducible-subset assertion for its closure in the intersection.
* `CompleteIntersectionPair.lean`: all relations between a relatively prime
  pair are multiples of the Koszul relation, with the prescribed homogeneous degree.
* `ProjectiveHilbert.lean`: homogeneous-piece dimensions, quotient Hilbert
  functions, and their monotonicity under inclusion of relation ideals.
* `HomogeneousIntersection.lean`, `SmallIntersectionHilbert.lean`: exactness in
  each homogeneous degree, the complete-intersection Hilbert identity, its explicit
  linear formula for equation degrees up to two and three, and the actual fiber's
  Hilbert-function growth bound with coefficient six.
* `HilbertCoordinateRecurrence.lean`: the colon-ideal and coordinate-section
  recurrence for actual homogeneous quotient dimensions, including preservation
  of homogeneity for the ideals used in the recurrence.
* `DiscreteHilbertPolynomial.lean`: a discrete antiderivative using Bernoulli
  polynomials and eventual-polynomial closure under the recurrence operations.
* `HilbertPolynomialExistence.lean`: existence of the Hilbert polynomial for
  every homogeneous ideal in finitely many variables over a field, proved by
  Noetherian induction on the relation ideal.
* `HilbertPolynomialBounds.lean`: the actual Hilbert polynomial, uniqueness,
  and degree and slope bounds deduced from eventual bounds on natural arguments.
* `FiberHilbertLowerBound.lean`: a transcendental coordinate supplies `n+1`
  independent sections in degree `n`; the actual fiber has such a coordinate
  because its source function field has relative transcendence degree one.
* `FiberHilbertDegree.lean`: the actual fiber's eventual Hilbert function is
  `en+b` with integral `b` and a unique positive integer `e ≤ 6`, over the actual
  coefficient field.
* `ScalarExtensionRank.lean`: preservation of linear-map rank under field
  extension and the actual scalar-extension isomorphism of polynomial quotients.
* `HomogeneousBaseChange.lean`: the canonical scalar-extension isomorphism
  between the homogeneous polynomial pieces.
* `HilbertFieldExtension.lean`, `GeometricHilbertBound.lean`: invariance of
  Hilbert functions and Hilbert polynomials under arbitrary field extension,
  preservation of Hilbert degree, and comparison with projective subcurves.
* `FiberAlgebraicBaseChange.lean`: contraction of the prime relation ideal and
  the minimal-prime assertion after adjoining algebraic coefficients, specialized
  to the actual coefficient field over the three-output field.
* `FiberComponentContraction.lean`: flatness of the polynomial coefficient
  extension and contraction of every extended component prime to the original
  prime, using going down.
* `ComponentGenericPoint.lean`: the generic coordinates in a prime quotient's
  fraction field and preservation of a transcendental source coordinate on
  every component after algebraic coefficient extension.
* `ConeFieldExtension.lean`, `GeometricFiberComponents.lean`: containment of
  each component's projective closure in the extended original closure, and the
  positive Hilbert-degree bound at most six for every component after algebraic
  extension of the actual coefficient field.
* `ProjectiveGraph.lean`, `GraphHilbert.lean`: the homogeneous graph pullback,
  its surjectivity in each degree, and invariance of Hilbert functions and
  degrees under graph embeddings and source-coordinate permutations.
* `FullSourceDegree.lean`, `FullSourceComponents.lean`: the degree bound in
  the original four-coordinate source space, including every component after
  algebraic extension of the actual coefficient field.
* `FullOutputField.lean`, `FullOutputFiber.lean`: algebraicity of the full
  output field over any independent output triple; identification of its
  relative algebraic closure with the actual coefficient field; the actual
  source prime as a component after extending from `K(H)` to that field.
* `FiberEmbedding.lean`, `AlgebraicCoefficientEmbedding.lean`: embeddings of
  the source function field into component generic fields, and recovery of
  algebraic coefficients inside the geometric constants.
* `GeometricGenericDegree.lean`, `FullOutputGeometricFiber.lean`: the degree
  comparison through each component's actual coefficient embedding; the
  coefficient-constancy and degree conclusions for every geometric generic
  component over `K(H)`, specialized to the original polynomial map.
* `HomogeneousRelations.lean`, `ImageRelationNormal.lean`: homogeneity of
  the relation ideal, a least-degree homogeneous relation, its irreducibility,
  nonvanishing of the evaluated gradient, and the normal's left-kernel equation.
* `ImageEquationWitness.lean`: constructs this image relation and normal from
  the original homogeneous nilpotent map; the normal belongs to `K(H)` and
  kills the right-kernel direction in rank three.
* `HomogeneousDifferentialDependence.lean`, `ConstantPlaneNormal.lean`:
  Euler contraction and the constant-plane normal criterion for component
  dependence, the algebraic step in Proposition 2.3.
* `PolynomialCoefficientHeight.lean`, `ImageRelationHeight.lean`:
  localization to a polynomial ring over a field bounds height by one when
  three output coefficients are independent.
* `ImageHypersurface.lean`: the rank-three relation prime has height one
  and is generated by the constructed irreducible equation; its equation
  is linear exactly when the components are dependent.
* `ProjectiveImage.lean`: the actual generic image point in the projective
  spectrum, its irreducible closure and exact homogeneous vanishing ideal,
  and identification of that closure with the constructed hypersurface.
* `ImageNormalDerivation.lean`: the actual image normal is constant under
  vertical derivations and annihilates every derived right-kernel direction.
* `SpanningCoordinates.lean`, `NormalSpanDependence.lean`, `TwoDirectionNormal.lean`:
  independent constant coordinate rows, the small component-span criterion,
  and dependence from two annihilated independent constant directions.
* `AffineLineDerivation.lean`, `TangentPlaneSpan.lean`: constant tangent
  ratios integrate to an affine line; a varying tangent in a fixed plane
  spans that direction plane and forces its annihilation by the normal.
* `FiberTangentVariation.lean`, `PlanarTangentDependence.lean`,
  `PlanarFiberDependence.lean`: the exact-constant-field derivation, actual
  tangent variation, and the fixed-plane criterion for the original map.
* `GeometricFiberNonlinearity.lean`: compatible source and coefficient
  embeddings preserve lines, so nonlinearity of a geometric component
  implies nonlinearity over the actual coefficient field.
* `ConstantPlaneProjection.lean`, `PlaneTranslateDescent.lean`: a constant
  complementary projection recovers translation coefficients algebraic
  over the full output field from a geometric plane translate.
* `GeometricPlanarFiber.lean`: descent of the fixed-plane hypothesis and
  completion of the geometric fixed-plane assertion of Proposition 2.3,
  with every intermediate choice constructed from the original hypotheses.
* `FirstOrderTaylor.lean`, `HomogeneousEvaluationScaling.lean`: first-order
  multivariate Taylor expansion with an actual square-divisible remainder,
  homogeneous scaling and the corresponding directional-gradient formula.
* `PolynomialDivergence.lean`, `RadialTaylorCongruence.lean`: product,
  radial and common-power divergence identities, and the congruence (3.1)
  after clearing the coordinate denominator of the radial presentation.
* `HomogeneousPowerRemoval.lean`: constructs a maximal common equation
  power and proves homogeneity of the resulting polynomial vector.
* `RadialTraceIdentities.lean`, `RadialTraceObstruction.lean`: exact cleared
  gradient/divergence identities and the characteristic-zero contradiction
  with the paper's coefficient `e+2+m(n−1)`, including exponent zero.
* `SaturatedRadialMap.lean`: primitivity passes the fixed-image equations
  to the saturated vector, excluding primitive radial behavior on nonlinear
  images even when the common factor vanishes there.
* `ProjectiveCoordinatePoint.lean`, `RadialBoundaryEquations.lean`: actual
  coordinate points in Mathlib's projective spectrum and specialization of
  homogeneous fiber equations at points of the projective closure.
* `GenericOutputPoint.lean`, `GenericFiberOutput.lean`: the actual generic
  value over the full output field, its exact image relations, and evaluation
  of the original polynomial map at every component generic point.
* `RadialInfinityExclusion.lean`: assembles the complete radial exclusion
  of Section 3.1 for actual geometric generic fiber components and identifies
  the excluded point as a point of the infinity hyperplane.
* `EulerDerivation.lean`, `CoefficientEuler.lean`: the Euler derivation of
  commuting coordinates, lowering of derivative weights, and the actual
  Euler derivation on the relative algebraic coefficient field.
* `SourceEuler.lean`, `HomogeneousCoefficientEuler.lean`: homogeneous
  degree in the original rational source, restriction to `d` times the output
  Euler derivation, and weight one for the last homogeneous output.
* `RadialDerivativeIdentities.lean`: the gradient and Hessian weights,
  radial Hessian contraction and radial cubic contraction, including their
  bilinear and trilinear formulations used in Proposition 3.1.
* `WeightedQuadratic.lean`, `HomogeneousFiberWeights.lean`: source and
  output Euler weights for the actual first and second fiber invariants,
  with varying coefficient derivatives accounted for in the quadratic form.
* `WeightedFiberInvariants.lean`: constructs all invariant coefficients,
  equations and the `h,c,q` weights directly from the original hypotheses.
* `IsotropicQuadraticCoordinates.lean`, `IsotropicQuadraticBaseChange.lean`:
  expansion of the quadratic form in radial and two isotropic directions,
  specialization to the actual coefficient arrays, and the same normal
  form after arbitrary extension of the coefficient field.
* `DerivationLineTaylor.lean`: coefficientwise polynomial derivation,
  the coefficient recurrence for the exact zero-acceleration line identity,
  and vanishing of every ground-field relation on the entire tangent line.
* `RankTwoIsotropicPlane.lean`, `NormalizedPlaneCoordinates.lean`: the
  rank-two Hessian orthogonal plane, independence from the normalization,
  and vanishing of a planar vector with both normalizing entries zero.
* `IsotropicDirectionDifferentiation.lean`, `CoordinateHessianDirection.lean`:
  differentiation of isotropy with varying Hessian coefficients, actual
  third-tensor contraction, and constancy of the normalized direction.
* `HessianDirectionLine.lean`: zero acceleration of the graph `(Y,h)`
  and integration to a polynomial line for commuting coordinate partials.
* `ExtendedCoefficientPartials.lean`: constructs the unique coordinate
  partials on further algebraic extensions of the actual coefficient field,
  their commutation, and the scalar-extended Hessian and cubic identities.
* `CoefficientIsotropicLine.lean`, `ImageIsotropicLine.lean`: the actual
  coefficient-field line construction, radial radical from Euler weight,
  transport of the original image equation to the graph chart and the
  final line identity in the original output order.
* `NormalizedGraphLines.lean`: independence of graph point and tangent
  vector and distinctness of line spans for distinct normalized directions.
* `CurvePoleValuation.lean`: valuation poles of transcendental functions,
  obtained from the valuative description of integral closure; nonscalar
  functions over algebraically closed constants satisfy this criterion.
* `HomogeneousResidueSpecialization.lean`: common homogeneous scaling
  for arbitrary coordinate indices, reduction of homogeneous equations,
  and reduction of actual-degree homogenized affine equations.
* `CubicRadialShift.lean`, `CubicRadialBaseChange.lean`: symmetric tensor
  contractions, cancellation of radial cross terms on an isotropic plane,
  and the actual coefficient cubic identity over further fields.
* `ValuationResidueCoordinates.lean`: coefficient algebras of valuation
  subrings, integral coordinate normalization and the nonzero residue
  direction obtained from a largest coordinate with a pole.
* `ExtensionFieldProjectivePoint.lean`, `ValuationProjectiveBoundary.lean`:
  extension-field coordinates define points of the original projective
  spectrum; normalized residue points lie on the actual closure, and a
  nonscalar coordinate supplies a point on its infinity hyperplane.
* `BoundaryLeadingEquations.lean`, `BoundaryIsotropicEquations.lean`:
  evaluation of homogenization at infinity, the exact leading quadratic
  and cubic fiber components, and their residue isotropy equations.
* `RadialValuationExclusion.lean`: applies the checked radial exclusion
  to an actual geometric generic component's normalized valuation residues.
* `QuadraticPoleOrders.lean`, `DominantRadialResidue.lean`: the pole
  comparisons in both quadratic normal forms and construction of the
  radial residue from a strictly dominant radial term.
* `GenericFiberPoleSeparation.lean`: simultaneous-pole exclusion for
  actual geometric generic components, given (3.3) and the stated
  full-source coordinate decomposition with nonzero radial/mixed coefficients.
* `RadialPoleBound.lean`, `GenericFiberSinglePoleBound.lean`: exclusion
  of a strictly dominant radial coordinate and the resulting single-pole
  bounds on actual geometric generic components.
* `SinglePoleResidueDirection.lean`: construction of integral normalized
  coordinates with residue `Z+rY`, including the actual residue of `tau/s`.
* `CubicResidueDescent.lean`, `SinglePoleCubicIsotropy.lean`: removal of
  the radial residue shift, descent through the injective constant embedding,
  and extraction of the cubic self-equation from a single pole.
* `GenericFiberCubicIsotropy.lean`: the single-pole argument on actual
  geometric generic components, including pole existence from a nonscalar
  parameter over algebraically closed constants.
* `QuadraticParameterNonconstancy.lean`: a scalar parameter in (3.3)
  gives an affine-line parametrization; nonlinearity therefore makes both
  isotropic parameters nonscalar.
* `GenericFiberTwoCubicDirections.lean`: assembles both cubic directions
  in the nonzero-radial normal form from actual component nonlinearity,
  without extra valuation, pole or nonscalarity hypotheses.
* `TwoCovectorKernel.lean`: the one-dimensional common kernel of two
  independent covectors and affine-line integration of a scalar tangent direction.
* `ZeroInvariantRadialCovectors.lean`: radial Hessian/cubic pairings with
  arbitrary source-field vectors and annihilation of the radial output
  by the two fiber covectors when `c=q=0`.
* `ZeroInvariantFiberLine.lean`: radiality of every actual vertical
  tangent in this branch and affine-line containment over the exact
  coefficient field, with the nonzero tangent and its constants derived.
* `ZeroInvariantGeometricFiber.lean`: derives the Euler identity from
  homogeneity and excludes simultaneous zero invariants on every
  nonlinear full-output geometric generic component.
* `HyperbolaParameterNonconstancy.lean`: on the nondegenerate hyperbola,
  a scalar parameter makes its partner scalar, forcing an affine radial
  line; hence nonlinearity supplies both nonscalar parameters.
* `GenericFiberHyperbolaCubicDirections.lean`: constructs the single-pole
  bounds, valuation poles and both cubic directions in the `c=0,q≠0`
  normal form on an actual nonlinear geometric generic component.
* `RankTwoOrthogonalPair.lean`: constructs two orthogonal nonisotropic
  vectors for a symmetric rank-two form with nonzero radial radical.
* `RankTwoIsotropicBasis.lean`: splits that pair over algebraically
  closed constants and constructs a radial isotropic basis with nonzero
  mixed pairing.
* `CoefficientIsotropicBasis.lean`: constructs the basis for the actual
  coefficient Hessian over algebraically closed extensions from its
  rank-two hypothesis, and proves coordinate decomposition over further fields.
* `GraphIsotropicNormalForm.lean`: uses the graph hyperplane to lift
  those coordinates to all four source coordinates and constructs the
  quadratic normal form from the weighted radial pairing and fiber equation,
  also with isotropic directions already constructed over a coefficient extension.
* `FiberInvariantTransport.lean`: transports all three actual invariant
  equations and their Euler radial data through compatible source and
  coefficient embeddings.
* `GeometricCoefficientRealization.lean`: constructs the actual coefficient
  realization on every full-output geometric generic component, including
  its output values, original scalar images and three fiber equations.
* `GeometricCoefficientNormalForm.lean`: constructs all normal-form data
  from that realization and the original rank-two coefficient Hessian;
  no rank hypothesis after coefficient extension is required.
* `GeometricFiberIsotropicDirections.lean`: assembles the actual nonlinear
  component, coefficient realization, Euler weights, both invariant cases
  and pole arguments to obtain both directions satisfying (3.4), with
  nonzero mixed pairing and independence modulo the radial direction.
* `RadialDirectionNormalization.lean`, `NormalizedIsotropicPair.lean`:
  construct a zero coordinate and a unit coordinate for each direction;
  radial shifts and rescaling preserve quadratic/cubic isotropy and the

* `RadialRankTwoBaseChange.lean`: preserves rank two over every coefficient
  extension using a nonzero radial kernel vector and an orthogonal
  nonisotropic pair; specialized to the actual coefficient Hessian.
* `GeometricNormalizedDirections.lean`: constructs both normalized
  directions and the mapped Hessian rank on the actual generic component.
* `IndependentGraphLines.lean`: lifts triple independence to graph
  vectors, preserves it under coordinate permutation and derives distinct
  line spans without a common unit-coordinate normalization.
* `RealizedCoefficientExtension.lean`: the coefficient realization
  preserves the full output field, constructs the scalar tower and

* `GeometricImageTwoLines.lean`: integrates both directions on the actual
  image equation in the original output order; identifies the common
  point with the generic output and proves triple independence and
  distinctness of the two line spans.
* `RectangularJacobianRank.lean`: completes independent rational outputs
  to a finite transcendence basis and proves independence of all their

* `RationalJacobianCriterion.lean`: proves the full characteristic-zero
  rational Jacobian criterion using the actual generated output field.
* `MatrixRankFieldMap.lean`: preserves square-matrix rank under arbitrary
  field embeddings and rank under a full-row-rank right factor.
* `GaussNormalChart.lean`: the actual image normal has a nonzero last
  chart coordinate, and its projective ratios are the negative coefficient gradient.
* `CoefficientGaussRank.lean`: factors the gradient Jacobian through the
  actual Hessian and identifies its output-field transcendence degree

* `GaussImageDimension.lean`: constructs the chart-independent actual

* `GeometricGaussTwoLines.lean`: supplies the complete two-line conclusion
  directly from Gauss-image dimension two and geometric fiber nonlinearity.
* `ImageIdealFieldExtension.lean`, `ScalarExtendedImageEquation.lean`: exact

* `GenericImageLineFiberFiniteness.lean`: smoothness of the actual generic

* `LineChartEmbedding.lean`: preservation of original-field line fibers

* `UniversalIncidenceFunctionField.lean`, `CoveringIncidenceAlgebraic.lean`:

* `OutputCoordinateFieldEquiv.lean`, `CoveringLineFamilyDimension.lean`:

* `LineComponentPoints.lean`, `LineComponentSeparation.lean`: actual

* `CoveringLineFamilyObstructions.lean`, `CoveringLineFamilySkewTriple.lean`:

* `CoveringLineFamilyDensity.lean`, `CoveringTransversalQuadric.lean`:

* `TranscendenceDegreeSpecialization.lean`, `GeneratedAlgebraDimension.lean`,

* `LinePairParameters.lean`, `LinePairComponentGeometry.lean`,

* `LinePairDimensionDensity.lean`, `LinePairIncidenceMatrix.lean`,

* `MatrixKernelDescent.lean`, `LinePairIntersectionDescent.lean`,

* `FiberInvariantDataForWitness.lean`, `GaussDimensionTwoQuadric.lean`:

* `LinearPullbackGradient.lean`, `NondevelopableImageQuadric.lean`:

* `AlgebraicImagePointExtension.lean`, `RelationGenericCoveringComponent.lean`,

* `PolynomialMapScalarExtension.lean`, `ScalarExtendedMapHypotheses.lean`:

* `AlgebraicCoefficientQuadric.lean`, `GradientFieldExtension.lean`,

* `AlgebraicNormalRatios.lean`, `ZeroDimensionalGaussImage.lean`:

* `GaussImageSurfaceReduction.lean`: the exhaustive actual Gauss-dimension

* `ProjectiveOutputField.lean`, `ProjectiveEulerWeight.lean`:

* `ProjectiveImageDimension.lean`: rank three gives function-field dimension

* `NormalJacobianGaussDimension.lean`, `GaussDimensionFieldExtension.lean`:

* `PrimitiveLinearIncidence.lean`, `ParameterizedLinearIncidence.lean`:

* `RationalIncidenceSection.lean`, `ParametrizedNormalRadialExclusion.lean`:

* `LocalizedRatioRelations.lean`, `CurveCoordinateSpecialization.lean`:

* `DifferentiatedCurveSpan.lean`, `GaussCurveJetIndependence.lean`:
