## Lemma 4.3: dominant restrictions cannot preserve the Gauss parameter

The eight modules `NilpotentFunctionalTransport`, `ResidueTangentLattice`,
`SimplePoleTangentAction`, `SimplePoleNilpotentObstruction`,
`NormalizedJacobianSimplePole`, `RationalParameterTangentTransport`,
`SourceConeParameterObstruction` and `ConeTangentParameterObstruction`
formalize the local algebra in the proof of Lemma 4.3.

For the actual source cone equation R, the local ring is the localization
at its principal prime and the residue field is its own residue field.
The original nonzero common factor g constructs g = R^m a and a local
unit representing a. The normalized polynomial Jacobian is proved equal
to JP + P (m dR/R + da/a), and inherits nilpotence from the original
Jacobian. Its explicit action on the tangent lattice is integral because
the normal pairing is divisible by R. The lattice reduction has exactly
the tangent hyperplane as its image.

The theorem `polynomial_ratio_preservation_implies_tangent_transport`
derives the directional differential identity from divisibility of
p(P) q - p q(P) by R. Equal nontrivial tangent kernels derive the
proportionality scalar. Iterating the resulting identity on a nonzero
residue functional contradicts nilpotence directly. Thus no quotient,
eigenvalue, invariant lattice operator, or differential transport identity
is supplied as an extra assumption.

The local theorem `actual_image_cone_parameter_preservation_obstruction`
uses the actual original map, its actual image-equation witness, and its
common-factor decomposition. The image equation descends to P, and a
common nonzero normal derives the required tangent conditions. It still
assumes that the source and target normals are proportional and that the
chosen degree-zero rational parameter has the same nontrivial tangent
kernel at source and target. These hypotheses are derived for the actual
original Gauss parameter by the constructions below and assembled in
`lemma_4_3_projective_restriction`. Fixed-parameter algebraicity of boundary
directions is proved by the subsequent projective-ruling argument below.

The seven modules `TranscendentalSeparatingDerivation`,
`ConeParameterDifferential`, `ImageConeGaussRealization`,
`DominantConeGaussRestriction`, `ConeTangentDerivation`,
`ConeGaussTangentDifferential` and `OriginalConeRulingKernel` construct
the geometric differential data in the actual original cone field.
Evaluation at H gives its faithful embedding in the original source
field and realizes the entire original Gauss field, including its
birational parameter and every normalized normal coordinate. An exact
cone substitution kernel expresses dominance and constructs a field
endomorphism; fixing the original parameter then derives proportional
source and target normals with a nonzero scalar.

A transcendental element of any characteristic-zero field extension
constructs a derivation with value one. Its coordinate values give a
tangent vector detecting the parameter differential. Conversely, every
actual cone tangent vector constructs a derivation of the cone residue
field by extending its dual-number polynomial point. The actual Gauss
normal derivative annihilates the actual cone point, so differentiation
gives the tangent identity involving the second normal pairing and the
original rational parameter differential. For the original nonplanar map,
the normal's constant pivot and its nonzero first derivative force that
second pairing to be nonzero. Thus the parameter differential kernel on
the cone tangent hyperplane equals the first-normal-derivative kernel.
No nonzero differential, tangent derivation, ruling incidence, or
second-pairing nonvanishing is an added hypothesis.

The four modules `FixedNormalKernelTransport`,
`ConeGaussHomogeneousFraction`, `Lemma43ConeRestriction` and
`Lemma43ProjectiveRestriction` complete the argument. Matrix-kernel
factorization transports the ruling kernel through the possibly
nonsurjective cone field embedding. The parameter constructs an
equal-degree homogeneous quotient with denominator nonzero in the cone
field. Parameter preservation gives its target denominator and polynomial
cross relation. The original nilpotent map therefore yields the full cone
contradiction, with its primitive part and original rational Gauss
parameter constructed from the map.

For the projective statement, primitivity proves that P is defined at the
original image generic point. The restriction's actual projective image
is computed from P composed with H; equality with the original projective
image closure gives equality of their actual vanishing ideals. This
derives the cone substitution kernel and its field pullback. The theorem
`lemma_4_3_projective_restriction` then proves that the original Gauss
parameter cannot be fixed. Projective dominance is a hypothesis of the
lemma, not a supplied affine-cone kernel or differential comparison.
The subsequent boundary argument is developed below. Exclusion of the
image generic point from the actual full-fiber boundary is now checked.
Finite parameter branches and Gauss-parameter algebraicity are now
derived directly below. The global boundary-incidence image itself has
not been constructed.

## Boundary specialization and the generic-point obstruction

The six modules `PrimitiveBoundaryImage`,
`HomogeneousProjectiveSubstitution`, `ActualFullFiberBoundary`,
`PrimitiveBoundaryParameter`, `ActualBoundaryTargetGeneric` and
`ActualBoundaryDirectionDominance` connect the actual generic fiber
boundary to Lemma 4.3.

The original nonzero fiber satisfies the homogeneous primitive cross
equations y_i P_j - y_j P_i = 0. These specialize at every extension-field
point of its actual projective closure, not just the radial point. If
P does not vanish there, its value is a nonzero scalar multiple of the
original fiber target. Homogeneous degree-zero quotient values therefore
agree at that primitive image and target.

The original map now constructs a valuation and a normalized boundary
point in all four original source coordinates, with one residue coordinate
equal to one and the homogenizing coordinate zero. It belongs to the
actual full-fiber projective closure, its residue field is algebraic over
the full graph coefficient field, and its primitive cross equations are
derived. No four-coordinate lift, valuation, pole, boundary point or
cross-equation assumption is supplied.

All four original output values are realized in the actual graph
coefficient field and retain their entire original polynomial relation
ideal after compatible field extension. Thus the actual boundary target
is still the original projective image generic point. At a projectively
generic source direction, primitivity makes P defined; homogeneous
substitution gives the composed image's generic point. The cross
equations then construct projective dominance directly from actual
closure membership.

For a homogeneous quotient representing the original Gauss parameter,
equality of its source-direction and target values gives an original
homogeneous cross relation. Projective source genericity turns this into
the cone field preservation identity. The theorem
`actual_full_fiber_boundary_not_generic_of_parameter_comparison` gives
the initial conditional obstruction. The nine further modules
`RulingScalarExtensionDensity`, `ActualRulingGaussParameter`,
`ActualBoundaryRulingParameter`, `ActualBoundaryRulingExclusion`,
`OriginalGaussGraphCoordinates`, `ProjectiveClosureLeadingEquations`,
`GraphNormalParameterRuling`, `ActualFullBoundaryOriginalRuling` and
`ActualBoundaryNonGeneric` derive its parameter comparison and actual
ruling hypotheses from the original map.

A two-dimensional matrix kernel admits a graph chart. Polynomial
vanishing over the original infinite field is identical vanishing of
its binary restriction, so it persists under arbitrary field extensions.
An equal-degree quotient r/s for the original Gauss parameter t gives
the polynomial relation r - t s at the original output. Actual ruling
density makes this an identity on the entire extended ruling. At an
original projectively generic direction, s is nonzero, so its parameter
value equals t. The original Gauss field's inclusion in the actual
coefficient field fixes the same parameter value at the actual target.

The graph gradient is then expressed in the original birational Gauss
parameter itself, deriving its nonzero ruling denominator and rank-one
Hessian. Leading equations specialize at every extension-field point
of the actual full projective fiber closure, without a chosen valuation
lift. The first invariant gives the graph hyperplane equation; the
quadratic invariant and rank-one Hessian give the graph ruling equation.
The original normal and its derivative satisfy these same two equations,
which derives full four-coordinate original ruling membership.

The theorem `original_map_constructs_full_boundary_generic_point_exclusion`
constructs an actual independent output chart in which every actual
full-fiber boundary direction at infinity is nongeneric on the original
projective image. It supplies its own invariants, primitive factorization,
rational Gauss parameter, ruling membership and parameter comparison.
It does not construct the global boundary-incidence space or prove
that the closure of its source image is proper. The following argument
derives the needed finite parameter branch directly from this exclusion.

## A finite boundary branch over the original Gauss field

The eight modules `AlgebraicProjectiveLineRoots`,
`NormalizedRulingAlgebraicity`, `ActualBoundaryGaussAlgebraicity`,
`FiniteGaussBoundaryBranch`, `BoundaryGaussFieldComparison`,
`ActualGaussBoundaryAcceleration`, `ActualFiniteGaussBoundaryConstruction`
and `OriginalMapFiniteGaussBoundary` close the parameter-algebraicity gap.

A nonzero homogeneous binary equation has only algebraic projective
coordinate ratios: its affine-chart polynomial is nonzero and vanishes
at the ratio. Graph-line coordinates are linear combinations of those
two binary coordinates. Normalizing any original coordinate to one
therefore makes every original coordinate algebraic. Consequently, a
normalized nonalgebraic point on a two-dimensional matrix kernel has no
homogeneous relations beyond those of the whole projective line.

Apply this to the actual Gauss ruling, whose actual output has relative
dimension two over the original Gauss field. A normalized nonalgebraic
boundary direction would have exactly the original output's homogeneous
relations over the original constants. It would be the original image
generic point, contradicting the checked boundary exclusion. Thus all
four normalized coordinates are algebraic over the original Gauss field
as realized inside the actual boundary residue field.

Adjoining these four coordinates gives a finite extension of that
specified Gauss image. The field is explicitly isomorphic to the original
rational Gauss field K(t). A normalized point over the finite extension
maps to exactly the original four residue coordinates and satisfies both
actual original ruling equations. The original and closed-cover parameter
embeddings are compared on every original Gauss element; algebraicity
therefore supplies the exact constancy hypothesis used in the cubic
boundary equation. It gives the actual zero acceleration `D_b^2(n c)=0`.

The theorem
`original_nonplanar_developable_map_has_finite_gauss_boundary_branch`
constructs the chart, invariant coefficients, nonzero first invariant,
closed parameter, actual four-coordinate valuation boundary, finite
original Gauss-field ruling point and zero acceleration from the original
map. No valuation, boundary point, fixed branch, coordinate algebraicity
or parameter comparison is assumed. This proves the finite-branch
conclusion directly; it does not assert a global closed incidence image.
The weighted calculation is now applied directly to this actual algebraic
coefficient extension, as described below.

## Actual weighted root forms in the boundary coefficient field

The nine modules `GaussParameterSurfaceDimension`,
`WeightedRulingCoordinates`, `ParametricWeightedRulingFactorization`,
`ParametricLinearRootForms`, `ExtendedCoefficientEuler`,
`ActualWeightedGaussRootForms`, `ActualBoundaryGraphDirection`,
`ActualGaussBoundaryRootConstruction` and `OriginalMapGaussRootForms`
construct the algebraic root and form equations (4.4) directly in the
actual boundary coefficient extension.

Three independent actual outputs give absolute dimension three to their
coefficient field and all its algebraic extensions. The specified actual
rational-parameter image has dimension one, so the boundary coefficient
extension has relative dimension at most two over that image. Its actual
directional and output Euler derivations both fix the parameter. They
are therefore derivations over the parameter image, and algebraicity
makes all actual direction coefficients constants for both.

The direction's commutator with the Euler derivation equals the direction.
At a nonzero direction coordinate b_a, r=Y_a/b_a has directional derivative
one and Euler weight one. Some transverse coordinate s=Y_b-b_b r is
nonzero: otherwise the Euler derivation would equal r times the direction,
contradicting nonzero nc, its weight 1+1/d and its zero acceleration.
These facts supply every input to the already checked weighted affine
factorization without assuming a binary coordinate field.

The result constructs original three-coordinate covectors m,s with
coefficients algebraic over the specified Gauss-parameter image. All
coefficients lie in one constructed finite extension. The actual original
denominator covector n lies there as well. In the actual boundary residue
field the equations are n(Y)c=m(Y)rho, rho^d=s(Y), s(b)=0 and D_b rho=0,
with n,m,s and rho nonzero. The rational formula rho=n(Y)c/m(Y) is retained.

The theorem
`original_nonplanar_developable_map_constructs_finite_gauss_root_forms`
constructs its chart, invariants, Gauss parameter, normalized valuation
boundary, nonzero graph direction, finite coefficient field, root and all
three covectors from the original map. No boundary branch, dimension
bound, binary coordinate, commutator, Euler weight, root or factorization
is supplied. The transfer of the rational root equation to the actual
normalized Gauss-pencil function field is checked below.

## Actual boundary root equation in the native pencil field

The seventeen modules `ClosedGaussCoefficientTensor`,
`FaithfulCoefficientTensor`, `ScalarExtendedSourceRelations`,
`NativeHomogeneousSourceRelations`, `FiniteCoefficientScalarEquiv`,
`ParameterImageFiniteExtension`, `CoefficientSourceTensor`,
`ClosedGaussInvariantLinearForm`, `NativeRationalRootTransfer`,
`BoundaryRationalRootTensor`, `SourceTensorPolynomialValues`,
`ActualBoundaryNativeRoot`, `ExtendedHomogeneousRootExistence`,
`NativeRootNonvanishingTransfer`, `ActualNativeRootNonzero`,
`ActualFiniteNativeRootConstruction` and `OriginalMapNativeRootForms`
provide the actual coefficient and function-field transfer.

The actual closed Gauss field is relatively algebraically closed in the
graph coefficient field: relative closedness descends from the original
source field. Tensoring the graph coefficient field with any algebraic
Gauss-coefficient extension therefore gives a field. Every compatible
realization of that tensor in the actual boundary field is injective.
The actual parameter-field isomorphism gives the finite boundary
coefficients their specified finite action over the original closed Gauss
field; compatibility with their boundary embedding is proved on every
coefficient. Extending the graph-coefficient inclusion into the original
source also preserves injectivity.

The actual source tensor evaluation has precisely the extended original
pencil relation ideal as kernel. Integrality of the algebraic coefficient
extension, prime contraction and prime incomparability identify that
kernel. The generic quotient chart embeds in the native normalized pencil
function field through the proved tensor-chart comparison, retaining
every coefficient and original coordinate ratio. Consequently, homogeneous
vanishing and nonvanishing over the full algebraic coefficient extension
are equivalent in the original source tensor and the native field.

The actual first invariant is the original source linear form L_t over
the closed Gauss field. Eliminating the boundary root from n(Y)c=m(Y)rho
and rho^d=s(Y) gives (n(Y)c)^d=s(Y)m(Y)^d. Faithfulness transfers this
equation to the source tensor. Its source polynomial is homogeneous of
degree d(d+1), so it transfers to the native field. The original nonzero
numerator and first invariant also prove the native denominator m(H)
nonzero. Thus rho_native=n(H)L_t/m(H) is a well-defined native rational
function and satisfies rho_native^d=s(H). The same faithful comparisons
prove the native root nonzero. Normality then constructs a nonzero actual
regular global hyperplane section with exactly this rational value. Its
d-th powered section equals the original degree-d source-form section
s(H), with equality of sections as well as equality of rational values.

The theorem
`original_nonplanar_developable_map_constructs_native_gauss_root_forms`
constructs its own chart, invariant linear form, rational closed Gauss
parameter, actual finite coefficient extension, native root equation
and nonzero actual regular global root section from the original
homogeneous nilpotent rank-three nonplanar map of Gauss dimension one.
No boundary, root, faithful realization, scalar-action
compatibility, nonzero native denominator or root section is assumed.
The comparison with the chosen native ruling, construction of the
binary relation, independent-branch exclusion and two remaining algebraic
normal forms are now checked below. The later geometric exclusions are
still required for the main theorems.

## The actual chosen ruling and the two remaining root cases

The fourteen modules `ClosedGaussNormalComparison`,
`SelectedOutputCovectors`, `NativeGraphNormalComparison`,
`NativeChosenRootForms`, `TwoDimensionalFormAlternatives`,
`RulingFormNonvanishing`, `ActualChosenRootAlternatives`,
`ConstructedRulingProductPower`, `RulingCoordinateBasis`,
`ProportionalRootFieldIdentities`, `NativeProportionalRootIdentities`,
`NativeProportionalPowerFactorization`, `FiniteChosenRootConstruction`
and `OriginalMapRootAlternatives` connect the actual constructed root
to the native ruling arguments and derive the remaining algebraic cases.

The first normal row is independent of the chosen rational Gauss
parametrization. Its graph pivot is nonzero, and dividing by this pivot
recovers exactly the actual graph-invariant covector in all four original
coordinates. The selected output forms are lifted back to their original
four-coordinate covectors with zero coefficient on the omitted output.
Rescaling only the numerator form by the inverse normal pivot retains
the root and its power equation while replacing the input linear form
by precisely the chosen native tangent-plane section. No normal-form
comparison or chosen-plane root formula is supplied as a hypothesis.

The actual nonzero native values prove that all three restricted forms
are nonzero on the actual two-dimensional ruling. Independent m,n form
a basis of its dual, so the coefficients of s=alpha m+beta n are
constructed. The actual regular section U=alpha L_t+beta rho then
satisfies U n(H)=rho^(d+1), with no supplied binary or product-power
identity. If alpha is nonzero, the already checked moving-divisor and
common-factor argument gives a contradiction. Thus only the following
two cases remain, with nonzero proportionality coefficients:

* m=gamma n on the actual ruling. The native root is exactly
  rho=gamma^(-1)L_t and s(H)=gamma^(-d)L_t^d.
* m,n are independent and s=beta n on the actual ruling. The native
  numerator and denominator values are n(H)=beta^(-1)rho^d and
  m(H)=beta^(-1)rho^(d-1)L_t. The actual ruling dual constructs
  independent original-coordinate vectors V,W in that same ruling,
  with n(W)=0, and every original native component satisfies
  H_i=rho^(d-1)(rho V_i+L_t W_i).

The coefficient vectors in this second case are constructed, rather
than supplied: their span contains the actual ruling, forcing their
independence. Equality of dimensions puts both vectors in the ruling.
The exact dual pairings then put W in the actual numerator kernel.
All coefficients remain in the same constructed finite extension of
the original closed Gauss field, with its specified scalar action.

The theorem
`original_nonplanar_developable_map_constructs_two_gauss_root_alternatives`
constructs the chart, finite coefficients, nonzero native root, chosen
tangent-plane formula and these two algebraic normal forms directly
from the original homogeneous nilpotent rank-three nonplanar map of
Gauss dimension one. No branch, binary relation, covector normalization,
vanishing divisor, coordinate basis or factorization vectors are inputs.

The distinguished vector W now spans exactly the actual chosen
characteristic line, by the comparison below. The remaining projective
fiber-degree and parabolic-fiber arguments, the remaining developable
cases and the smooth-quadric exclusion remain incomplete. Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 are
therefore still open in this formalization.

## The actual root numerator and characteristic line

The six modules `RationalCoverJetComparison`,
`GaussRootNumeratorCoefficients`, `GraphNormalSecondJet`,
`GraphDenominatorCharacteristic`, `ExtendedChosenGaussCharacteristic`
and `ActualRootCharacteristicComparison` identify the numerator kernel
with the original characteristic direction on the actual ruling.

The weighted boundary construction now retains the exact second
derivatives of the actual graph slopes. Their values are preserved by
the constructed finite coefficient extension and by the comparison of
the two scalar actions. Thus the original-map construction supplies the
canonical numerator itself, rather than merely a numerator satisfying
the root formula.

The derivation on the rational closed Gauss cover has nonzero speed
relative to the original Gauss parameter. Its second derivative differs
from the original second derivative by the square of this speed and a
first-derivative term. On the actual ruling the latter term vanishes.
Rescaling the normal likewise contributes only lower-derivative terms
that vanish on the ruling. The graph normal's nonzero pivot then gives
the exact equivalence between the canonical numerator kernel and the
chosen characteristic kernel in the original four coordinates.

The original nonzero three-column normal minor survives the coefficient
extension. Consequently the chosen characteristic matrix has three
independent rows and its kernel has dimension one. The distinguished
factorization vector W is nonzero, lies in the actual ruling, and is
annihilated by the canonical numerator. Its span is therefore exactly
this characteristic kernel. The theorem
`original_nonplanar_developable_map_constructs_two_gauss_root_alternatives`
now includes this conclusion without a supplied characteristic vector,
parameter-speed scalar or normal-rescaling comparison.

## The simple fixed root and the local doubled-line estimate

The four modules `SimpleFixedRootHessian`, `ParametricSimpleFixedRoot`,
`FiniteParameterGraphRuling` and `SimpleRootLocalIntersection` prove
the differential and local algebra calculations used in Section 4.3.

For rho^d=s and f=(a r+c s)rho, the root equation itself gives both
derivatives of rho. The directional Hessian factors into the transverse
direction coordinate times a second linear factor, with an explicit
nonzero scalar. If a is nonzero, the second factor does not vanish at
the fixed root [1:0], so the two projective roots are distinct. For d>1
its longitudinal coefficient is nonzero. Differentiating the Hessian
equation in the longitudinal coordinate therefore forces every
parameter-algebraic direction to have zero transverse coordinate.

For two independent forms m,s on the graph ruling, the actual first
graph-normal derivative defines a ruling over the finite parameter
coefficient field. Its kernel is proved to have dimension two and maps
to the original graph ruling. The ruling dual constructs a longitudinal
vector; its coordinate derivation fixes the actual parameter. The
modified boundary equation supplies zero acceleration and hence s(v)=0
for every parameter-algebraic boundary direction v in this simple case.
The same ruling dual constructs a single nonzero finite-coefficient
vector b in ker(s); every nonzero such boundary direction over the
output field is a nonzero scalar multiple of b. No longitudinal vector,
chosen fixed direction or exclusion of the varying root is supplied.

The local algebra calculation applies to a local F-algebra whose
maximal ideal is generated by u,v, with residue field F, u^2=0, and a
quadratic equation G with nonzero linear v coefficient. A local unit
eliminates v and proves uv=v^2=0. Every element is a scalar combination
of 1,u, so the algebra is finite over F, its dimension is at most two,
and its module length is at most two. If u survives, the length is
exactly two, retaining the nonreduced intersection multiplicity.

The actual projective fiber has not yet been identified with this
local intersection model. The global hyperplane-section degree bound,
the degree-two and parabola conclusions, and the later exclusions in
Sections 4.4--5 remain open. These local and differential results do not
complete Proposition 4.1 or either main theorem.

## Actual polynomial local intersections and hyperplane-section degree

Seven further modules `RationalPointLocalModel`,
`DoubleLinePolynomialLocalModel`, `DoubleLineLocalMultiplicity`,
`BinaryFirstOrderDivision`, `SimplePolynomialLocalAlgebra`,
`SimplePolynomialIntersection` and `HyperplaneSectionHilbertDegree`
construct the polynomial local models and the Hilbert-degree comparison
needed on the two sides of the remaining geometric argument.

The quotient by equations vanishing at the rational origin constructs
its own origin prime. Localizing this actual quotient proves that the
original coordinate images generate the maximal ideal and that the
original coefficient field is its residue field. For the explicit
intersection (u^2,G), with G quadratic and nonzero linear v coefficient,
the actual local ring therefore has dimension and module length at most
two. Applying both first partial derivatives to a polynomial annihilator
proves that u survives localization. Its dimension and length are
exactly two, without assuming a nonzero nilpotent coordinate.

The result is also proved for an arbitrary original polynomial P, with
P(0)=0 and nonzero transverse derivative at zero. Exact polynomial
division writes P=a u+u^2 R+v Q, with Q(0) equal to that derivative.
The actual local point map proves Q a unit. Thus the maximal ideal is
generated by u and has square zero. The first-derivative annihilator
argument again proves that u survives, giving exact dimension and
length two for the actual quotient localization F[u,v]/(u^2,P) at the
origin. Every surjective local algebra quotient of this intersection
inherits the dimension and length bound of two. No explicit coefficient
presentation, maximal-ideal generators, residue-field identification,
unit factor or nonzero nilpotent is supplied as an auxiliary hypothesis.

For a homogeneous prime, a coordinate outside it leaves the coordinate
colon ideal unchanged. The checked Hilbert recurrence then proves that
the actual hyperplane-section Hilbert function is eventually equal to
the actual curve Hilbert degree. This applies to the original projective
closure and its infinity coordinate, which is proved not to belong to
the closure prime. An eventual bound of two on that actual infinity
section consequently bounds the original curve degree by two.

The leading-equation intersection is now constructed in an invertible
finite-coefficient infinity chart, and the entire actual chart quotient
has the local length bound at a simple-root closure point, as described
below. The parametric graph's point identification and the general
single-support local-to-global Hilbert comparison are now checked.
Their installation on the original-map infinity section is still
incomplete. Proposition 4.1 and Theorems 1.1--1.2 remain open. The audit
also includes exported type abbreviations.

## Original boundary equations in a constructed infinity chart

Ten modules `RulingMixedDerivative`, `FiniteRulingSimpleHessian`,
`QuadraticBoundaryChart`, `ParametricModifiedQuadricJets`,
`RulingBoundaryBasis`, `RankOneBoundaryChart`,
`FiniteGaussBoundaryLocalIntersection`, `RationalPointLocalQuotient`,
`SimplePolynomialSubschemeLocalBound` and
`ProjectiveInfinityChartLocalBound` construct the local model from the
original graph equations and prove the bound for the full chart ideal.

Mixed directional derivatives equal the original ordered Hessian
pairings, with all coefficient derivatives accounted for. On the ruling,
the Hessian of nc retains both denominator-gradient cross terms. The
actual finite ruling dual supplies fixed-root and transverse vectors b,z.
The root equation rho^d=s derives the zero self-pairing at b and the
nonzero mixed pairing theta rho/s in the transverse direction. The
original nonzero normal supplies its own normalized normal vector w.
The three original-coordinate vectors w,z,b have nonzero determinant
over the finite parameter coefficient field and after its specified
embedding in the full coefficient field. Thus b+u w+v z is an actual
invertible infinity chart, with no supplied chart basis.

The original graph gradient constructs its rank-one Hessian. The first
fiber quadric's original leading equation becomes a nonzero scalar
multiple of u^2 in this chart. The second equation is precisely the
original modified cubic's degree-two component. Its origin value is
-3/n times the Hessian self-pairing of nc, and its transverse derivative
is -6/n times the mixed pairing. The constructed root data therefore
give its zero value and nonzero transverse derivative. Removing only
the nonzero first scalar preserves the full pair ideal. The actual
quotient by these two original charted leading equations, localized at
its constructed origin prime, has dimension and length exactly two.

For any containing polynomial chart ideal through this point, equation
containment constructs the canonical map of the two actual origin
localizations. The map preserves every original polynomial value.
Lifting both numerator and denominator of every local fraction proves
it surjective. Consequently the full containing chart ideal inherits
the dimension and length bound, without a supplied local quotient map.

The actual projective closure's infinity-chart ideal is the image of
its entire original homogeneous relation ideal under the explicit
chart substitution. Every closure relation, including inhomogeneous
ones, vanishes at an actual projective closure point. This constructs
the origin prime of the full chart ideal. Homogenizing the original
affine quadratic and cubic fiber equations puts both actual leading
boundary equations in that full ideal. The local bound for the actual
infinity-chart quotient follows at a simple-root closure point, without
assuming either equation membership or a surjective local map.

The point identification, graded comparison, original-map local
construction, global degree bound, exact nonlinear degree two and
nondegenerate parabola parametrization are now proved below in the
independent-root case. The actual coordinate quotient is now identified
with a polynomial ring and proved smooth; radial projective degree two
is also checked below. The remaining developable cases and smooth-quadric
exclusion are still incomplete. Neither main theorem is claimed proved.

## The specified boundary point and the local-to-global Hilbert comparison

Nine modules `SpecifiedRulingDualCoordinates`, `FiniteGaussBoundaryPoint`,
`ProjectiveModifiedBoundaryEquation`, `FiniteGaussActualBoundaryLocalBound`,
`ProjectiveEmptyHilbertVanishing`, `HomogeneousSectionMultiplication`,
`ProjectiveDehomogenizedIdeal`, `ProjectiveOriginLocalHilbertBound` and
`SingleSupportHyperplaneDegree` connect the actual boundary point to
the chart construction and prove the general graded-to-local comparison.

The four dual pairings uniquely identify the specified ruling vectors.
Those exact vectors therefore reconstruct every coordinate of every
scalar-extended ruling point. The simple-root boundary equation makes
each nonzero parameter-algebraic boundary direction a nonzero scalar
multiple of the same specified fixed vector. Proportional full homogeneous
vectors define the same actual projective-spectrum point, so actual
closure membership transfers to this chart point. The original quadratic
and cubic equations derive ruling membership and the modified equation
at arbitrary actual closure points without a valuation lift or an
assumed cubic factor.

`finite_parameter_graph_constructs_actual_boundary_chart_local_bound`
combines these results with the actual chart constructor. Given the
parametric graph, its finite independent root forms and an actual
parameter-algebraic boundary point of an affine fiber over the same
coefficient field, it constructs an invertible chart at that point and
proves the full actual chart quotient localization finite, of coefficient
dimension at most two and module length at most two. Point identification,
simple jets, rank-one Hessian formulas, equation memberships and a
surjective local quotient map are all derived internally.

For a homogeneous ideal with empty projective support, homogeneous cores
of arbitrary primes put all variables in its radical. Finite generation
then gives a power of the whole variable ideal inside it, proving that
all sufficiently high actual Hilbert pieces vanish. Hence a homogeneous
equation avoiding every support point acts surjectively in sufficiently
large degrees. An eventually constant actual Hilbert function makes
these multiplication maps injective as well.

Every element of the actual dehomogenized ideal has a homogeneous lift
inside the original ideal. A homogeneous form lying in that chart ideal
is an original relation after multiplication by a power of the chart
coordinate. Vanishing in the actual origin localization consequently
clears by a homogeneous original denominator nonzero at that point.
When all projective support is the standard rational chart origin,
the multiplication results remove these denominators. The actual
homogeneous quotient pieces thus inject into the actual local ring
in every sufficiently large degree. Its coefficient dimension bounds
the actual eventual Hilbert value. Applying the proved hyperplane
recurrence bounds a homogeneous prime curve's actual Hilbert degree
by the actual local dimension of its single-support hyperplane section.
No geometric degree comparison or graded-to-local injection is assumed.

`ExtendedProjectiveInfinityChart` also constructs the full infinity-chart
ideal when the original fiber and its extension-valued boundary are
over different coefficient fields. Its chart ideal equals the image of
the original homogeneous ideal after the specified coefficient extension.
Actual closure membership over the original field still constructs the
chart origin prime, and the original affine equations still put their
coefficient-extended leading equations in that full ideal. The simple
first equation and transverse jet then give the actual local dimension
and length bound over the extension field. No common overfield for the
original fiber and the boundary coordinates is required.

The original-map local construction, coverage of every infinity prime,
uniform fixed-root support bound and global degree bound by two are now
checked below. The graded comparison is applied through the constructed
homogeneous coordinate change. Exact degree two is now proved for a
nonlinear actual fiber in this case. Its original-coordinate parabola
parametrization, smooth polynomial coordinate quotient and radial
projective degree two are proved below.

## Original-map local models and every extended infinity prime

Eight modules `FiberBoundaryCoefficientExtension`,
`ProjectiveBoundaryProjection`, `ActualClosedGaussSimpleBoundaryChart`,
`OriginalMapSimpleRootLocalModel`, `ProjectivePrimeCoordinates`,
`ProjectiveNormalizedPrimeCoordinates`, `ProjectivePrimeBaseExtension`
and `ActualInfinityPrimeAlgebraicity` install the local model on the
original fiber and extend the boundary argument to every infinity prime.

The original leading quadratic and modified second quadric preserve
their exact coefficient arrays under the specified field extension.
The actual extended coordinate partials give exactly the mapped original
Hessians. Coordinate selection commutes with the entire homogeneous
line map, so a nonzero projection of an actual full-coordinate closure
point is an actual point of the projected closure over the original
coefficient field.

`actual_closed_gauss_independent_root_boundary_chart_local_bound` uses
the original map's graph slopes, original quadratic and cubic invariants,
actual extended partials, and the finite root construction's s(v)=0.
It constructs both the invertible chart and its specified fixed point,
identifies this point with the actual projected boundary, and bounds
the entire original coefficient-extended infinity-chart localization
by two in coefficient dimension and module length. All simple jets
are derived from the actual finite root equation and matched to the
original leading equations.

`original_nonplanar_developable_map_constructs_simple_root_local_chart_data`
starts with the original homogeneous nilpotent rank-three nonplanar map
of Gauss dimension one and degree greater than one. It constructs the
chart, invariant, parameter, algebraic coefficient extension, finite
root coefficient field and actual root witnesses. For these same
witnesses, independence of the two root forms on the actual ruling
implies the full local chart conclusion, the uniform rational
full-fiber infinity-support bound and the bound two on the original
full-fiber Hilbert degree proved below, with equality for a nonlinear
actual fiber, together with a nondegenerate original-coordinate quadratic
parametrization. A positive integral Hilbert degree is also constructed over
that same selected coefficient field. Independence remains the
explicit case distinction from Section 4.3; this theorem does not
assert that every root witness belongs to that case.

Every actual relevant homogeneous prime now constructs its own original
coordinate values in the fraction field of its prime quotient. Relevance
supplies a coordinate outside the prime. Adjoining just the ratios to
that coordinate constructs a normalized coordinate field with that
coordinate equal to one. Homogeneous equation membership proves that
the normalized vector represents exactly the original projective prime.
Its coordinates generate the whole normalized field.

If the prime contains the specified coefficient extension of the original
closure ideal, this representative is an actual point of the original
coefficient-field closure. A prime on the infinity hyperplane has a
nonzero direction coordinate for normalization. The original
ruling-preservation boundary algebraicity theorem therefore applies
to every such prime. Its original Gauss-field image lies inside the
extended coefficient image, so the normalized coordinates are algebraic
over the extended coefficients. Since they generate the whole ratio
field, that entire field is algebraic over the extended coefficient
field. This supplies the field needed for the coordinate partials at
every infinity prime while avoiding the extra radial variable in the
full cone fraction field.

The original-map simple-root point identification, local-model
construction, uniform infinity-prime bound and global degree bound by two
are now proved in the independent-root case. The actual graded quotient
and its support are transported to the constructed chart for the
local-to-global degree comparison. Exact degree two is now proved under
the actual fiber nonlinearity hypothesis. Its parabola parametrization
is proved below, together with smoothness and radial projective degree
two. Both main theorems remain incomplete.

## The same fixed root at every actual infinity prime

Eight modules `ParameterAlgebraicFieldExtension`,
`SpecifiedSimpleRootDirection`, `FiniteRootBoundaryFieldExtension`,
`ActualUniformSimpleBoundaryDirection`, `GraphBoundaryProportionality`,
`ActualSimpleRootInfinitySupport`, `GradedSurjectiveHilbertTransport`
and `InfinityCoordinateElimination` prove the uniform support statement
and the first graded coordinate-transport steps.

The two actual images of the rational parameter are canonically
isomorphic under every specified coordinate-field embedding. Transporting

in every further coordinate field. The fixed-root derivation proof

dual values. It needs no new choice of finite coefficient field or
dual basis for each prime.

The same finite covectors and the same original dual vector consequently

these coordinate fields. The modified boundary equation derives zero

root. Original-coordinate reconstruction then makes each direction
a nonzero scalar multiple of the same mapped finite fixed vector.

derives every input of this argument from the original map in an

determines the fourth graph coordinate, so projected proportionality
gives proportionality of the entire original four-coordinate direction.
Nonzero proportional coordinates give equality of actual projective
points over the original extended coefficient field.

`actual_extended_full_fiber_simple_root_infinity_support_single_point`
applies this to every normalized prime field of the actual full-fiber
infinity section. In the independent-root case, every relevant
homogeneous prime containing the specified extended original closure

length bound and this uniform rational support bound for the same
constructed field and finite root witnesses. Its support assertion

construction also proves that this fixed rational point actually

A surjective graded polynomial coordinate map now identifies the actual

The graded transport through the original graph hyperplane and the

A polynomial coordinate map onto every homogeneous piece pulls an actual

The nonzero determinant of the constructed ruling basis supplies an actual

the affine chart `b+u*w+v*z`.

The entire original full graph-closure ideal maps onto the projected
closure ideal. These identities commute with the specified coefficient

is exactly `extendedProjectiveInfinityChartIdeal`, whose actual local
dimension and length were already bounded by two. No equation ideal is
replaced by the ideal of its support.

The original first invariant supplies the graph identity in the original
source function field. Field extension, graph elimination, infinity

dimensions used in the comparison. The hyperplane-section recurrence and
the proved eventual injection into the actual local ring therefore bound
the original curve's Hilbert degree by two.

The strengthened original-map root construction now retains this bound
for the same finite coefficient field and root witnesses that supply
the local model and the uniform full infinity support. It also constructs

## Exact degree two from actual nonlinearity

Seven modules `HomogeneousPrincipalHilbert`, `AffinePrincipalCone`,
`CoordinateProjectionHilbert`, `PlaneEquationDegreeBound`,
`AffineTwoCoordinateLine`, `DegreeOneAffineLine` and
`ActualNonlinearFiberDegree` prove the degree-one affine-line theorem
and apply it to the actual original fiber. They add 23 checked theorems
and 24 audited declarations.

Multiplication by a nonzero homogeneous equation identifies every shifted
polynomial space with its actual principal relation space. Counting
monomials in three homogeneous coordinates gives the exact identity
`2*HF(n+d)=d*(2*n+d+3)`. This derives the plane equation's degree as its
actual Hilbert slope.

For a transcendental source coordinate and any other coordinate, the
affine relation prime has height at most one. Its generator, allowing
zero for the zero prime, is constructed by unique factorization.
Actual-degree homogenization generates the entire projective closure
ideal: divisibility of every dehomogenized homogeneous relation lifts
back to divisibility of that original homogeneous relation.

Selecting coordinates embeds the actual chart evaluation range into
the original chart evaluation range, so it cannot increase the actual
Hilbert function. A projected plane generator is nonzero, since the
zero ideal would have quadratic Hilbert growth. Its degree is bounded
by the original curve's actual Hilbert slope. If that slope is one,
every such plane equation is affine-linear after dehomogenization.
Transcendence forces a nonzero coefficient on the other coordinate,
giving its affine-linear expression over the original coefficient field.

These expressions construct an actual affine line for any degree-one
curve with a transcendental coordinate, including a nonzero direction.
The original rank-three map supplies that coordinate: its actual source
field has relative transcendence degree one, and its original coordinates
generate that field. Thus a nonlinear actual full fiber has Hilbert
degree at least two. Combined with the constructed independent-root
infinity-chart upper bound, its degree is exactly two.

The original-map finite root data now retain both the upper bound and
the exact-degree conclusion under actual nonlinearity, for the same
field, finite root witnesses, local model and uniform infinity support.
This does not assume or assert that all generic fibers are nonlinear.
The original-coordinate parabola parametrization is proved below.
Scheme smoothness is proved below. The remaining main-theorem arguments
are still incomplete.

## The original-coordinate parabola parametrization

Ten modules `AffineProjectionHilbert`, `AffinePlaneGeneratorDegree`,
`BinaryQuadraticExpansion`, `BinaryQuadraticShear`, `QuadraticShearGraph`,
`ParabolaFromAffineSquare`, `TwoConstantCovectorsLine`,
`RankOneQuadricParabola`, `AffineGraphParabola` and
`ActualDevelopableParabola` construct a nondegenerate quadratic
parametrization over the actual original coefficient field. They add
28 checked theorems and 32 audited declarations.

The Hilbert comparison now covers any specified affine-linear projection
of the actual source coordinates. Homogenizing its coordinate polynomials
to degree one gives the actual graded pullback and the evaluation-range
inclusion. Consequently the entire affine relation ideal of a projected
pair with a transcendental coordinate has a nonzero generator of total
degree at most the original curve's Hilbert degree.

An invertible quadratic shear supplies the decisive algebraic step. A
quadratic relation in `(Y,T)` whose inverse shift `Y-T^2` also has total
degree at most two has no `Y^2` or `Y*T` term: its actual fourth and third
`T` coefficients force their coefficients to vanish. Transcendence of
`T` then forces a nonzero constant `Y` coefficient. Thus `Y` is an actual
quadratic polynomial in `T`, with coefficients in the original field.
The whole original relation ideal, rather than just a vanishing equation,
justifies the inverse-shear divisibility and its degree bound.

If a transcendental source coordinate `T` and its square are both
affine-linear functions of the original source coordinates, apply this
argument to each original coordinate and to that coordinate plus the
specified square coordinate. Every original coordinate becomes
`a_i+b_i*T+c_i*T^2`. Actual nonlinearity forces the original coefficient
vectors `b,c` to be independent; a dependent pair would explicitly put
the actual source point on an affine line.

The original developable map supplies the required square coordinate.
Its actual first quadratic fiber equation and rank-one Hessian give
`T^2=2*n*(gradient(c) dot x-q)` for the actual normal linear form `T`.
The normal pairs to zero with the original output vector, while Euler
weight pairs `gradient(c)` to a nonzero multiple of the nonzero first
invariant. These two covectors are independent. If `T` were algebraic
over the actual coefficient field, relative algebraic closedness would
put both covector values in that field. A constructed right inverse,
one-dimensional kernel and its scalar-extension coordinate formula
would then put the nonlinear fiber on an actual affine line.

This derives the transcendental parameter from the original map. The
degree bound constructs its projected quadratic parametrization, and
the actual graph inclusion preserves independence of its directions
when returning to all four original source coordinates. The strengthened
original-map finite root construction retains that parametrization and
exact degree two in the nonlinear independent-root case, for the same
finite root witnesses, actual local model and uniform infinity support.

`LiesInAffineParabola` records a transcendental actual parameter, actual
original-field coefficient vectors, independence of the linear and
quadratic directions, and every original coordinate identity. The
coordinate-algebra isomorphism with a polynomial ring, smoothness, and
preservation of degree two under radial projection to the original
projective source level are now proved below. The remaining exclusions
and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 are still incomplete.

The independent original parabola directions construct an actual

The original valuation boundary point contains the entire specified

The original finite-root witness construction now retains the actual

The coordinate isomorphism also proves the constant-unit fact needed

The verified reduced Gauss-pencil constancy theorem now supplies this

is an original-degree homogeneous form over K(t) for each original map
entry, evaluated by the same original parameter source homomorphism.
Neither a coefficient-field realization nor a ternary expansion is
supplied in this combined statement.

The fixed-vertex parabolic parametrization, specified moving-normal
nonconstancy and incidence, normalized direction, quadratic coefficient
formula, and fixed-vertex cone coordinate identities with algebraic
coefficients remain explicit geometric premises. Deriving all of that
geometry remains incomplete. The subsequent common-pole and polynomial
parameter-change argument is checked below. The later denominator,
fixed-linear-factor and trace arguments, the remaining developable and
quadric exclusions, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## Constant poles and a polynomial parameter in the original vertex coordinate

`PolePullbackIntegrality`, `PolynomialPolePullback`,
`PrimitiveParameterDenominator`, `GenericParameterPole`,
`PolynomialParameterRegularity`, `ActualVertexPolynomialChart`,
`ActualGaussParameterPoles`, `PolynomialParameterChange`,
`GaussVertexOutputDependence`, `ActualGaussPolynomialParameter` and
`GeometricConePolynomialParameter` add 30 checked theorems and four
audited definitions for the next part of Section 4.5.

A genuine constant pole of a rational expression constructs a monic
equation for the reciprocal of the translated parameter. Specifically,
from n(t)=H d(t), d(a)=0 and n(a) nonzero, reversal of the translated
relation makes 1/(t-a) integral over the ring containing H. Normality
then makes this reciprocal regular in the original source fraction
field. For a polynomial ring in one vertex variable it is an actual
polynomial. Two different constant translates cannot both have
polynomial reciprocals unless the parameter is scalar. This proves
uniqueness of a finite constant pole even across different map entries.

The numerator and denominator needed for the argument are constructed.
Every ternary form over K(t) has a nonzero polynomial common denominator;
choosing one of least degree removes every common constant zero from
its numerator coefficients. At each root of that denominator its
numerator remains a nonzero ternary form. Algebraic independence of the
original transverse coordinates prevents cancellation when they are
moved to the coefficient field K(x_1,x_2,x_3). Hence every such constant
root constructs a nonconstant polynomial reciprocal parameter. Generic
pole persistence and pole pullback are conclusions, not hypotheses.

The original rational source is constructed as the fraction field of
K(x_1,x_2,x_3)[w], with both the transverse coordinates and the vertex w
retained exactly. Each original H_i has its own constructed univariate
polynomial in w. Any derivation annihilating H annihilates its whole
closed Gauss field. Therefore the original vertex-dependent reduced
Gauss pencil constructs an H_i with nonzero vertex derivative and a
nonconstant polynomial vertex value; no extra output-dependence
assumption is needed.

For that actual coordinate, a finite constant pole gives the polynomial
parameter u=1/(t-a). If the reduced common denominator is constant, a
nonconstant polynomial value makes t integral and hence polynomial in
w. Thus the actual original map's descended forms construct a
nonconstant U in K(x_1,x_2,x_3)[w] whose original source value is either
t or 1/(t-a) for an original constant a. Separately, a checked Bezout and
degree argument proves that substituting a nonconstant polynomial
parameter cannot cancel a reduced rational-function denominator.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_polynomial_vertex_parameter`
starts from the same specified actual cone and parabolic-fiber data as
the preceding construction. It constructs both the original-degree
homogeneous ternary forms over K(t) and this polynomial vertex
parameter. It assumes no ternary expansion, source-field model, output
vertex dependence, constant pole or pole-pullback assertion.

The retained fixed-vertex and parabolic parametrization, moving-normal
nonconstancy and incidence, normalized direction, quadratic coefficient
formula and cone coordinate identities remain explicit geometric inputs.
The change on the original closed Gauss cover, its original coprime
transverse denominator q=q(x), and polynomial coefficients of every
changed ternary form are constructed below. The primitive fixed linear
factor, final trace argument, remaining developable and quadric
exclusions, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## Polynomial coefficients on the same Gauss cover and q=q(x)

`ConstantReciprocalParameterEquiv`, `TransverseSourceFractionField`,
`VertexPolynomialCoefficientClearing`, `PolynomialGaussCoverParameter`,
`VertexIndependentReducedDenominator`, `PolynomialParameterFormCoefficients`,
`ActualPolynomialGaussForms` and `GeometricConePolynomialForms` add 20
checked theorems and one audited definition for the next part of
Section 4.5.

The constant change u=1/(t-a) now constructs an actual K-algebra
automorphism of the entire rational-function field. The changed
parameter remains transcendental and generates the same field, since
t=a+1/u. Composing with the original Gauss equivalence therefore gives
a parameter of the same actual closed cover. Applying the inverse
change to every ternary coefficient preserves the full form, its
homogeneous degree and its exact original source value. The identity
and reciprocal alternatives both produce a nonconstant polynomial
vertex parameter on that same cover.

The actual transverse coefficient field is now identified as the
fraction field of the original ternary polynomial ring. Clearing the
finitely many coefficients of a polynomial in w constructs a nonzero
ternary common denominator c(x) and an original four-variable polynomial
V with c(x)U(w)=V(x,w). If U=p/q is any original reduced quotient,
clearing its source identity gives c p=q V. Original coprimality forces
q to divide c. Additivity of the vertex degree then proves that q has
vertex degree zero, and variable elimination constructs its actual
nonzero ternary polynomial representative. No localized coprimality
or assumed source denominator model is needed.

For every changed ternary form the previously constructed reduced
common coefficient denominator is now proved constant. A constant
root would construct a polynomial reciprocal of the already polynomial
vertex parameter. The checked unit argument would make the parameter
scalar, contradicting its positive vertex degree. Algebraic closedness
of the original field rules out every nonconstant denominator. Scaling
the numerator constructs the entire coefficient polynomial over K[t],
with injectivity preserving its homogeneous degree.

The theorem
`actual_gauss_forms_construct_polynomial_coefficients_and_transverse_denominator`
combines these results for the original homogeneous map. It constructs
a constant change of the same closed Gauss cover, homogeneous ternary
forms over K[t], a nonconstant polynomial U in K(x)[w], and an original
reduced homogeneous quotient p'/q(x) of equal positive degree. Both
polynomial parameter evaluation and every original map value are
retained exactly. The source quotient and its ternary denominator are
constructed from the original homogeneous map and cover.

The combined geometric theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_polynomial_parameter_forms`
starts with the same specified actual fixed-vertex cone and parabolic
fiber data as the preceding constructions. It constructs all four
polynomial-parameter forms, their actual cover, and the reduced
original homogeneous source parameter with ternary denominator. No
ternary expansion, parameter pole, output vertex dependence, polynomial
coefficient lift or changed source quotient is supplied.

The fixed-vertex and parabolic parametrization, moving-normal
nonconstancy and incidence, normalized direction, quadratic coefficient
formula and cone coordinate identities remain explicit geometric
premises. The rational linear factor's descent, primitive polynomial
normalization and polynomial scalar factors are now constructed below.
The source-prime argument forcing a fixed source linear form and the
final trace exclusion remain incomplete, as do derivation of all retained
cone geometry, the remaining developable and quadric exclusions, and
Theorems 1.1 and 1.2.


## The common linear factor and primitive polynomial scalar factors

`LinearPowerCoefficientDescent`, `PrimitiveParameterLinearForm`,
`PrimitiveParameterScalarGauss`, `PrimitivePolynomialLinearPowerFamily`,
`ConeLinearFactorDescent`, `RealizedConeLinearFactor`,
`GeometricConeRationalLinearFactor`, `ActualPrimitiveConeForms` and
`GeometricPrimitiveConeForms` add 20 checked theorems and one audited
definition for the next paragraph of Section 4.5.

The projective linear factor is recovered directly from a nonzero
coordinate form. If P=c L^d after coefficient realization and L(e_j)
is nonzero, the normalized linear coefficients are
(partial_i P)(e_j)/(d P(e_j)). These expressions already lie in P's
original coefficient field. The resulting normalized covector recovers
the whole pure-power form. One nonzero member of a common linear-power
family therefore recovers the same covector and all scalar factors for
every member, including zero members. This works under any field
embedding into a characteristic-zero field, without algebraicity or
Galois data.

The actual cone gradient construction retains the entire coefficient
branch forms, so this recovery now applies to its original first three
coordinates. Independence of the original map coordinates supplies a
nonzero member. The original cone and fiber data construct a common
covector over K(t), together with scalar factors there; a rational
covector or a root-descent assertion is not supplied.

The rational covector then constructs its own primitive polynomial
representative in K[t]. Primitivity prevents all its coefficients from
vanishing at a constant parameter value: otherwise X-a divides them
all. Every power of that linear form therefore has nonzero finite
specialization. Clearing the reduced numerator and denominator of a
rational scalar shows that a denominator root would force this nonzero
specialization to vanish. Over algebraically closed original constants
the denominator must be constant. Thus all three normalized scalar
factors are actual polynomials in the parameter, and injective
coefficient inclusion recovers the entire polynomial form.

These factors are transported through the same constructed constant
change of the original closed Gauss cover. The result retains all four
homogeneous polynomial-parameter forms, their original source values,
the polynomial vertex parameter and reduced homogeneous source quotient
with actual ternary denominator. Independence of the original map
coordinates forces one polynomial scalar factor to have positive
degree: if all scalar factors were constant, the first two nonzero
original coordinates would have a constant linear relation.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_primitive_polynomial_linear_factors`
starts from the same specified actual cone and parabolic-fiber data.
It constructs the common rational covector, the original cover change,
all four polynomial-parameter forms, their source quotient p'/q(x),
a primitive polynomial covector with no finite base point, and three
polynomial scalar factors, at least one nonconstant. The first three
forms are exactly a_i(t) times the d-th power of that polynomial linear
form. No rational covector, primitive representative, polynomial scalar
factor or nonconstant scalar member is assumed.

The fixed-vertex and parabolic parametrization, moving-normal
nonconstancy and incidence, normalized direction, quadratic coefficient
formula and cone coordinate identities remain explicit geometric
premises. The polynomial source linear form, fixed ternary covector
and denominator power, followed by the fourth-coordinate comparison,
are constructed below. The final trace exclusion, derivation of all retained cone
geometry, remaining developable and quadric exclusions, and Theorems
1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## A fixed original ternary linear factor and its denominator power

`PolynomialScalarFactorRegularity`, `ParameterLinearSourceValue`,
`PolynomialScalarDenominatorDivisibility`, `SourceEulerLinearFactor`,
`LinearSourceDenominatorPower`, `GaussLinearCoefficientRigidity`,
`ActualFixedConeLinearForm` and `GeometricFixedConeLinearForm` add 18
checked theorems for the source-linear-factor step in Section 4.5.

A valuation argument now constructs the evaluated factor as an original
source polynomial. More generally, if z belongs to R[t] and A(t)z^d is
regular for a nonconstant polynomial A over the constant field, then z
is integral over R. A valuation containing R but excluding z would also
exclude t. Triviality on the original field gives A(t) valuation greater
than one; the product would then have valuation greater than one as
well. The proved valuative integral criterion gives integrality, and
normality of the original source polynomial ring gives an actual source
polynomial Z with exactly the evaluated linear-form value.

The original Gauss parameter has source Euler weight zero. Its polynomial
coefficients therefore have weight zero, whereas each original source
coordinate has weight one. Thus Z has weight one, and coefficientwise
Euler comparison makes it homogeneous of degree one. This constructs
an original source linear form with coefficients in K.

The complete denominator conclusion is obtained without assuming a
source-prime pole comparison. The polynomial relation A(t)Z^d=H has
leading coefficient lc(A)Z^d as a relation for t. Integral normalization
and the invertible original scalar lc(A) make Z^d t integral, and hence
regular. For the original reduced quotient t=p'/q, coprimality then
forces q to divide Z^d. The nonzero homogeneous linear polynomial Z is
prime. Every divisor of its power is associated to a power of Z, and
the original homogeneous denominator degree fixes that exponent to D.
Consequently q=gamma Z^D with gamma in K nonzero and D at most d.

The constructed denominator already has vertex degree zero and positive
homogeneous degree. Its power identity forces Z to have vertex degree
zero as well, constructing an actual nonzero ternary linear form y.
The original reduced pencil constructs transverse-coordinate independence
over the entire closed Gauss field. Comparing the evaluated parameter
linear form with y therefore makes every polynomial covector coefficient
an actual original constant, even after changing the cover parameter.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_fixed_ternary_linear_factor`
retains the specified geometric cone and parabolic-fiber data and all
previously constructed parameter and coordinate forms. It constructs
a fixed nonzero original ternary covector c, proves every primitive
polynomial covector entry equals C(c_i), gives the exact ternary
denominator q=gamma y^D, and proves the original first-three coordinate
identities H_i=a_i(t)y^d. The a_i are polynomials, with a nonconstant
member, and 0<D<=d. No source factor, source-prime divisibility, fixed
covector or denominator power is supplied.

The fixed-vertex and parabolic parametrization, moving-normal
nonconstancy and incidence, normalized direction, quadratic coefficient
formula and cone coordinate identities remain explicit geometric inputs.
The full fourth-coordinate factorization is constructed below in the
original coordinates. The coordinate conjugacy and polynomial affine-chart
parameter are constructed below. Identifying that polynomial with w-Q(x,z),
the trace exclusion, derivation of all retained cone geometry, remaining
developable and quadric exclusions, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## Complete polynomial fourth-coordinate factor with the same fixed source form

`LinearPowerQuotientRecovery`, `LinearPowerFactorUniqueness`,
`CompleteConeLinearFactorDescent`, `FixedLinearPowerPolynomialQuotient`,
`RealizedCompleteConeFactorization`, `GeometricCompleteRationalConeForms`,
`ActualCompleteFixedConeForms` and `GeometricCompleteFixedConeForms` add
19 checked theorems for the complete coordinate-free form of (4.10).

The quotient-recovery argument works over any commutative coefficient
ring embedded in another commutative ring. If a linear form has a unit
coefficient and its r-th power times a rational linear quotient has
coefficients in the original ring, coordinate-point values and first
partial derivatives recover every quotient coefficient there. For a
normalized pivot a_j=1, the i-th quotient coefficient is
(∂_i P)(e_j) - r a_i P(e_j). Unit normalization gives the general case.
No divisibility or polynomial-quotient premise is supplied.

The complete cone expression constructs its fourth rational linear
quotient over the same original parameter field as the first three
pure-power coordinates. Both factors are transported through the same
constant change of the closed Gauss cover. Equality of a nonzero
pure-power coordinate forces the transported rational covector to be
proportional to the already constructed fixed original covector c.
Its parameter scalar is absorbed into the fourth quotient. A nonzero
constant entry of c is a unit in K[t], so quotient recovery constructs
all three coefficients B_j in K[t].

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_complete_fixed_polynomial_forms`
starts from the same specified actual cone and parabolic-fiber data.
It constructs a nonzero original ternary linear form y=Σ c_j x_j,
polynomials A_i and B_j in K[t], and the exact original source identities

    H_i = y^d A_i(t)                  (i=1,2,3),
    H_4 = y^(d-1) Σ_j B_j(t) x_j.

The forms before source evaluation satisfy the same polynomial identities
over K[t]. The same theorem retains the constructed parameter change,
its polynomial expression in the vertex coordinate, a reduced homogeneous
parameter quotient p'/q, q=γ y^D with γ nonzero and 0<D≤d, all four
homogeneous forms and their original source values, and a nonconstant
A_i. In particular no fourth quotient, proportionality scalar, polynomial
quotient coefficient or fourth source identity is assumed.

This is (4.10) with y kept as the constructed original linear form.
The source/target conjugacy choosing y as the second coordinate and the
polynomial affine-chart parameter are constructed below. The fixed-vertex
and parabolic parametrization,
moving-normal nonconstancy and incidence, normalized direction, quadratic
coefficient formula and cone coordinate identities remain explicit
geometric inputs. Identifying the affine-chart polynomial parameter with
w-Q(x,z), the trace exclusion, derivation of those retained geometric
inputs, remaining developable and quadric exclusions, and Theorems 1.1
and 1.2 remain open.


## Actual coordinate conjugacy and the polynomial affine cone parameter

`LinearPolynomialConjugacy`, `FixedCovectorCoordinateChoice`,
`FixedConeCoordinateConjugacy`, `FixedConeAffineParameter`,
`LinearConjugacyRank` and `GeometricCoordinateConeForms` add 27 checked
theorems for the coordinate form of (4.10) and its polynomial parameter
on the chart y=1.

The constructed nonzero original ternary covector c supplies mutually
inverse constant matrices T,S with cT equal to the second coordinate
covector. Extending these matrices by the identity on the fourth original
coordinate constructs a source change that fixes the vertex coordinate.
The original source linear form becomes exactly the second source
variable. Both the polynomial substitution and its inverse, as well as
their actual original source-fraction-field automorphism, are constructed.

The actual coordinate conjugate is S H(Tx). Its full polynomial Jacobian
is S JH(Tx) T. The constructed inverse substitutions preserve the original
homogeneous degree, polynomial Jacobian nilpotence, rank over the source
rational function field, and component independence over K. Every
original source polynomial has exactly its substituted value under the
source-field automorphism; no arbitrary isomorphic realization is used.

The original complete cone forms then construct polynomial families
A'_i and B'_j for that actual conjugate. The same source automorphism
transports the original changed-cover parameter to τ. Its first three
coordinates have source values y^d A'_i(τ), while its fourth has value
y^(d-1) Σ_j B'_j(τ)x_j. Component independence forces a nonconstant A'_i
after the target change as well.

The exact denominator q=γ y^D is transported by that same conjugacy.
The transformed homogeneous numerator divided by γ y^D, dehomogenized
on y=1, constructs a polynomial P in the actual x/y,z/y,w/y coordinates.
Its evaluated source-field value is exactly τ, and its total degree is
at most D, hence at most d. The transported parameter is transcendental
over K, so P has positive total degree. This establishes polynomiality
on the actual affine chart, not yet the more specific shape P=w-Q(x,z).

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_coordinate_forms_and_affine_parameter`
starts from the same specified original cone and parabolic-fiber data.
It constructs the constant change of the original closed Gauss cover,
the inverse vertex-preserving matrices, their exact source-field parameter
value, the actual conjugate satisfying (4.10), the preserved map
hypotheses, nonconstant scalar member, and the nonconstant polynomial
P(x/y,z/y,w/y) of degree at most d. No coordinate change or affine-chart
polynomial is supplied as a premise.

The fixed-vertex and parabolic parametrization, moving-normal nonconstancy
and incidence, normalized direction, quadratic coefficient formula and

constraint, nilpotent-row obstruction, geometric integrality of the affine

The tangent-field argument identifying P with w-Q(x,z),
derivation of the retained geometric inputs, remaining developable and
