## Actual affine cone coordinate identities and the nilpotent trace constraint

`SecondCoordinateAffineChart`, `AffineConePolynomialForms`,
`AffineConeTrace`, `NilpotentConstantConeCoordinate` and
`GeometricAffineConeTrace` add 19 checked theorems for the affine
polynomial identities and trace step following (4.10).

The original y=1 chart is identified with the entire polynomial
substitution (x,1,z,w). Its x,z,w derivatives commute with the corresponding
original-source partial derivatives. Applying the original homogeneous
Euler identity reconstructs the omitted y derivative as

    (∂_y F)|_(y=1) = d F|_(y=1) - x ∂_x(F|_(y=1))
                       - z ∂_z(F|_(y=1)) - w ∂_w(F|_(y=1)).

The already constructed source-field values determine actual identities
in K[x,z,w], rather than only identities in its containing source field.
Injectivity of evaluation at the original three coordinate ratios and
homogeneity cancel the precise y powers. Thus the conjugate map's first
three affine coordinates are A_i(P), and its fourth is
B_1(P)x+B_2(P)+B_3(P)z. In particular the full fourth mixed expression
is recovered as a polynomial identity, without assuming any affine form.

The actual original nilpotent Jacobian then forces the polynomial
`affineConeTracePolynomial` to vanish. In the paper's one-based indexing
its explicit equation is

    0 = d A_2(P) - (x P_x + z P_z + w P_w) A_2'(P)
        + A_1'(P) P_x + A_3'(P) P_z
        + (B_1'(P)x + B_2'(P) + B_3'(P)z) P_w.

This retains the currently constructed P(x,z,w); it does not assume the
stronger shape P=w-Q(x,z), or substitute independent t before that step
has been proved. The univariate parameter chain rule and all original
homogeneous Jacobian rows are accounted for.

The nilpotent-row obstruction is also complete. If an actual output in
its own coordinate position is c y^d, its Jacobian has the nonzero
polynomial left covector supported at that position with eigenvalue
c d y^(d-1). Nilpotence, positive d and characteristic zero force c=0.
A constant second affine chart coordinate reconstructs by homogeneous
chart injectivity to exactly c y^d in the original source ring, so the
original second component vanishes and gives component dependence.
Consequently, for the independent nilpotent conjugate, the actual
second scalar polynomial A_2 has nonzero derivative.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_affine_polynomial_forms_and_trace_constraint`
starts from the same specified original geometric cone and fiber data.
It retains the constructed coordinate conjugacy, all original map
hypotheses, its exact source-field parameter value and polynomial chart
parameter. It adds the four actual affine polynomial identities, the
zero trace polynomial above and A_2' nonzero. None of these affine
identities, derivative conditions or trace constraints is supplied.

The retained fixed-vertex, parabolic-fiber, moving-normal and cone
coordinate geometry remains explicit. The quadratic trace comparison and
resulting conditional algebraic exclusion are constructed below. The
affine tangent-field argument identifying P with an
affine rescaling of w-Q(x,z), nonzero quadratic part of Q, derivation of
the retained geometry, remaining developable and quadric exclusions, and
Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## Equation (4.11), its quadratic comparison, and the conditional cone exclusion

`QuadraticConeLevelSubstitution`, `QuadraticEulerComparison`,
`QuadraticConeTraceExclusion` and `GeometricQuadraticConeObstruction` add
17 checked theorems for the complete final algebraic trace argument in
Section 4.5, once the quadratic graph parameter has been identified.

The actual substitution (x,z,w)=(x,z,t+Q(x,z)) is constructed as a map
from K[x,z,w] to K[t][x,z]. It preserves every original binary polynomial
under the canonical coefficient inclusion, carries the entire parameter
P=w-Q to the independent coefficient t, and carries each scalar A_i(P)
and B_j(P) to exactly the same polynomial in t. Its complete parameter
partial identities are proved as well. Applying this substitution to
the previously constructed original affine trace gives precisely (4.11):

    0 = d A_2(t) - t A_2'(t)
        + A_2'(t)(x Q_x + z Q_z - Q)
        - A_1'(t)Q_x - A_3'(t)Q_z
        + B_1'(t)x + B_2'(t) + B_3'(t)z.

The quadratic comparison is taken in the actual transverse variables
x,z while K[t] remains the coefficient ring. The Euler coefficient
formula is proved over any commutative ring, and its degree-two component
shows that Euler(Q)-Q has quadratic part Q_2. A degree-at-most-two
polynomial has partial derivatives of degree at most one, so every other
nonconstant term in (4.11) vanishes in the quadratic component. Thus that
component is exactly C(A_2') times the coefficient extension of Q_2.
Coefficient extension preserves homogeneous components and is injective;
therefore a nonzero original Q_2 remains nonzero over K[t]. The domain
property forces A_2'=0.

The theorem
`nilpotent_quadratic_parameter_cone_forms_force_dependence` applies this
comparison to the original homogeneous nilpotent map's actual four
affine polynomial identities. Characteristic zero makes A_2 constant.
The already proved homogeneous reconstruction and actual left-eigenvalue
obstruction then give H_2=0 and component dependence. No scalar-derivative
vanishing, constant component or nilpotent-row conclusion is assumed.

The exclusion also handles P=a(w-Q)+b, including all affine parameter
rescalings required in the paper. The transformed scalar families are
constructed by polynomial composition with aT+b; their exact parameter
values are retained. The same final algebraic exclusion applies to them.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_excludes_affine_quadratic_graph_parameter`
starts from the same specified original cone and fiber geometry, retains
the complete constructed affine presentation, and proves that its actual
polynomial parameter cannot equal a(w-Q)+b for any Q of degree at most
two with nonzero quadratic part. This exclusion uses the actual
conjugate's nilpotence and component independence, without supplying a
trace equation or any coefficient comparison as a new hypothesis.

The tangent-field construction, primitive-parameter argument and final
nonlinear-fiber contradiction for the retained cone data are constructed
below. Derivation of the retained cone geometry, remaining developable
and quadric exclusions, Proposition
4.1 as a whole, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## The actual affine parameter has nonzero vertex differential

`VertexPreservingSourcePartial`, `ChangedGaussVertexDifferential`,
`AffineConeVertexDifferential` and `GeometricAffineVertexDifferential`
add nine checked theorems constructing the P_w nonvanishing needed
in the affine tangent-field argument after (4.10).

The original reduced homogeneous vertex-dependent pencil already has a
nonzero rational vertex partial. This remains true for every other
parameter equivalence of the same actual closed Gauss field: the
original parameter is a rational function of the new one, and the full
rational chain rule would make its derivative zero if the new derivative
were zero. No derivative property of the changed cover is assumed, and
no separate reciprocal-change case analysis is required.

The constructed vertex-preserving block coordinate matrices commute
with the original vertex partial. The polynomial chain rule first proves
this on every original source polynomial. The actual source fraction
representation then proves it on every rational source function. Thus
the same source-field automorphism used by the complete coordinate
conjugacy preserves the actual nonzero parameter differential.

The multivariate derivation chain rule is also checked for arbitrary
polynomial coordinate values in an ambient field. On the original y=1
chart, the vertex derivation kills x/y and z/y and sends w/y to 1/y.
For the constructed parameter value τ=P(x/y,z/y,w/y), this gives exactly

    ∂_w τ = P_w(x/y,z/y,w/y) / y.

The already proved nonzero source differential therefore forces the
actual affine polynomial P_w to be nonzero. Because that same derivation
kills the two transverse affine coordinates and their generated field,
τ is transcendental over the entire actual K(x/y,z/y), not just over K.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_affine_parameter_nonzero_vertex_partial`
starts from the same specified original cone and fiber data and retains
all constructed coordinate forms, the original source-field parameter,
its affine polynomial expression, the trace constraint, the nonzero
second-scalar derivative and the quadratic-parameter obstruction. It
adds the nonzero original rational vertex derivative, P_w nonzero, and
transcendence over K(x/y,z/y). None of these derivative conditions is a
new geometric premise.

The affine tangent fields and their specialization to two independent
projected directions have not yet been constructed from the retained
geometry. The algebraic normalization, commutator, quadratic-potential and

generic-level integrality are constructed below. The geometric field

remain incomplete.


## Independent affine tangent fields construct the quadratic composite parameter

`AffineTangentCommutator`, `AffineTangentQuadraticPotential`,
`CommutativePolynomialChainRule`, `AffineQuadraticShearInvariants`,
`AffineTangentNormalization`, `IndependentAffineTangentParameter` and
`GeometricAffineTangentParameter` add 24 checked theorems for the
commutator, quadratic-potential and simultaneous invariant-ring steps
of the affine argument following (4.10).

Two affine triangular tangent equations have constant horizontal
projected directions and full vertical coefficients b_0+b_x x+b_z z.
The inverse of their actual two-by-two direction matrix constructs
normalized lifts of ∂_x and ∂_z. Both new vertical coefficient families
and their exact annihilation equations for P are constructed. The
intrinsic linearly independent direction-pair formulation derives the
nonzero determinant internally.

For normalized lifts ∂_x+f(x,z)∂_w and ∂_z+g(x,z)∂_w, the commutator is
proved as an equality of actual original polynomial derivations:

    [D_x,D_z] = (g_x-f_z) ∂_w.

Both lifts annihilate P. The already constructed P_w nonvanishing
therefore forces g_x=f_z. Writing f=b_0+b_x x+b_z z and
g=c_0+c_x x+c_z z, this constructs the closedness relation b_z=c_x.
It supplies the explicit original binary potential

    Q = (b_x/2)x² + b_z xz + (c_z/2)z² + b_0 x + c_0 z,

with total degree at most two and with the entire derivatives Q_x=f,
Q_z=g. Neither commutativity nor an exact potential is supplied.

The invariant-ring step is complete in both directions. The actual
substitution w=t+Q takes the two gradient lifts to ordinary x,z partials
over K[t]. The multivariate chain rule is checked for a commutative target
algebra, and a characteristic-zero coefficient-domain argument makes
every polynomial killed by all transverse partials a coefficient
constant. A constructed inverse substitution t=w-Q then reconstructs
the entire original invariant as F(w-Q), with F in K[T]. Conversely every
such polynomial is killed by both gradient lifts. Thus the simultaneous
polynomial invariant ring is exactly K[w-Q]. For P_w nonzero the recovered
F has positive degree.

The theorem
`independent_affine_tangent_fields_construct_quadratic_composite_parameter`
starts with the two original affine tangent equations, their independent
projected directions and P_w nonzero. It constructs the normalized lifts,
closedness, the quadratic potential Q and a nonconstant F with the entire
original polynomial identity P=F(w-Q). No normalized fields, commuting
condition, potential, invariant-ring assertion or polynomial composite
representation is assumed.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_parameter_independent_tangents_construct_quadratic_composite`
retains the actual original geometric cone construction and its entire
affine presentation. It applies the result to that same constructed P,
using the already derived P_w nonvanishing. Given any two independent
affine tangent fields annihilating this P, it constructs Q and F above.
This earlier theorem keeps the two fields conditional. Their construction
from the full original nilpotent cone forms is checked below.

Primitive-parameter relative closedness under the actual source change
and the resulting degree-one conclusion for F are constructed below.
The two independent constant tangent lifts are constructed below directly
from the nilpotent cone forms. The final nonlinear-fiber contradiction is
constructed below. Derivation of the retained cone geometry, remaining
developable and quadric exclusions,
Proposition 4.1 as a whole, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## Actual primitive-parameter relative closedness forces the outer polynomial linear

`RelativeClosureCoordinateTransport`, `PrimitivePolynomialParameterDegree`,
`ActualPrimitiveAffineParameter` and `GeometricPrimitiveAffineParameter`
add nine checked theorems for the primitive-parameter step after the
quadratic simultaneous invariant-ring construction.

Relative algebraic closedness is transported with the actual original
source automorphism. The original intermediate field is identified with
its image by the induced coefficient-field equivalence; the ambient
source equivalence then identifies algebraicity over the two coefficient
fields. Thus the transported actual closed Gauss parameter generates an
exact relatively closed intermediate field in the entire original source
fraction field. This is not supplied as a new property of the conjugated
or affine parameter.

The polynomial degree argument is also constructed. If t=F(s) with F
nonconstant, s is integral over the actual field K(t). Relative closedness
therefore puts s in K(t), giving s=N(t)/D(t). Transcendence and the nonzero
denominator give the entire polynomial identity

    X D(F(X)) = N(F(X)).

Taking polynomial degrees shows that deg(F) divides one, hence deg(F)=1.
The nonzero affine slope and intercept are recovered from the original
polynomial coefficients. This argument uses actual polynomial identities
rather than an assumed rational inverse or field-degree formula.

The theorem
`actual_conjugate_gauss_affine_composite_parameter_constructs_affine_graph`
applies the result to the exact transported original Gauss parameter and
its evaluated affine polynomial P. Every representation P=F(w-Q), with
F nonconstant, therefore has deg(F)=1 and gives the complete original
polynomial identity P=a(w-Q)+b with a nonzero. The original closed Gauss
field constructs all required relative closedness and transcendence.

Combining this with the independent affine tangent-field construction
produces a quadratic graph parameter with nonzero affine slope, directly
for the actual original source-field realization. The previously proved
quadratic trace obstruction then forces the quadratic component of Q
zero. Its degree-at-most-two bound makes Q affine. Since P_w is nonzero,
the complete actual affine parameter P has total degree exactly one.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_primitive_parameter_independent_tangents_force_affine_parameter`
retains the entire original geometric cone construction and its affine
coordinate identities. It adds the exact relative closedness of K(τ) in
the original source. Given two independent annihilating affine tangent
fields for its same constructed P, it proves P has total degree one and
constructs Q of degree at most one and a nonzero affine slope in
P=a(w-Q)+b. The two fields are conditional inputs to this earlier
implication; their construction from the original data is checked below.

Global annihilation from actual generic-point tangency and descent to
two original constant directions from independent generic horizontal
coefficients are constructed below. The actual generic tangent and
horizontal coefficient independence are also constructed below directly
from the nilpotent cone forms. The nonlinear-fiber contradiction is
constructed below. Derivation of the retained cone geometry, remaining developable and quadric exclusions, Proposition
4.1 as a whole, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 also remain incomplete.


## The actual generic affine level is geometrically integral

`GenericAffineLevelKernel`, `AffineLevelParameterField`,
`AffineLevelGeometricIntegrality` and `GeometricAffineLevelIntegrality`
add twelve checked theorems for the generic-level step after (4.10).

The exact source relation ideal is constructed first. For algebraically
independent actual source coordinates x and the identity t=P(x), every
polynomial relation over K(t) is a multiple of the literal level equation
P-t. Parameter denominators are cleared in K[T][x]; swapping the source
and parameter polynomial presentations reduces the relation to the
univariate factor theorem. Injectivity at the independent actual source
coordinates gives divisibility by P-T before coefficient localization.
The canonical transcendental identification K(T)=K(t) then transports
the full kernel to the actual parameter field. Thus this identifies all
relations, rather than only exhibiting a single relation.

The quotient by P-t is identified with the actual source-coordinate
subalgebra over K(t). The previously checked relative closedness and
characteristic-zero algebraic base-change theorem make its tensor product
with every algebraic coefficient field a domain. The coefficient-extended
polynomial quotient is identified with this tensor product, so the
literal generic affine level is geometrically integral. The original
nonzero w-partial remains nonzero under both coefficient embeddings;
therefore the level equation is nonzero and remains irreducible over
every algebraic extension of K(t).

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_geometrically_integral_affine_level`
starts from the same specified original geometric cone and parabolic-fiber
data. It retains the complete coordinate conjugacy, affine presentation,
trace constraint, primitive-parameter relative closedness, nonzero vertex
partial and conditional tangent conclusions. For its same constructed
actual P, it now proves the whole relation ideal equals (P-t), and that
the quotient remains a domain and its defining equation irreducible after
every algebraic coefficient extension. No new generic-level integrality
hypothesis is supplied.

Global annihilation from actual generic-point tangency and linear
coefficient descent to two normalized constant directions are constructed
below. The generic affine tangent and horizontal independence are also
constructed below from the original nilpotent cone forms. The final
nonlinear-fiber contradiction is also constructed below. Derivation of
the retained cone geometry, remaining developable and quadric exclusions, Proposition 4.1
as a whole, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 also remain incomplete.


## Actual generic-point tangency gives global annihilation and coefficient descent

`AffineTriangularFlow`, `AffineTriangularDivisibility`,
`ActualAffineLevelTangent`, `ConjugatedGaussAffineLevelTangent`,
`AffineTangentCoefficientDescent` and `ActualGaussGenericTangentGraph`
add twenty-one checked theorems. The existing combined original-cone
construction is also strengthened to retain the new conclusions for
its same constructed affine parameter.

For an actual affine triangular field with constant horizontal
coefficients u_0,u_1 and vertical coefficient
B=b_0+b_1 x+b_2 z, the entire polynomial flow is constructed:

    x(s) = x + u_0 s,
    z(s) = z + u_1 s,
    w(s) = w + B s + (b_1 u_0+b_2 u_1)s²/2.

Its value at time zero is the original source point. The full
multivariate chain rule identifies time differentiation of every
substituted polynomial with the original triangular tangent action.
A nonzero scalar eigenvector would give a nonzero univariate polynomial
Q(s) dividing Q'(s); the strict derivative-degree bound forces Q'=0,
and then the scalar eigenvalue is zero. This proves the required
obstruction directly, without assuming local nilpotence or a flow.

The total-degree bound is constructed from the original partial
coefficients, including every zero-partial case. If a nonzero polynomial
divides its affine triangular tangent derivative, its cofactor has
degree zero. The flow obstruction therefore gives global annihilation.
In particular, divisibility of D(P) by the nonzero literal level P-t
forces the entire polynomial D(P) to be zero.

This divisibility is now derived for the actual source point after every
algebraic extension of the actual parameter field. The point's relation
prime is a minimal prime over the extended original relation ideal.
The checked geometric integrality makes this extended ideal prime, so
it is the unique minimal prime. Hence the whole actual point relation
ideal remains exactly (P-t). A tangent derivative vanishing at that point
is therefore a multiple of P-t, and vanishes globally by the preceding
argument. The actual transported closed Gauss parameter supplies its
own relative closedness, transcendence and independent chart coordinates;
none is added as a new primitive-parameter or integrality assumption.

The two-direction step also has a direct linear coefficient construction.
The tangent equation is linear in its five field coefficients. Applying
any K-linear functional from the extension field to K to every polynomial
coefficient gives a full tangent identity for the same original P over K.
If the two generic horizontal coefficients are linearly independent over
K, their linear-combination map has a constructed linear left inverse.
Its two coordinate functionals give exactly the horizontal values (1,0)
and (0,1), and construct both normalized original-field tangent lifts.
No points on a finite parameter cover are chosen for this descent.

The theorem
`actual_conjugate_gauss_generic_tangent_constructs_original_quadratic_graph_parameter`
combines these steps. One affine triangular tangent at the actual generic
source point, over an algebraic parameter extension, with independent
horizontal coefficients over K, constructs two original normalized lifts,
a quadratic potential Q and a nonconstant polynomial F with P=F(w-Q).
The actual primitive-parameter argument then gives the entire original
identity P=a(w-Q)+b with a nonzero and deg Q at most two. Global
annihilation, constant tangent fields, a potential and the affine outer
slope are conclusions rather than supplied hypotheses.

The strengthened theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_geometrically_integral_affine_level`
retains the complete original cone construction and now also proves both
the actual generic-point tangency-to-global-annihilation implication and
the single independent-coefficient generic tangent-to-original quadratic
graph implication for its same P. Its earlier trace exclusion and
conditional affine-degree-one conclusions remain available.

The generic affine tangent and horizontal coefficient independence are
constructed below directly from the complete nilpotent cone forms. This
supplies the fields that were conditional in the earlier linear descent
theorem. The final nonlinear-fiber contradiction is constructed below.
Derivation of the retained cone geometry, remaining developable and
quadric exclusions, Proposition 4.1 as a whole, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2
remain incomplete.


## The original nilpotent cone forms construct the tangent fields and an affine parameter

`ConeRankOneTraceElimination`, `AffineConeJacobianMatrix`,
`ConeWronskianHorizontalIndependence`, `ConstructedConeTangentPolynomials`
and `ActualConeTangentConstruction` add thirteen checked theorems.
The original geometric cone construction now supplies the two normalized
constant tangent lifts and P.totalDegree=1 without tangent hypotheses.

The entire original four-by-four Jacobian is reconstructed on y=1.
Its first three rows have entries

    M_ij = d A_i(P) delta_(j,1) + A_i'(P) g_j,
    g = (P_x, -x P_x-z P_z-w P_w, P_z, P_w).

The fourth fixed-parameter row is
(B_0,(d-1)L+B_1,B_2,0), where L=B_0 x+B_1+B_2 z;
its parameter-gradient contribution is L' g. The actual chart map
preserves nilpotence. Squared trace minus trace of the square is proved
to be twice an explicit triangular tangent expression. Both traces vanish
by nilpotence, so the full radial-free tangent identity follows.

All five tangent coefficients are original univariate polynomials. Write

    U_0 = d(A_1 A_0'-A_1' A_0),
    U_1 = d(A_1 A_2'-A_1' A_2),
    T_i = d A_1 B_i'-(d-1)A_1' B_i,
    V_0 = T_1-sum_i B_i A_i',  V_1 = T_0,  V_2 = T_2.

The resulting actual identity is

    U_0(P) P_x + U_1(P) P_z + (V_0(P)+V_1(P)x+V_2(P)z) P_w = 0.

Independence of the original components proves independence of the three
scalar polynomials A_i in their common-factor source presentation.
The two horizontal Wronskians above are then independent over K. A
constant relation between them would make
(c_0 A_0+c_1 A_2)/A_1 have zero rational parameter derivative. The exact
constant-field theorem puts this ratio in K, contradicting independence
of the three A_i. Thus horizontal independence is constructed from the
original map, rather than supplied as geometric direction data.

The polynomial identity gives an actual generic tangent over K(t).
The already checked relation ideal and polynomial-flow argument force
its whole polynomial action to vanish. Linear coefficient functionals
then construct both normalized tangent lifts over the original field.
No slope, generic tangent field, horizontal independence assertion or
specialization points are supplied to this construction.

The strengthened theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_geometrically_integral_affine_level`
retains the full specified original cone and fiber data and now constructs
both tangent lifts for its same actual P. Their simultaneous invariant
ring gives P=F(w-Q). Actual primitive-parameter linearity makes F affine;
the original trace exclusion makes the quadratic part of Q zero.
Consequently it constructs Q of degree at most one, a nonzero slope a,
the entire identity P=a(w-Q)+b, and P.totalDegree=1. These conclusions
no longer have independent tangent fields as extra hypotheses.

The final contradiction with the actual nonlinear fiber is constructed
below. The retained fixed-vertex and cone geometry still has to be derived in
the remaining developable classification. The other developable and
quadric exclusions, Proposition 4.1 as a whole, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2
remain incomplete.


## The actual nonlinear fiber completes the final fixed-vertex cone exclusion

`AffineParameterParabolaObstruction`, `VertexPreservingParabolaTransport`,
`ConjugatedAffineParabolaObstruction` and `GeometricFixedConeExclusion`
add nine checked theorems. They close the final contradiction for the
specified original cone and parabolic-fiber hypotheses.

For an affine polynomial P(x,z,w) with nonzero w-partial, its w coefficient
is a nonzero scalar in the original field. If P is constant on an actual
vertex parabola, clearing the actual nonzero chart denominator gives a
linear relation among its four homogeneous source coordinates. The first
three coordinates are affine in the fiber parameter; the vertex coordinate
has nonzero quadratic coefficient C. Substitution gives a univariate
polynomial which vanishes at the actual transcendental fiber parameter.
It therefore vanishes identically, but its quadratic coefficient is the
nonzero w coefficient times C. This is the required contradiction.

The complete inverse source-coordinate change is also constructed on
the source fraction field. Its transverse block takes the first three
actual affine coordinate expressions to new affine expressions over the
same geometric constants. Its vertex coordinate is fixed, preserving
the whole quadratic vertex polynomial and its nonzero coefficient.
The transformed chart denominator is nonzero because the actual source
field realization is injective; no chart-regularity assumption is added.

The actual original geometric component constructs compatible embeddings
of its source field and the entire closed Gauss field into the component
field and geometric constants. Thus the original closed Gauss parameter,
including the constructed constant parameter change, has its actual value
in the geometric constants. Composing the source realization with the
inverse coordinate change gives that same constant value for the actual
transported affine parameter. The preceding coefficient obstruction then
applies to the original parabola.

The combined theorem `geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_data_impossible` starts
from the same original map, original geometric component, reduced
vertex-dependent pencil and retained cone hypotheses as the prior
construction. It constructs a closed Gauss realization g and proves that
every retained cone/fiber presentation over its field range gives False.
The entire tangent construction, affine parameter, compatible source
realization, constant parameter value, nonzero denominator and final
quadratic coefficient comparison are derived. It does not assume the
final affine form or any formal polynomial identity on the curve.

The retained geometry still must be constructed from the original
classification hypotheses: the fixed vertex, moving normal, cone
coordinate presentation and specified quadratic coefficient formula.
This includes keeping the chosen Gauss realization compatible with
those presentations. The other developable and quadric exclusions,
Proposition 4.1 as a whole, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## The fixed vertex constructs its directrix, scalar root and moving normal

`TernaryRulingDirection`, `OriginalConeScalarDirection`,
`ClosedConeScalarDirection`, `GeometricConeScalarRoot`,
`RealizedConeNormalIncidence`, `RealizedConeMovingNormal` and
`GeometricConeMovingNormal` add ten checked theorems. They start from the
actual dimension-one Gauss image and the specified zero fourth normal
coordinate. The first three scalar cone formulas and the moving-normal
incidence are constructed from those data.

The actual Gauss curve constructs independent successive normal covectors.
Their common zero fourth coordinate leaves two independent ternary
covectors. Their cross product is nonzero. The actual first three outputs
are annihilated by both, so normalizing a nonzero cross-product coordinate
constructs a rational vector V with V_j = 1 and the exact original-source
identities H_i = V_i H_j. The polynomial H_j is nonzero. Original component
independence proves linear independence over K of the three V_i. The
construction also proves nu.V = nu'.V = nu.V' = 0.

The inclusion of the original Gauss field in its actual relatively closed
cover transports this entire construction to every rational parameter of
that cover. The source realizations agree exactly. The rational chain rule
proves both derivative incidences after the parameter substitution; no
degree-one assumption on the finite cover is made.

On every actual geometric generic component, compatible embeddings f of
the original source field and g of the entire closed Gauss field realize
these same identities. The normalized generic output H_j is nonzero, and
algebraic closedness constructs R != 0 with R^d equal to that output. The
first three formulas in (4.7) therefore hold with coefficients g(V_i).
Their independence over K and all source and coefficient compatibilities
are retained.

The normalized actual normal also lies in g's exact field range. The
original Gauss dimension and zero vertex coordinate give a nonzero
ternary normal chart with positive-dimensional projective image. Its
incidence with the projected fiber direction is a conclusion: a
separating derivation of the transcendental fiber parameter constructs
the tangent. Differentiating the actual constant output proves that
tangent lies in the realized Jacobian kernel. Rank three and nilpotence
persist through f, so the kernel-image inclusion makes the realized
image normal kill the tangent. Dropping its zero fourth entry gives
nu.A = 0. This argument needs no formula for the fourth fiber coordinate.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_scalar_root_and_moving_normal`
constructs the root formulas, nonlinear directrix, moving normal and
projected-direction incidence using one compatible actual component
realization. The fixed vertex itself is still supplied. The last output
formula in (4.7), its nonzero coefficient b, the quadratic fiber
coefficient kappa/R, and the classification and exclusions needed to
reach this case remain open. These results do not establish the whole
cone case, Proposition 4.1, or Theorems 1.1 and 1.2.


## The actual second root case constructs the complete affine cone formula

`FixedVertexCharacteristicAxis`, `NativeFixedVertexConeForm`,
`HomogeneousConeFormulaTransfer`, `AffineSourceConeForm`,
`HomogeneousRationalRootTransfer`, `AffineFixedVertexRootAlternatives`,
`OriginalFixedVertexAffineRootCases` and `AlgebraicSourceTensorRealization`
add sixteen checked theorems and five definitions. They derive the last
coordinate of (4.7) in the actual second root alternative and return the
entire formula to the original affine source after the actual algebraic
extension of closed Gauss coefficients.

The specified zero fourth image-normal coordinate makes the normalized
fourth coordinate and both successive parameter derivatives zero. The
fourth axis lies in the actual extended characteristic kernel. The
previously proved dimension-one kernel is therefore exactly that axis.
Every nonzero characteristic vector W has its first three entries zero
and W_4 nonzero. The actual characteristic factorization
H = r^(d-1)(r V + L W) now gives all four native formulas
H_i = V_i r^d (i < 4) and H_4 = a r^d + b r^(d-1)L, with b != 0.
Independence of the original factorization vectors makes the projected
V nonzero. Both actual ternary ruling incidences are also derived.

The root has its original rational formula r = N L / M. Clearing M^d
turns each coordinate formula into a homogeneous polynomial identity of
degree d(d+1). In the last coordinate the cleared terms are
H_4 M^d = a (N L)^d + b (N L)^(d-1)L M. The exact homogeneous relation
ideals of the actual native pencil and the actual original source tensor
agree. They therefore transfer the denominator's nonvanishing, the
root's nonvanishing, and every coordinate formula to the fraction field
of that tensor. This field retains all four original affine source
coordinates and the original algebraic coefficient action. The root's
output-linear power identity and the scaled-plane root formula in the
other remaining case transfer in the same way.

The combined theorem
`original_nonplanar_fixed_vertex_map_constructs_affine_root_alternatives`
starts with the original positive-degree homogeneous independent map,
nilpotent rank-three Jacobian, dimension-one Gauss image and specified
fourth-axis vertex. The actual boundary construction supplies its own
finite coefficients with their original closed Gauss action. The result
constructs a nonzero affine rational root and its exact output-linear
power, together with either the scaled-plane case or the complete cone
formula (4.7). Neither root alternatives nor cone-coordinate formulas
are premises of this combined theorem.

A compatible realization of a relatively closed source and algebraic
coefficients also constructs one injective homomorphism of the source
tensor's fraction field, preserving every source and coefficient value.
This supplies a transfer tool for realizing the newly constructed
affine alternatives on the same actual geometric component used earlier.

The scaled-plane alternative is still present and needs exclusion.
The complete second-case formula has not yet been realized together with
the geometric-fiber data of the final cone exclusion. The quadratic
fiber coefficient kappa/R, the remaining classification and exclusions,
Proposition 4.1 as a whole, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain open.


## The complete root alternatives are realized on the actual geometric component

`CompatibleAlgebraicSourceRealization`, `ClosedGaussAlgebraicRealization`,
`RealizedAffineConeRootCases`, `FiniteAffineRootCaseRealization` and
`GeometricFixedVertexRootCases` add seven checked theorems and one
definition. They place both original root alternatives, including the
complete second-case cone formula, on the same actual geometric generic
component with the actual moving normal.

The actual closed Gauss embedding g into algebraically closed geometric
constants extends to every actual algebraic coefficient field. The
source and this coefficient embedding construct one injective map from
the source tensor's fraction field to the geometric component field.
It preserves all four original coordinates and every original polynomial
value. Every extended coefficient image is algebraic over g's exact
field range. The first chosen ruling row maps to the actual normalized
closed Gauss normal, for the same chosen cover parameter.

The affine rational root has a positive-degree power equal to a
coefficient linear combination of the original outputs. Its realized
power therefore lies in the geometric constants. The realized root is
algebraic over those algebraically closed constants, so the proof
constructs its actual preimage R there; source injectivity proves R != 0.
Both remaining root cases transfer with this same R. In the cone case
all four original output formulas hold, including
H_4 = a R^d + b R^(d-1) sum_i nu_i x_i with b != 0. The coefficients
V_i, a and b are algebraic over the same actual closed-field range.
The other branch retains its exact scaled-plane root identity with its
nonzero parameter-algebraic multiplier.

The combined theorem
`geometric_fixed_vertex_map_constructs_realized_root_alternatives`
starts from the original homogeneous nilpotent rank-three independent
map, its dimension-one Gauss image, its specified fourth-axis vertex,
and any actual geometric generic component. The original boundary
construction supplies its own finite coefficients and root alternatives;
the component supplies compatible source and closed Gauss embeddings.
The resulting root cases, nonzero constant root, full second-case cone
formula, moving normal, positive-dimensional normal chart and projected
fiber-direction incidence are constructed together. No cone formula,
root alternative, extended coefficient embedding or normal is supplied.

The scaled-plane branch still needs exclusion. The quadratic fiber
coefficient kappa/R and the remaining classification hypotheses needed
by the final cone exclusion remain open. The whole cone case,
Proposition 4.1 and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 are not yet proved.


## The original Jacobian derives the cone slope and its quadratic coefficient calculation

`RankOneUpdateTrace`, `ConeParameterJacobianSlope`,
`VertexParameterDerivationFrame`, `VertexFrameConeJacobian`,
`ConeRootSlopeIdentity`, `RationalParameterAlgebraicDerivative`,
`ConeQuadraticCoefficient`, `ConstructedConeRootSlope`,
`AlgebraicSourceTensorPartials` and `ConeQuadraticRootNormalization`
add thirty-four checked theorems and five definitions. They formalize
the nilpotent calculation of (4.8)-(4.9) and the subsequent kappa/r
calculation under its retained independent-direction hypotheses.

For a matrix K_0 + c dt, the trace and square trace are expanded exactly.
If trace(K_0^2) = trace(K_0)^2, nilpotence gives
dt(c) = -trace(K_0) and dt(K_0 c) = -trace(K_0)^2. The fixed-parameter
cone block has upper block V f_x, bottom row h_x and zero fourth column;
its square-trace identity and complete parameter-column action are
proved. Constancy of f and h along A gives both scalar equations in
(4.8). Eliminating Z_t gives
Z_A = (h_t - (f_t + alpha f) h_V/f_V)/(beta f).

The source parameter frame is constructed directly from the original
nonzero vertex derivative t_w: T = t_w^(-1) D_w and
delta_i = D_i - (t_i/t_w) D_w. Its coordinate and parameter values,
nonzero Z_t, graph differential dt = (-Z_x,1)/Z_t and complete chain
rule are conclusions. The first three cone values and parameter-algebraic
directrix coefficients construct the full Jacobian update (4.8). The
original polynomial nilpotence survives through the actual extended
original partials, so the update's nilpotence and trace identities are
not additional inputs.

For f = r^d and h = a f + b r^(d-1)L, fixed-parameter differentiation
gives h_V/f_V = a + (d-1)bL/(d r). The root parameter derivative cancels
from the eliminated slope, giving the exact formula
Z_A = (a_t - alpha a)/beta +
((b_t - alpha(d-1)b/d)L + b L_t)/(beta r).
The combined theorem `algebraic_original_cone_root_constructs_graph_slope`
starts from the original polynomial Jacobian in an algebraic source
extension and derives this formula through the constructed frame.

Parameter differentiation preserves the actual relative algebraic closure
of K(t), by the previously proved coefficient chain rule. Every direction
fixing t therefore annihilates the parameter derivatives of all
parameter-algebraic coefficients. The normal linear form has the exact
mixed derivative D_A(L_t) = nu_t.A. Independent normal covectors and
independence of A from V make this pairing nonzero. Differentiating the
slope then gives D_A^2 w = b(nu_t.A)/(beta r). If the actual quadratic
acceleration is 2C, the proof constructs
kappa = b(nu_t.A)/(2 beta), proves it nonzero and parameter-algebraic,
and proves C = kappa/r.

The actual source tensor carrying the cone formula is proved algebraic
over the original source field. Its original coordinate partials,
preservation of every original rational derivative and mixed-partial
commutation are constructed. A nonzero algebraic kappa can also be
absorbed by replacing r with r/kappa: all four cone formulas retain
their form, their coefficients remain parameter-algebraic, the last
coefficient stays nonzero, and C = 1/r_new.

The independent-direction geometry is still retained: A must be
independent of V, V_t must have its stated decomposition, and f_V must
be nonzero. The actual fiber derivatives are connected to this source
frame below. The scaled-plane branch exclusion and the unconditional
geometric quadratic-coefficient construction remain open. Proposition 4.1
and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.

## The actual geometric fiber supplies the source-frame derivative conditions

`AlgebraicSourceDerivationChain`, `ComponentSourceAlgebraicity`,
`ParabolicFiberDerivation`, `GeometricAlgebraicSourceEmbedding`,
`ActualFiberSourceFrame`, `ParameterDirectionAlgebraicity`,
`ActualParabolicConeSlope`, `GeometricAlgebraicRootCases`,
`GeometricQuadraticRootNormalization`, `CompatibleGeometricParameterFrame`
and `GeometricOriginalSourcePartials` add twenty-four checked theorems.

Every derivation on an algebraic extension of the original rational
source has the original coordinate chain rule. A derivation that fixes
the actual parameter and has projected coordinate values A is exactly
the constructed transverse-frame direction. This uses uniqueness of
derivation extension through the entire algebraic source extension,
rather than treating its four coordinates as formal independent symbols.

The original generic coordinates generate the entire prime-quotient
component fraction field. When the geometric constants are algebraic
over the full output field K(H), each actual component is algebraic over
the compatible embedded original source. The same source and closed
Gauss embeddings retain the constructed root alternatives and moving
normal. In the actual quotient field they also construct all four
original coordinate partials, their original rational values, commutation,
the chain rule for every fiber derivation, and the nilpotent original
polynomial derivative matrix.

A transcendental parameter of the actual projected line and vertex
parabola constructs a constant-field derivation with projected values A,
vertex slope B + 2Cs and acceleration 2C. It is proved equal to the
source transverse-frame direction. Constancy of the actual outputs and
root now supplies f_A = h_A = r_A = 0. Thus the combined theorem
`actual_parabolic_original_cone_constructs_slope_and_acceleration`
derives (4.9) and the actual quadratic acceleration from the fiber
equations and original polynomial nilpotence. These derivative conditions
are no longer supplied separately.

The directrix derivative decomposition also proves that the direction
coordinates and the normal-derivative pairing are parameter-algebraic.
Under the retained independent normal and directrix/fiber direction
hypotheses, the combined theorem
`actual_parabolic_original_cone_constructs_nonzero_quadratic_coefficient`
constructs kappa != 0 and C = kappa/R, with kappa parameter-algebraic.
Because C and R are actual geometric constants, kappa descends to the
same constant field. Rescaling R there preserves all four cone formulas,
parameter algebraicity and b != 0, and gives C = 1/R_new.

Compatibility identifies the entire source parameter embedding with
its geometric constant realization. Every cone coefficient algebraic
over the realized closed Gauss field maps to an algebraic coefficient
over the identical source parameter range. A nonzero original vertex
differential survives in this actual algebraic source field, and the
parameter's geometric constant value is constructed.

At this stage the actual projected fiber direction's independence from
the directrix, the derivative decomposition and f_V != 0 were retained.
The following results derive the last two conditions and the required
normal geometry. The competing geometric cases and the remaining surface
classification still keep Proposition 4.1 and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 open.

## The original rank and actual Gauss jets construct the quadratic numerator

`RankThreeDerivationKernel`, `ConeDirectrixDerivativeNonzero`,
`TernaryTangentDecomposition`, `NormalJetTangentDecomposition`,
`RankThreeActualParabolicCone`, `RationalJetMinorTransfer`,
`ClosedGaussNormalJetRealization`, `ClosedConeSourceIncidences`,
`ParameterAlgebraicFieldReflection` and
`ActualClosedConeQuadraticCoefficient` add twenty-five checked theorems.

The original rank-three Jacobian retains rank three in the actual
algebraic source extension. Every derivation killing the original
outputs gives a right-kernel vector through its original coordinate
values. Two such derivations cannot have independent projected directions,
because that kernel has dimension one. If f_V vanished, the full cone
formula would make the constructed directrix direction kill all four
outputs. Together with the actual fiber derivative this contradicts
independence of V and A. Thus f_V != 0 is a conclusion of the original
rank-three hypothesis, without supplied gradient independence.

Two independent directions annihilated by a nonzero ternary normal span
its whole tangent plane. Independent successive normal jets show that
V and V_t are independent and that V_t lies in that plane. The plane
therefore constructs V_t = alpha V + beta A with beta != 0. Actual
nonzero coordinate minors give explicit expressions for alpha and beta,
and prove both parameter-algebraic when the direction coordinates are.

For an arbitrary rational parameter embedding and field derivation, the
normalized three-column normal-jet determinant changes by the cube of
the actual parameter speed. The second derivative of that speed cancels
exactly. This transports the original map's constructed nonzero Gauss
jet minor through every rational closed cover. In the actual source
parameter frame the speed is one. Dropping the specified zero fourth
normal coordinate then constructs three independent ternary normal jets;
their independence is no longer a geometric input.

Every actual first-three-coordinate cone presentation has the constructed
closed Gauss normal and its first parameter derivative as annihilators.
Original nilpotence and rank three make that same normal annihilate the
projected coordinate values of every output-constant derivation. Applying
this to the actual fiber derivation constructs normal-direction incidence.
Algebraicity over the exact parameter image is also proved to reflect
through the source embedding, so the numerator constructed in the source
has the required algebraicity in the geometric constants themselves.

The combined theorem
`actual_closed_fixed_vertex_cone_constructs_quadratic_numerator`
starts from the original homogeneous nonplanar nilpotent rank-three map,
its dimension-one Gauss image and specified fixed vertex, a compatible
actual closed/source realization, a genuinely vertex-dependent parameter,
the full cone expression and actual parabolic fiber equations. For a
parameter-algebraic projected direction independent of V it constructs
C != 0 and kappa != 0 in the same geometric constants, proves kappa
algebraic over the same realized closed Gauss field, and proves C = kappa/R.
Normal jets, both normal/directrix incidences, fiber-direction incidence,
alpha,beta, their algebraicity, f_V != 0 and every fiber derivative are
derived. They are not separate assumptions of this combined theorem.

The preceding result retains the projected direction's parameter
algebraicity and independence from V. The following results remove these
two assumptions from the actual geometric quadratic-coefficient
construction. The complete cone case and remaining source/surface
classification still keep Proposition 4.1 and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 open.

## The proportional direction is excluded and the actual geometric numerator is constructed

`TransverseFrameCommutation`, `SquareZeroRankOneKernel`,
`ConstantKernelAcceleration`, `ConeSquareZeroBlock`,
`ProportionalConeKernelDirection`, `ActualProportionalConeExclusion`,
`NonlinearClosedConeQuadraticCoefficient`, `ParabolaDirectionNormalization`,
`NormalizedClosedConeQuadraticCoefficient` and
`GeometricConeQuadraticCoefficient` add nineteen checked theorems.

The actual parameter and transverse source coordinates determine each
derivation on the algebraic source extension. This proves that its
constructed transverse partials commute. A direction with
parameter-algebraic coordinates commutes with every such partial. If it
fixes a scalar output, it also fixes the entire transverse gradient of
that output.

For the proportional-direction case, the fixed-parameter cone block
K_0 is square-zero and has trace zero. Nilpotence supplies dt(c) = 0 and
dt(K_0 c) = 0. Rank three forces K_0 c != 0: otherwise the complete
Jacobian's cube would vanish, contrary to its one-dimensional kernel.
Thus K_0 c is a constructed nonzero actual kernel vector. The terms
containing f_t cancel from its explicit value, leaving
K_0 c = (f f_(V_t) V, f h_(V_t)). Commuting partials and parameter
coefficient constancy prove that all four entries are constant along
the directrix direction.

A derivative-constant nonzero kernel vector of a rank-three Jacobian
forces zero acceleration whenever one transverse tangent component is
nonzero and constant. The actual parabolic fiber derivative supplies
such a component. If its projected direction were proportional to V,
rescaling that derivative by the proportionality constant would give the
directrix direction. Its zero vertex acceleration contradicts the
actual acceleration 2C != 0. The zero-proportionality possibility is also
excluded using the original nonzero vertex partial and the separating
fiber derivative. Consequently
`actual_nonlinear_parabolic_cone_constructs_independent_projected_directions`
constructs independence of V and A. This exclusion uses only the first
three cone formulas; no last-coordinate cone expression, normal jet or
parameter algebraicity of A is assumed.

For any A_j != 0, the actual parameter change s_new = A_j s gives
A_new = A/A_j, B_new = B/A_j and C_new = C/A_j^2. It preserves
transcendence, the entire original parabolic source point and C_new != 0,
and normalizes (A_new)_j to one. Thus algebraicity of the projective
direction ratios suffices for the quadratic-coefficient calculation;
no algebraicity of the originally scaled vector A is required.

The combined theorem
`geometric_original_cone_constructs_normalized_quadratic_numerator`
starts from the original homogeneous nilpotent rank-three independent
map, its dimension-one Gauss image and specified fourth-axis vertex,
an actual nonlinear geometric parabolic component, a genuinely
vertex-dependent reduced Gauss pencil and the full cone formulas. It
constructs compatible actual source and closed Gauss embeddings. The
original pencil and moving normal construct algebraicity of A_i/A_j
over that same realized closed Gauss field. The preceding exclusion and
normalization then construct kappa != 0 in the same geometric constants,
prove it algebraic over that field, and prove C/A_j^2 = kappa/R.
For a normalized direction A_j = 1 this is C = kappa/R. Direction
algebraicity and independence, normal jets and incidences, derivative
decompositions, f_V != 0, kernel vectors and every derivative condition
are all constructed rather than supplied.

The actual vertex-parabola form, vertex-dependent pencil branch and full
cone presentation remain explicit geometric premises of this combined
result. The following construction joins its compatible realization to
the final geometric exclusion. The scaled-plane alternative and remaining
source/surface classification still keep the main theorems open.

## The constructed coefficient closes the specified full cone branch

`CompatibleGeometricSource`, `GeometricConstructedConeExclusion`,
`NonlinearProjectedDirection` and `GeometricScaledPlaneReduction` add
six checked theorems and one definition. Ten existing construction
theorems, from complete rational cone forms through the final affine
parameter exclusion, are strengthened to retain the original source
embedding compatible with their chosen closed Gauss embedding.

An original rational source-field embedding is determined by all four
original coordinate values. Consequently the actual geometric generic
point determines a unique compatible embedding of the entire original
closed Gauss field into its constants. This identifies the realization
used by the quadratic-coefficient construction with the one used by the
final geometric cone exclusion, including their exact coefficient-field
ranges.

The combined theorem
`geometric_original_full_cone_data_impossible_with_constructed_quadratic_coefficient`
works over the paper's algebraically closed base. Given the original map,
an actual nonlinear vertex parabola and a genuinely vertex-dependent
reduced Gauss pencil, it excludes every full cone presentation in the
compatible actual realization. It constructs C/A_j^2 = kappa/R,
normalizes the actual projected direction to A_j = 1, rescales the
actual cone root to absorb kappa, and preserves all four cone formulas
and their parameter-algebraic coefficients. The constructed moving
normal and actual projected-direction incidence supply the remaining
inputs to the affine-parameter contradiction. No quadratic numerator,
normalized direction, direction independence, algebraicity, normal data
or derivative conditions are supplied as geometric assumptions.

If the projected direction of an actual nonlinear affine fiber vanished,
its first three coordinates would be constant and the entire point would
lie on the original vertical affine line. Thus the nonzero direction
coordinate required for normalization is also constructed.

The theorem
`original_fixed_vertex_map_reduces_to_actual_scaled_plane_root`
starts with the original homogeneous nilpotent rank-three independent
map, its dimension-one Gauss image and specified fixed vertex. It
constructs its own independent output chart, closed Gauss parameter and
finite root alternatives. On an actual nonlinear vertex parabola in the
genuinely vertex-dependent pencil branch, the full cone alternative is
excluded by the preceding theorem. Its actual nonzero root must satisfy
R = gamma L and R^d = sum_i cS_i y_i, with gamma != 0 and cS != 0,
all coefficients parameter-algebraic in the same actual constants.
No root alternatives or cone presentation are assumed by this reduction.

The scaled-plane branch still needs exclusion, including the zero/pole
divisor geometry of Section 4.4.2. The vertex-parabola and
vertex-dependent source-pencil geometry and the remaining surface
classification also remain to be assembled or proved. The complete
cone case, Proposition 4.1 and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.


## Section 4.4.2: local zero/pole intersection and general-level closure

The local algebra and level-closure argument now has complete proofs in
10 new modules (33 theorems and five definitions). The theorem
`normal_branch_zero_pole_general_quotient_level_closure` starts with a
normal Noetherian local domain, its specified fraction field, an actual
quotient f = a/b with b != 0, and actual height-one branch germs at which
f and 1/f respectively vanish. For all coefficient-field constants c
outside a constructed finite set, the original closed point belongs to
the Zariski closure of V(a - c*b) intersected with the original principal
open D(b). The branch valuation subrings and residue maps are constructed;
no codimension-one regularity rule, level-closure hypothesis, exceptional
set, or additional valuation extension is assumed.

The proof first excludes regularity of every translated reciprocal
1/(f-c) by incompatible zero-branch and pole-branch residue
specializations in a local ring. Denominator ideals then prove the
needed normality consequence: every nonregular fraction in a normal
Noetherian domain has an actual height-one prime localization where it
is nonregular. A prime denominator ideal cannot be preserved by its own
fraction, since the finite-module integrality criterion and normality
would make that fraction regular. This gives a principal ideal over
which the denominator prime is minimal, and hence height one.

The actual height-one localizations are proved to be discrete valuation
rings. A pole of 1/(f-c) therefore supplies a height-one level point
specializing to the original closed point. At a fixed prime a rational
function has at most one coefficient-field residue value. There are
only finitely many height-one primes containing b, so only finitely many
constants can make the constructed level point lie outside D(b).
This last step treats the chosen quotient's domain explicitly; closure
on the maximal regular domain alone would not justify it.

This proves the local zero/pole subargument of Section 4.4.2 under its
actual branch-germ hypotheses. Constructing those zero and pole branches
and their common point on the original normalized source pencil is still
open. The scaled-plane branch, the remaining source-pencil and
vertex-parabola geometry, Proposition 4.1, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain
incomplete. The goal is still active.


## Section 4.4.2: original pencil base and the actual root numerator

Nineteen new checked theorems in seven modules connect the local closure
argument to the original source pencil. The affine and realized root
alternatives were also strengthened, and every affected construction
was recompiled. The theorem
`original_fixed_vertex_map_reduces_to_actual_scaled_plane_root` now
retains a parameter-algebraic output numerator cN, its nonzero actual
geometric value, its zero fourth coefficient, and the radical relation
of its original source polynomial on the extended base V(p,q).

For the original homogeneous independent map with Gauss dimension one
and specified fourth-axis vertex, every reduced closed-Gauss pencil
presentation p/q satisfies
H_i in radical((p,q)) for the first three coordinates. This is proved
for every prime containing the base equations and every field-valued
base point; no normal incidence on the base is supplied. The exact
independent-parameter pencil kernel makes the normal/output relation
vanish on the entire base. Its polynomial coefficient equations have
kernel of dimension at most one, and the fixed fourth axis belongs to
that kernel. The scalar-extension argument then forces the three
transverse output values to vanish. The radical equations survive
arbitrary coefficient-ring extension.

The retained actual second-normal root numerator annihilates the vertex:
its zero direction on the ruling is the proved characteristic axis.
Consequently its output polynomial belongs to the radical of the
original coefficient-extended base ideal. This proves n(H)=0 on the
base, including the generic points of its curve components.

The source-field restriction step is also explicit. Every polynomial
relation in the actual affine tensor source belongs to the extended
principal pencil ideal, hence to the original extended base ideal.
Thus the constructed scaled-plane root power gives its polynomial
power congruence on the entire base even where the original source
pencil denominator is zero. No quotient evaluation at those denominator
zeros is assumed.

Finally, at a given branch prime above the base where the tangent-plane
section L is a unit, the actual numerator radical relation constructs a
regular vanishing germ of n(H)/L^d in that prime's actual local algebra.
This removes the zero-germ hypothesis once that branch prime and the
unit condition have been established. It does not construct the branch
prime or its incidence with the pole divisor.

The line classification is now proved in the later section below. The
pole-divisor and common-intersection geometry must still be constructed
on the original normalized pencil. The scaled-plane branch,
remaining source-pencil and vertex-parabola geometry, Proposition 4.1,
and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete. The goal remains active.


### Free graph parameter and the generic ruling-level boundary

Forty-two new theorems and ten definitions in eleven modules construct
the boundary over the entire rational ruling-level field E(lambda),
with lambda = n(H)/L^d. This passage does not choose a scalar level.

The actual height-one zero and pole branches first force the graph
relations to vanish coefficientwise at the constructed normal vertex.
The key algebraic argument works with a polynomial relation P(f) = 0.
If its residue polynomial were nonzero, some original scalar c would
make P(c) a unit. Reversing the translated polynomial makes (f-c)^-1
integral. Normality would then make it regular, contradicting the actual
zero and pole branches. Thus every graph relation has all coefficients
in the vertex maximal ideal.

`rationalGraphResidueHom` consequently maps the actual subalgebra R[f]
inside the original function field to kappa[T], sending f to the free
variable T. Its kernel defines a prime over the original vertex maximal
ideal. Localizing there yields an actual subalgebra of the same function
field containing E(lambda). Its constructed residue map goes to
kappa(T), keeps lambda free, and reduces every original R-coefficient
by the original residue homomorphism. No unspecified replacement field
or specialization of lambda is assumed.

`rational_graph_constructs_generic_level_projective_boundary` uses
these maps and actual regular homogeneous coordinate germs to retain
the original boundary over E(lambda). The original native vertex germs
are also proved to have their exact zero and unit residues. They therefore
identify this boundary with [0:0:0:0:1] in the original four-coordinate
affine source's projective completion.

`actual_affine_root_alternatives_construct_generic_target_solution_vertex_boundary_or_full_cone`
combines the construction with the original root alternatives. It either
retains their full cone form or constructs a coefficient-preserving
embedding alpha: E(lambda) -> F_native, an actual dual ruling basis bS,bN,
and the original normalized source coordinates v_i = w_i/L(w). These
satisfy L(v) = 1 and all four exact equations
H_i(v) = alpha(a^d bS_i + lambda bN_i). The fixed vertex at infinity
belongs to the actual projective closure of this same solution over
E(lambda). The homogeneous pencil, base component, zero and pole branches,
and native power identity are constructed internally.

This E(lambda) result treats the normalized level. The independent
homogeneous scale and original source-field embedding are constructed
in the later section below. Identification with a component over the
original full generic output field K(H), and the finite root-data
transport needed to apply the simple-root infinity theorem, remain open. Those steps,
the Section 4.4.2 scaled-plane contradiction, remaining source-pencil and
vertex-parabola geometry, Proposition 4.1, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain
incomplete. The audit keeps their completion flags false; the goal remains
active.


## Section 4.4.2: constant projection of base primes off the vertex

Twenty-four new checked theorems and three definitions in eight modules
prove the constant-projection calculation from the actual scaled-plane
base power congruence. For any given original base prime Q containing
p,q and avoiding at least one transverse source coordinate, the theorem
`scaled_plane_base_prime_constructs_vertex_line_equations` constructs a
nonzero original-field direction b and an index j with b_j != 0 such
that every polynomial b_j X_i - b_i X_j, for i among the first three
coordinates, belongs to that same Q. The fourth output H4 is not in Q,
and the scaled output covector's fourth coefficient is nonzero.
Neither nonvanishing conclusion is assumed.

The theorem
`scaled_plane_base_prime_all_points_contained_in_vertex_line` then
shows that every field-valued point annihilating that component ideal,
after every coefficient-field extension, lies in the same vector plane
spanned by (b,0) and the fourth axis. Its projectivization is the line
through the specified vertex. The native affine root alternatives now
construct these line equations in their scaled-plane branch, or retain
the complete full-cone alternative, without a supplied base power
congruence or tangent-restriction proportionality.

The proof constructs its own independent parameter model: the algebraic
closure of the rational function field of the component coordinate
field. Its parameter stays transcendental over that whole field, and
the actual algebraic Gauss coefficients embed compatibly with the
original constants and closed Gauss parameter. No disjointness or
tensor-domain condition is supplied.

The algebraic descent step is also proved. A polynomial over a field
extension which is algebraic over the original polynomial ring descends
to that ring when the original field is algebraically closed. Clearing
the algebraic denominator gives an integral polynomial multiple;
integrality of coefficients and polynomial divisibility descend the
multiple and then the polynomial itself. Monic divisors likewise
descend. Applied to the scaled normal power, this constructs projective
descent of the whole polynomial pairing. The actual independent normal
jets make its transverse coefficient system injective, so the original
three source coordinates descend up to one common scalar. This supplies
the fixed projected direction without choosing tangent sample values.

This containment milestone is strengthened to equality and component
existence in the next section. The pole-divisor and common-intersection
geometry on the original normalized pencil remain open.


## Section 4.4.2: full base components and the source vertex intersection

Twenty-eight new checked theorems and two definitions in eight modules
complete the dimension step of the base-line argument.

`vertexLinePrime b j` is the exact kernel of the original-coordinate
two-variable parametrization of the affine cone over the line through
the fourth-axis vertex. It is prime. An explicit nonzero hyperplane
prime strictly below it proves its height is at least two. The original
cross-coordinate line equations factor any component's generic point
through this parametrization; they imply containment of the entire
line prime, not merely pointwise incidence.

Krull's height theorem bounds every minimal prime over the two original
base equations by two. Every prime with that height bound avoids some
transverse source coordinate: otherwise the same line-prime comparison
would force equality with a coordinate line prime that excludes one
of the assumed coordinates. Thus the off-vertex generic-coordinate
hypothesis of the preceding projection theorem is constructed.

The theorem `scaled_plane_base_component_eq_vertexLinePrime` now
identifies every minimal prime of the actual original base ideal with
a full vertex-line prime, and gives height exactly two. Its direction
is over the original field. The fourth output is nonzero generically
on that component, and the scaled output covector has nonzero fourth
coefficient. Neither conclusion is supplied as a hypothesis.

The base is also constructed rather than assumed nonempty. Positive
homogeneous equations vanish at the origin, so their ideal is proper.
For the actual coprime reduced closed Gauss pencil, this constructs a
minimal prime of height exactly two. The native root alternatives
construct their own positive homogeneous pencil and nonempty base
components, then either retain their full-cone formula or classify
every component as a full vertex line. They require no supplied base
power congruence, component, height bound, or line direction.

Exact line-prime equations commute with the actual coefficient
extension. Independent normal jets show that the actual chosen
generic normal pairs nontrivially with every nonzero original
transverse direction. Consequently its plane contains the source
vertex but contains no whole original vertex line. The theorem
`scaled_plane_base_component_constructs_vertex_normal_intersection`
links the same original component to its full extended line, places
that line in every extended pencil member, and proves that it and the
actual normal plane share the source vertex.

This source-incidence milestone is lifted to the actual normalization
in the next section. The required pole orders there remain open.


## Section 4.4.2: normalized base branches, vertex points and quotient zero germs

Twenty-six new checked theorems and two definitions in nine modules
construct the base-line specialization on the actual normalized pencil
and its actual vanishing quotient germ.

The line prime is proved homogeneous, using a general theorem that
degree-preserving polynomial homomorphisms preserve every homogeneous
component. It therefore defines its actual projective generic point.
The fourth-axis vertex is also an actual point of Mathlib's projective
spectrum, and the line's generic point specializes to it. These are
points of the same original-coordinate projective space, not auxiliary
points with an assumed comparison map.

The normalization in an integral scheme's own function field is
dominant by its checked generic-point factorization, and is surjective
because its morphism is integral and closed. Specializations lift on
the same branch. The exact reduced source parameter equation places
the extended full original line on the actual extended projective
pencil. Consequently
`original_base_vertex_line_constructs_normalized_branch_and_vertex`
constructs a normalized point above its generic point and a point
above the vertex in that normalized point's closure. The stronger
given-branch theorem proves this for every normalized generic preimage.
No normalization point, nonempty fiber, or branch incidence is assumed.

`scaled_plane_base_component_constructs_normalized_branch_through_vertex`
applies this to every constructed original base component. Its actual
vertex-annihilating output numerator belongs to the extended line
prime, whereas the actual normal form L does not. Both the line's
generic point and the vertex lie in the fourth-coordinate chart.
The native affine root alternatives also construct the independent
output covectors s,n: s is nonzero at the vertex, n annihilates it,
and the original output numerator is nonzero. They retain the exact
root-power and scaled-plane identities while constructing these
normalized branches, or retain their full-cone alternative.

The local statements use actual regular polynomial sections and
actual scheme stalks. A homogeneous polynomial belongs to a projective
point's ideal exactly when its regular chart coefficient belongs to
that point's chart prime, or its germ belongs to the maximal ideal.
The same equivalence holds after pullback through the specified
scheme morphism, since its stalk maps are local.

Finally,
`scaled_plane_base_component_constructs_normalized_quotient_zero_germ`
constructs a regular element of the actual normalized branch stalk
whose value in that normalization's own function field is n(H)/L^d
and which belongs to its maximal ideal. On the same branch closure it
constructs a point above the vertex where the actual regular
denominator coefficient vanishes. The fraction is expressed using
the same pulled-back fourth-chart polynomial sections, so the source
equations, normalized points, stalk and function-field value are linked.

The height-one local branches and the canonical-chart pole argument
are now checked in the next section. Their localization comparison and
the connection to the original generic-fiber boundary remain open.


## Section 4.4.2: height-one local branches and canonical-chart poles

Twenty-six new checked theorems and one definition in eleven modules
construct the local branches and prove the common-factor pole argument.

`scaled_plane_base_component_constructs_local_height_one_branches`
starts with an actual original scaled-plane base component and its
nonzero actual numerator. It constructs a normalization point above
the source vertex, then two distinct height-one primes in that same
point's actual local ring. The numerator vanishes on the first, and
the denominator is nonzero there. The denominator vanishes on the
second. No local branch prime, branch height, vanishing germ, or
valuation extension is supplied. The first branch carries an actual
regular vanishing germ with value exactly n(H)/L^d in the normalization's
own function field; the original homogeneous frame cancels explicitly.

The construction uses the existing specialization from the normalized
base-line generic point to the vertex point. Its stalk map pulls back
the generic point's maximal ideal to a separating prime in the vertex
stalk. The nonzero numerator supplies a minimal height-one prime below
it. Denominator nonvanishing survives passage to that smaller prime.
A denominator zero in the vertex stalk independently supplies the
second height-one prime. Original affine-source nonvanishing, actual
projective point equations, native coefficient germs and stalk
specialization are all linked by checked equalities.

The ruling-span data are also derived. The actual vertex is in the
chosen ruling. The scaled output covector is nonzero there, whereas
the actual numerator covector vanishes there. Its nonzero output value
shows that its restriction to the ruling is a nonzero form. These facts
construct independent ruling restrictions, which then construct the
original four component-span coefficients after scalar extension.

`actual_fixed_vertex_scaled_plane_quotient_nonregular_over_source_divisor`
proves the pole argument at every actual denominator zero over an
original source divisor, for a finite coefficient extension. If n(H)/L^d
were regular, its numerator would be an actual local multiple of L^d.
The scaled-power identity gives the same ideal-power bound for s(H).
The constructed component spans then put every original H_i in the
d-th power of the local maximal ideal. The proved Lemma 4.2 would force
the original homogeneous common factor to have degree at least d.
The original map constructs its primitive factorization with strictly
smaller common-factor degree, giving the contradiction. No component
order, primitive factorization, numerical degree bound, independent
ruling restrictions, or output spans are supplied to this theorem.

`actual_normal_plane_divisor_scaled_quotient_nonregular` applies this
argument to every height-one divisor in every actual canonical tensor
normalization chart where the normal coefficient vanishes. Its
original source image has codimension one, derived from the actual
Gauss-plane residue incidence and the previously proved source-dimension
calculation. The actual input-linear section germ is proved equal to
the same original polynomial coefficient germ used in the pole test.

The local zero/pole closure criterion now constructs both branches
internally from a separating local map and a denominator zero. It
requires nonregularity only on the actual height-one denominator
divisors. Such a pole constructs its own vanishing reciprocal germ in
the same height-one localization and yields the general-level closure.

The subsequent localization and projective-level results below complete
the transport to the actual vertex stalk and construct its general
level closures. Identification with the original generic affine-fiber
boundary and the simple-root contradiction remain open. The remaining
source-pencil and vertex-parabola geometry, Proposition 4.1, and
Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete. The goal remains active.


### Actual vertex-stalk poles and general output-target levels

Twenty-two new theorems in eleven modules connect the canonical chart
pole theorem to the constructed vertex stalk, prove general-level
closure there, and identify those levels by the original output equations.

`affine_stalk_prime_constructs_point_with_same_regular_fractions`
constructs an actual affine-chart point from any prime in a specified
actual stalk. It preserves the prime height, the vanishing test for every
regular section, and regularity of every element of the scheme's own
function field. The proof compares iterated localizations using their
actual coefficient maps. No replacement fraction field or supplied
isomorphism is used. Every actual normalized-chart point is also proved
to arise from a canonical normalization-ring prime with the same intrinsic
codimension.

`actual_stalk_denominator_height_one_prime_scaled_quotient_pole`
therefore proves the pole test for every height-one prime of the actual
vertex stalk containing the original normal coefficient. The source
codimension and canonical chart prime are constructed internally. A second
theorem states this for the quotient of the actual numerator and normal
germs, using explicit cancellation of the original homogeneous frame.
These results retain the finite coefficient extension and exact native
scaled-power hypotheses of the preceding pole theorem.

`scaled_plane_base_component_constructs_vertex_general_level_closure`
applies these pole results to the actual normalized base-line branch and
its vertex specialization. It constructs a finite exceptional set of
constants outside which the local closed point lies in the closure of
the numerator/normal-power quotient level on the prescribed denominator
open. No zero prime, pole prime, branch height, or regularity criterion
is supplied.

The canonical map from the spectrum of the stalk then sends these local
levels into the same original affine-chart levels. Their native regular
coefficients are linked to the actual projective point equations. Thus
`scaled_plane_base_component_constructs_vertex_projective_general_levels`
places the same normalization point above the vertex in the closure of
every general homogeneous level n(H) = lambda L^d on L != 0.
The affine root alternatives construct the original positive homogeneous
pencil, a base component, the native scaled-power identity, and this point,
or retain their full-cone alternative.
