The scaled output forms also construct their actual dual ruling basis
bS, bN, including all four pairings with s and n and both ruling
memberships. `actual_scaled_plane_levels_iff_original_output_target_equations`
proves at every actual normalization point that n(H) = lambda L^d is
equivalent to all four original equations
H_i = (c bS_i + lambda bN_i) L^d, where s(H) = c L^d.
The final root-alternatives theorem combines these equations with the
vertex closure. Its constructed targets a^d bS + lambda bN are nonzero,
lie in the original actual ruling, and have s-value a^d and n-value lambda.

The next results lift these homogeneous target loci to actual affine
fibers with L fixed to one and construct individual affine branches
whose own projective closures contain the vertex at infinity. Passage
to the original full generic output field, followed by the simple-root
exclusion from Section 4.3, remains open. The scaled-plane branch
exclusion and the main dependence and tameness theorems remain
unproved; the audit keeps those completion flags false.


### Actual affine fiber lift and constructed boundary branches

Twenty-two new theorems and six definitions in seven modules construct
the affine lift and an actual affine solution branch for every general
target in the scaled-plane alternative.

`linearHyperplaneProjectiveGraph` is the actual everywhere-defined
scheme morphism [X] -> [L(X):X]. Its positive-degree images contain all
original source variables, so it is defined also at L = 0. Polynomial
equation membership is exactly substitution of L for the homogenizing
coordinate. Its affine chart pulls back precisely to the original L != 0
open, and its action on original or extension-valued points retains all
original coordinate values.

The graph identifies the original homogeneous target level
H_i = Y_i L^d on L != 0 with the projective affine chart defined by
L = 1 and H_i = Y_i. The affine locus is explicit: its equations are
L(X) - X_0 and H_i(X) - Y_i X_0^d, with X_0 != 0.
Every prime of this chart, including a nonclosed prime, constructs its
own normalized-coordinate field and affine coordinates solving exactly
those original equations. This does not assume field-valued coordinates
or an independent identification of function fields.

The actual original normal form vanishes at the fixed vertex. The graph
therefore sends that vertex to [0:0:0:0:1] in the original affine source's
projective completion. The root alternatives now either retain the full
cone form or construct this infinity point in the closure of every
general L = 1 affine target fiber. The targets are the same nonzero
ruling vectors a^d bS + lambda bN, with the same original s- and n-values.

The stronger theorem
`actual_affine_root_alternatives_construct_affine_solution_vertex_boundary_or_full_cone`
constructs an individual affine solution, rather than only the closure
of the entire target locus. A general topology lemma proves that a
point in the closure of a locally closed subset of a Noetherian sober
space is a specialization of an actual point of that subset. Applied
to the actual normalized target level, it supplies a specializing
normalization point. The graph and its normalized coordinate field then
give v with L(v) = 1 and every H_i(v) equal to the stated target.
An affine projective-chart point is proved equal to the projective
closure generic point of its own normalized coordinates. The same
specialization consequently places the vertex at infinity in the actual
`projectiveClosureSet` of this constructed v.

The result concerns general targets over the finite extended Gauss
parameter field. It does not yet supply the missing identification with
a component over the original full generic output field. That passage
must preserve the two independent target parameters (position on the
ruling and homogeneous scale) and the finite root data used in the
simple-root boundary theorem. The final Section 4.4.2 contradiction,
remaining source-pencil and vertex-parabola geometry, Proposition 4.1,
and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete. The goal remains active.


### Independent homogeneous scale and the original rational source

Twenty-four new theorems, eight definitions and one abbreviation in nine
modules adjoin the independent homogeneous scale and reconstruct the
original rational source embedding. The generic ruling parameter and
homogeneous scale now remain free simultaneously.

`rationalScalarLocalization` is the actual localization of A[r] obtained
by inverting only nonzero polynomials in r over the existing coefficient
field L. Both the original coefficient inclusion A -> F and its actual
specialization A -> kappa extend to this localization, with values in
F(r) and kappa(r). The first extension is proved injective. This permits
the same specialization after adjoining the free scale, even when the
original specialization has a nontrivial kernel.

`independent_rational_scale_preserves_residue_projective_boundary`
applies these maps to actual regular homogeneous coordinates. The
homogenizing coordinate stays unchanged while all four affine coordinates
acquire the same new variable r. The fixed vertex specializes to
[0:0:0:0:r], which is proved to represent exactly the original fixed axis.
Thus the actual graph boundary retains its vertex over E(lambda)(r).
The variable r is transcendental over the entire native source field;
it is independent of the already free lambda.

`actual_affine_root_alternatives_construct_independently_scaled_generic_target_boundary_or_full_cone`
constructs this situation from the original root alternatives, or retains
their full cone form. Its actual source coordinates are x_i = r v_i,
where v_i = w_i/L(w). They satisfy L(x) = r and all four original output
equations
H_i(x) = gamma(r^d (a^d bS_i + lambda bN_i)).
The coefficient-preserving embeddings of E(lambda) and E(lambda)(r),
the actual dual output basis, and the vertex in the projective closure
of this same original four-coordinate solution are constructed internally.

`native_original_homogeneous_polynomial_zero_iff` proves that no nonzero
homogeneous polynomial over the original field K vanishes at the actual
native original projective source coordinates. The proof uses the actual
source tensor and its injective original field inclusion. Normalization
by the nonzero form L preserves this property. Adjoining r separates
all homogeneous components of an arbitrary polynomial relation, so the
four coordinates r v_i are proved algebraically independent over K.

`nativeIndependentScaleSourceFieldHom` consequently embeds the actual
original field K(X_1,X_2,X_3,X_4) into F_native(r). It retains each original
coordinate label, every original polynomial, and the specified original
ground-field coefficient map. Its polynomial values are also proved
equal to evaluation of the extended original polynomials at the same
scaled native coordinates. This connects the original output values to
the exact constructed target equations.

The following results identify the coefficient realization of the
original closed Gauss field under this source embedding and prove the
algebraic coefficient-extension comparison with K(H). Transport of the
finite root data to the same geometric generic-fiber component remains
open. The simple-root contradiction, Section 4.4.2 scaled-plane
exclusion, remaining source-pencil and vertex-parabola geometry,
Proposition 4.1, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete. Their audit
flags remain false; the goal remains active.


### Actual algebraic full-output extension and a full generic-fiber component

Twenty-two new theorems and two definitions in ten modules connect the
constructed two-parameter boundary with an actual component of the
original full generic fiber over an algebraic extension of K(H).

The independently scaled source embedding first retains every original
equal-degree homogeneous quotient, with its exact native value. The
original homogeneous representation of the closed Gauss parameter then
proves compatibility on that parameter. Extensionality of rational-field
homomorphisms extends this equality to the entire closed Gauss field.
Every original closed Gauss coefficient therefore has exactly its specified
native coefficient value under the same original source embedding.

The original Gauss field lies in the full output field. Thus every closed
Gauss coefficient is algebraic over K(H). Compatibility of coefficient
images carries this algebraicity to the given algebraic extension E.
All these assertions use the full output action induced by the actual
source embedding. An unrelated coefficient embedding is not substituted.

`compatibleFullOutputFieldHom` constructs an embedding of the entire
original output field from the common source and target realization.
It preserves every prescribed output coordinate and commutes with the
source embedding on every element of K(H). Applying it to the actual
scaled target gives delta: K(H) -> E(lambda)(r), with
H_i mapping to r^d (a^d bS_i + lambda bN_i).

The two original dual output pairings recover r^d times a^d and r^d times
lambda from the actual output values. Since every E-coefficient image is
algebraic over K(H), r^d is algebraic there, and positivity of d makes r
algebraic. Division by its nonzero power gives algebraicity of lambda.
Polynomial numerators and denominators then prove algebraicity of the
entire rational field E(lambda)(r) over the same delta realization of K(H).
Both lambda and r remain free in the constructed field; this argument
does not replace them by scalar values.

`compatible_algebraic_source_relations_form_actual_component` proves the
component identification. The actual coordinate relation prime contracts
to the original full generic-fiber relation prime through the compatible
injective source embedding. The algebraic coefficient extension makes
the polynomial coefficient map integral. Incomparability then proves
that this actual relation prime is minimal over the extended original
fiber ideal.

`actual_affine_root_alternatives_construct_algebraic_full_output_vertex_boundary_or_full_cone`
constructs all of these data from the original root alternatives, or
retains their full cone form. It provides the same original four-coordinate
source realization, both free parameters, the exact full-output embedding,
algebraicity of its coefficient extension, an actual minimal full generic-
fiber component, and the fixed original vertex at infinity in the
projective closure of that component's actual source coordinates.

The following results extend this boundary to algebraically closed
constants on an actual geometric component. Transport of the finite root
forms, dual ruling pair and root identities to that same component with
compatible coefficient realization remains open. That transport,
the Section 4.4.2 scaled-plane exclusion, remaining source-pencil and
vertex-parabola geometry, Proposition 4.1, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2
remain incomplete. Their audit flags remain false; the goal remains active.


### Geometric component through the original fixed vertex

Twelve new theorems in six modules extend the constructed boundary to
algebraically closed algebraic constants while retaining an actual
component of the original full generic fiber and its original source labels.

A minimal prime over the coefficient extension of an original homogeneous
ideal is proved homogeneous. Its prime homogeneous core contains each
original generator, by decomposition into homogeneous components.
Minimality then identifies this core with the entire prime.

`projective_rational_boundary_constructs_extended_projective_component`
uses the actual extended original boundary point to choose a minimal
projective component through it. Flat coefficient extension makes the
component prime contract to the exact original cone prime. The original
homogenizing coordinate does not belong to that prime, so the selected
component meets the original affine chart. Its generic point specializes
to the prescribed extended original boundary point.

The component's own normalized affine coordinates consequently have that
same boundary in their actual projective closure. Homogenization and
dehomogenization prove exact contraction of every affine polynomial
relation, including nonhomogeneous relations. For algebraic coefficients,
this affine relation prime is a minimal component of the extended original
affine ideal. Component minimality is also proved transitive through the
algebraic coefficient tower, using the exact two prime contractions.

`actual_affine_root_alternatives_construct_geometric_full_output_vertex_boundary_or_full_cone`
now constructs an actual geometric full generic-fiber component through
the fixed vertex, or retains the full cone alternative. Its constants
are the algebraic closure of the same E(lambda)(r), with the same embedding
of the entire original K(H). They are proved algebraic over K(H).
The component's own four normalized source coordinates have
[0:0:0:0:1] in their projective closure. Their relation prime contracts
exactly to the relation prime of the independently scaled native source
coordinates over E(lambda)(r). The two free parameters, original dual
output basis, exact output values and original coefficient labels are retained.

`full_generic_component_coordinates_construct_source_realization`
constructs the original rational source-field embedding from any such
actual component, preserving all four source coordinates and the entire
full-output action. A second general comparison proves that equality of
the original coefficient-field coordinate relations preserves every
rational source value already realized as a specified coefficient constant.
It compares actual polynomial numerators and denominators under the two
source embeddings; no unrelated coefficient embedding is chosen.

### The same geometric vertex component retains the original affine tensor and finite root equations

`full_generic_component_constructs_coefficient_compatible_source` combines

Compatibility on this entire closed field constructs a map from the

R^d=s(Y), the nonzero numerator and denominator n(Y),m(Y), the vanishing

component with its own original source coordinates. No new conjugate

The original normal ruling now supplies both exact graph equations after

and s(a) nonzero construct independence of these precise forms m,s on

`actual_normalized_boundary_annihilates_specified_simple_root_form`

The same component's original source realization now also constructs an

These comparisons are now instantiated together for the same actual

`canonical_scaled_plane_root_has_no_actual_fixed_vertex_boundary`

### Actual nonlinear vertex-parabolic components are excluded in both pencil cases

`affine_full_cone_transfers_to_actual_source_realization` transports the
complete affine cone form through the same original source and closed

algebraic extension of the original source field. The closed Gauss
parameter can be specified in advance.

For coordinates `x=x0+s A`, `w=w0+s B+s^2 C`, with a transcendental
parameter, nonzero projected direction and nonzero quadratic vertex
coefficient, `VertexParabolaGeometry` constructs the nondegenerate
parabola and proves nonlinearity. A separating derivation gives a

derivative, excluding every affine-line presentation.

`original_fixed_vertex_map_has_no_vertex_dependent_parabolic_component`
constructs the original finite cone form in the same realization used by

vertex-dependent pencil and these actual parabolic coordinates give a

numerator, normal jet, algebraic direction or independent directrix.

The transverse case uses a coprime-base dimension argument. Two
vertex-independent homogeneous pencil members descend to an actual
coprime ternary pair, retaining their original degree and polynomial
values. Constancy on the fiber forces both to vanish at its projected
direction. A nonconstant direction, multiplied by an independent radial
variable, would generate a field of dimension at least two. A prime
containing the original coprime ternary pair has dimension below two.
Hence every projective direction ratio is algebraic over the original
field. The checked fixed-plane case of Proposition 2.3 then contradicts
independence of the original map.

`original_fixed_vertex_map_has_no_nonlinear_vertex_parabolic_component`
constructs its own closed parameter and positive reduced homogeneous

Its hypotheses are the original algebraically closed characteristic-zero
fixed-vertex rank-three nonplanar Gauss-dimension-one map hypotheses and
the actual component's displayed nonlinear vertex-parabola coordinates.
No pencil, cone presentation, root or derivative data are assumed.

Construction of those vertex-parabola coordinates for the supplied
nonlinear component is still open, including the required degree and
infinity-direction comparison. The remaining developable classification
and smooth-quadric exclusion, Proposition 4.1, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2
are also incomplete. Their completion flags remain false.

### Constant vertices now have checked coordinate normalization at the map level

`ImageRelationConjugacy` transports the original irreducible homogeneous

### Segre ruling-preservation differential calculation in Section 5.1

`SegreRulingRestrictionMatrix` proves the exact determinant and second
trace identities for equation (5.2), retaining the Euler term in the
dominance factor. `SegreRulingNilpotenceObstruction` derives the slope
relation from the determinant, differentiates that relation, and obtains
the contradictory second trace. Both nonzero degree factors follow
from `2*j < d` in characteristic zero. Dominance is retained as the
original Wronskian `f*g' - g*f'`.

`SegreRulingTangentReduction` constructs the faithful invariant three-space
inside the full four-dimensional reduced action. Nilpotence on that
space follows from the ambient operator. `SegreRulingTangentFrame`
constructs the simultaneous input/output changes `b' = b - A*v` and
`v = (alpha, s*alpha+beta)`, with an explicit inverse, and identifies the
raw action with the normalized one. `SegreRulingHomogeneousProfiles`
works at an arbitrary nonzero radial coordinate. Its invertible radial
frame and nonzero common scalar replace the informal specialization
`x = 1`, preserving the actual generic field throughout.

`MatrixNilpotentIntertwiner` supplies faithful invariant-matrix nilpotence
transfer and nilpotence after regular specialization from a faithful
generic realization. `SegreRulingLocalTangentModel` constructs a regular
normalized tangent matrix and its exact polynomial identity with the
output chart Jacobian. Every transverse derivative term has its explicit
uniformizer factor. Specialization at that uniformizer gives the full
residue chart action.

`SegreRulingLocalJacobianReduction` derives the chart Jacobian from the
exact output rows `pi^j*(f,g)` and `(t+pi*a)*pi^j*(f,g)`. A nilpotent
generic Jacobian and its specified chain rule construct nilpotence of
the residue tangent matrix; no nilpotence of a reduced matrix is supplied.
`SegreOriginalSourceChart` constructs those coordinate derivations on
the original rational source field, proves that the uniformizer is

### The original Segre graph and the two actual infinity points

`SegreGraphDifferentials` proves the gradient and Hessian of the original

`SegreOriginalTwoInfinityPoints` completes this existence argument from

`original_segre_nonlinear_geometric_fiber_has_two_infinity_points`

### Section 5.3: canonical global power factors from exact ruling presentations

`BinaryLinearPowerHessian` proves the exact binary Hessian identity
`Hess(L_a * L_b^d) = -d^2 * det(a,b)^2 * L_b^(2*d-2)`.
`BinaryLinearPowerDescent` uses it to descend a complete nonzero
linear-times-dth-power factorization across any characteristic-zero field
embedding when `d>1`. The proportional-factor case is also proved, using

coefficient realization. `UnivariateLinearPowerDescent` homogenizes the
actual affine factors, applies the binary descent, and dehomogenizes. It
covers constant affine factors and roots at infinity without extra cases.

`FactorizationPowerRemainder` constructs the actual normalized-prime
remainder and quotient by reducing each original exponent modulo `d` and
dividing it by `d`. `NormalizedPrimeFactorProducts` checks their exact
multiplicities. `PrimeExponentDivisibility` and `PowerRemainderUniqueness`
prove their divisibility characterization and uniqueness up to units when
the remainder has all exponents below `d`. No divisor decomposition is
assumed.

`FractionPolynomialPrimeLift` constructs a primitive irreducible polynomial
over a coefficient domain from each fraction-field polynomial prime.
`FractionPolynomialPowerFree` reflects powers of that prime using Gauss's
lemma, proving that the exponent bound survives coefficient localization.
`FractionPolynomialCanonicalFactors` consequently compares the original
and fraction-field canonical factors. `LinearPolynomialPowerRemainder`
bounds their original degrees by one from an actual rational ruling
presentation. `EquivPowerCanonicalFactors` retains the same canonical
factors, up to units, under a ring equivalence.

`BivariateRulingPowerFactorization` applies these results to both original
variables and their exchange. Two exact rational ruling identities produce
one exact original-constant identity `P = R * B^d`, with both `R` and `B`
nonzero and of degree at most one in each variable. The original unit is
absorbed into `R`. `BivariateAlgebraicRulingFactors` derives this global
factorization when the two exact ruling identities are initially over
arbitrary extension fields of the original rational coefficient field.

`BivariateBilinearCoefficients` proves the transposed coefficient formula
and the exact four-term form of each bilinear factor.
`SegreBilinearHyperplaneLift` constructs the two nonzero constant covectors
in the original Segre order `1,s,t,st`. Their hyperplane evaluations give
exactly the original radial-product identity (5.3).
`SegreRulingGlobalPowerReduction` packages the entire argument without
supplied normalization choices or a global divisor decomposition, retaining
nonzero values on the original output. It also derives `d>1` from the
original homogeneous nilpotent and component-independence hypotheses.

These conditional global-factor results are now instantiated from the
actual original finite-boundary factorizations below. Their selected

degree two. The whole-paper goal remains open for the outstanding cases
listed above.

### Proposition 2.1: the positive classified-direction argument

The paper invokes the homogeneous quasi-translation classification in
[de Bondt, Corollary 5.9](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1501.05168), then treats a
primitive positive-degree direction `(0,0,A(x,y),B(x,y))`. The argument
after that coordinate choice is now checked over every original
characteristic-zero field. The external classification is an outstanding
proof obligation and is not asserted as a theorem or an axiom here.

`InvariantVectorTraceConstraints` constructs the polynomial directional
derivation from the actual original kernel vector. Differentiating its
invariant equations gives the full identity `D(JH)=-JH*Ju`. The original
nilpotent trace and squared trace then give
`trace(JH*Ju)=trace((JH)^2*Ju)=0` with the exact factor two retained in the
second derivative calculation.

`TwoAxisKernelTraceReduction` computes both actual traces in the
two-axis direction. The kernel equation constructs the vertical
derivative vector and the two binary Wronskian constraints.
`BinaryAnnihilatorEulerReduction` combines them with the original source
Euler equations. A nonzero first-pair vertical derivative forces the
cross equation `X0*H1=X1*H0`. `NilpotentBinaryRadialPair` differentiates
this equation, constructs the polynomial scaled left eigenvector
`(-X1,X0,0,0)` and forces both outputs to vanish. Its scaled nilpotent
eigenvalue lemma works directly over an integral domain.

`BinaryBlockNilpotence` proves that an actual split matrix quotient
inherits nilpotence and constructs the leading binary block when the
vertical derivatives vanish. `NilpotentBinaryOutputPair` uses that
block's zero determinant and the original homogeneous differential
relation to construct a constant component relation. This includes the
zero-gradient cases.

`PrimitiveBinaryDirection` derives both nonzero entries and the nonzero
Wronskian from primitivity, binary dependence and positive homogeneity.
`TwoAxisKernelRadialOutput` supplies the entire nonzero-derivative
branch in the original polynomial source coordinates. The theorem in
`ClassifiedBinaryDirectionDependence` combines both branches, with no
supplied trace, Wronskian nonvanishing, integration, invariant-ring
factorization or radial output identity.

`PolynomialKernelConjugacy` and `PrimitivePolynomialConjugacy` preserve
the actual original right-kernel equation and primitivity under inverse
linear source-target changes. `ClassifiedKernelCoordinateDependence`
also derives homogeneity and nilpotence after that change, so the
dependence conclusion holds for the original map. Its final theorem
constructs the original nonzero primitive homogeneous right kernel and
retains this complete positive-degree classified branch. The actual
classification coordinate choice remains an explicit input to that
branch; the theorem does not claim to construct it from line fibers.

The audit records this classified branch as complete and
`proposition_2_1_complete` as false. The primitive direction's nonvanishing,
its zero acceleration on an actual line component, and the resulting
quasi-translation construction are now checked as described below.
The homogeneous quasi-translation classification and the degree-zero
kernel case remain separate obligations.

### Proposition 2.1: actual line fibers construct the primitive quasi-translation

`PrimitiveVectorGenericAvoidance` proves that a prime containing every
component of a nonzero primitive vector in four variables has residue
transcendence degree below three. It constructs an actual principal prime
from a hypothetical dimension-three common-zero prime, contradicting
primitivity. `OriginalPrimitiveKernelNonvanishing` applies this to the
independent output triple selected from the original Jacobian rank. Thus
the primitive vector has no zero at any point over the full generic output,
including the entire zero locus of every actual geometric component.

`GeometricComponentSourceEvaluation` constructs an embedding of the whole
original source field into every actual component field. It retains all
original polynomial values, algebraic independence of the four original
coordinates, and the original Jacobian rank. `FullDimensionAffineLineParameter`
uses the actual generic constant field's transcendence degree three to
prove that any affine-line parameter for those four coordinates is
transcendental over the generic constants.

`AffineLinePolynomialRestriction` constructs actual univariate restrictions
and their exact directional derivative. `AffineLineKernelConstancy` uses
the original rank-three Jacobian to place the restricted primitive vector
in the one-dimensional line direction. Since the vector has no affine
zero, its polynomial multiplier has no root over the closed generic
constants and is constant. `OriginalLineFiberKernelAcceleration` then
reflects the resulting identity faithfully back to the original source:
`(Ju)u=0`. It constructs the primitive homogeneous kernel from the original
homogeneous rank-three map; no acceleration or kernel nonvanishing is
assumed.

`ZeroAccelerationPolynomialTranslation` integrates that identity using
the checked coefficient-derivation recurrence. It constructs the actual
substitutions `X+t u`, proves that both `u` and every original polynomial
map with Jacobian kernel `u` are invariant, proves the additive parameter
law, and constructs polynomial algebra automorphisms with inverses
`X-t u`.

Finally, `OriginalRankThreeQuasiTranslationReduction` constructs its own
actual component over an algebraic closure of `K(H)`. For an independent
original map over closed original constants, the checked nonlinear-fiber
branch forces this component to lie on a line. Its native endpoint
`original_independent_rank_three_map_constructs_polynomial_quasi_translation`
therefore needs no supplied component, line hypothesis, field extension,
primitive vector, acceleration equation, or polynomial inverse. It
constructs all of them from the original homogeneous nilpotent rank-three
map and independence. This completes the reduction to the external
homogeneous quasi-translation classification, which remains unproved
here. Proposition 2.1 and the whole-paper goal remain incomplete.

### Proposition 2.1: the remaining classification is the rank-two span bound

`PrimitiveHomogeneousQuasiTranslationRank` extracts the highest coefficient
of the actual formal-parameter identity, proving that every positive
homogeneous invariant vanishes on the direction. In particular `u(u)=0`.
The prime of the actual image of `u` therefore contains every component of
`u`. Primitivity bounds its residue transcendence degree below three; an
independent output triple contradicts rank three. The existing homogeneous
rank-one argument excludes ranks zero and one. Thus the actual primitive
positive zero-acceleration direction has rank exactly two, over every
original characteristic-zero field. This proves the primitive
four-variable instance of [de Bondt, Theorem 5.2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1501.05168).

`PrimitiveTwoAxisQuasiTranslation` shows that a primitive homogeneous
direction `(0,0,A,B)` with zero acceleration is already binary. The two
entries are relatively prime. Their acceleration equations make `A`
divide its own fourth partial and `B` its own third partial, forcing those
partials to vanish by degree. The other two partials then vanish as well.
No binary-form or nonzero-entry assumption is needed for this step.

`IndependentCovectorPairCoordinates` constructs actual inverse matrices
from two independent original constant relations. It extends the
isomorphism of their two-dimensional spans to the whole constant space,
then constructs the full polynomial conjugacy with two zero direction
entries. A component-span bound of two supplies the two relations itself.

`QuasiTranslationSpanReduction` therefore needs only that span bound for
the positive-degree kernel branch. Its native original-map endpoint
constructs either a degree-zero primitive kernel, or a primitive positive
homogeneous rank-two quasi-translation whose components span at least
three original constant dimensions. The latter is now excluded by the
checked primitive four-variable instance of the
[Gordan-Noether span theorem, de Bondt Theorem 5.3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1501.05168),
as assembled below. The theorem is not assumed as an axiom or an unproved helper.

Two ingredients toward that span theorem are now checked.
`QuasiTranslationInvariantDivisors` uses the actual formal-parameter
identity to prove that the original directional derivation's invariants
are factorially closed. Every polynomial divisor of a nonzero invariant
is invariant under both the derivation and all actual polynomial
translations. `QuasiTranslationFiberDivisorTangency` constructs the finite
gcd cancellation for a primitive vector and uses the Nullstellensatz for
actual squarefree hypersurfaces. A hypersurface lying over a specified
image line divides the constant-direction derivative of every invariant;
a degree comparison makes that derivative zero. This proves the algebraic
divisibility-and-degree step of (5.2) in de Bondt's proof. The required
image-line hypersurfaces and sufficiently large direction family are
constructed in the following completion. The constant-direction
three-variable case, general rank-two map case, and final Theorems 1.1
and 1.2 remain incomplete.

### Proposition 2.1: the primitive positive classification and dependence branch are complete

`HomogeneousRankTwoProjectiveParameter` proves that any positive
homogeneous rank-two polynomial map has a projective ratio field of
transcendence degree one. The existing source-line and Lüroth arguments
construct an actual rational parameter. Euler weight zero gives a reduced
homogeneous source pencil `t=p/q`, with coprime positive-degree generators.
Clearing the four parameter-coordinate denominators constructs a primitive
polynomial vector `v(T)` and a nonzero original source scalar representing
the original direction as `r v(t)`. These constructions do not assume a
parametrization, image-line equations or a source pencil.

`HomogenizedParameterPencil` constructs the exact polynomial numerators
of `v_i(p/q)`, and their exact remainders modulo every literal pencil
member `p-a q`. `SourcePencilProjectiveFibers` derives divisibility of all
original cross equations by that literal member, cancelling denominator
powers through coprimality. Thus the entire zero locus, including points
where both pencil generators vanish, maps into the line of `v(a)`.

`PrimitiveQuasiTranslationSourcePencil` proves that the primitive parameter
vector has no finite base point and that some original constant cross
combination is nonzero for each parameter. Every pencil member divides
such a nonzero invariant. Factorial closure therefore makes every member
invariant, and values zero and one make both original generators invariant.
All members are nonzero and have the same actual homogeneous degree.

`GenericReducedHomogeneousPencil` constructs a finite set of exceptions
outside which all members are squarefree. A repeated irreducible factor
divides a fixed nonzero source Wronskian. Only finitely many normalized
prime factors occur, and coprimality allows each prime in at most one
parameter member. No generic squarefreeness or separability statement is
assumed.

The checked hypersurface tangency and degree obstruction now make both
pencil generators' constant derivatives vanish along every remaining
parameter direction. `CofinitePolynomialParameterKernel` turns these
cofinite incidences into the complete original coefficient equations.
`QuasiTranslationPencilGradientKernel` places both actual pencil gradients
in that coefficient kernel after extension to the original rational
source field. `HomogeneousPencilGradientIndependence` constructs their
independence from Euler contraction and the nonzero Wronskian.

`PrimitiveGordanNoetherSpan` shows that a coefficient kernel of dimension
at most one could not contain both gradients after scalar extension.
It therefore constructs two independent original constant kernel vectors.
Their polynomial parameter relations transfer to the original direction,
giving two independent constant component relations and a component span
of dimension at most two. This completes the primitive positive
four-variable Gordan-Noether bound over algebraically closed original
constants.

`PrimitiveQuasiTranslationClassification` constructs the inverse two-axis
coordinates, binary coprime homogeneous direction entries, and the full
positive primitive-kernel dependence conclusion. Its native original-map
endpoint proves that a hypothetical independent homogeneous nilpotent
rank-three map has a degree-zero primitive kernel, hence an actual
nonzero constant right-kernel vector. The only remaining closed-field
rank-three case is the homogeneous three-variable consequence of that
constant direction. Proposition 2.1 and the complete paper remain open
for that case, the separate general rank-two argument, field descent and
final theorem assembly.

### Constant-direction reduction, polynomial rank-two structure, and the full quasi-translation branch

`ConstantKernelSourceReduction` constructs inverse matrices from the
original nonzero constant direction. Every transformed component has
zero last partial, which constructs its actual smaller-source polynomial
representative. A split matrix quotient supplies leading-block
nilpotence, and injectivity of variable inclusion reflects it to the
smaller polynomial ring itself. These statements work in every
dimension and over the original characteristic-zero field.

`SmallerSourceDependenceTransport` lifts a leading smaller-source
constant relation through variable inclusion and inverse coordinates.
`OriginalRankThreeTernaryReduction` therefore constructs, from any
remaining independent original rank-three map over closed constants,
an independent homogeneous ternary nilpotent map of rank exactly two
and degree at least two. The exact interface with the homogeneous
ternary theorem is checked; that theorem has not been supplied or
proved, and Proposition 2.1 remains incomplete for this case.

`PrimitiveHomogenizedParameterVector` treats both finite parameter
values and infinity. The entire common-zero locus of the exact
homogenized vector lies in `p=q=0`. A common prime divisor would then
divide both original coprime pencil generators by the Nullstellensatz,
so the evaluated vector is primitive. `PrimitiveVectorRationalScalar`
cancels its components from an actual reduced scalar denominator and
constructs the original polynomial common factor.

`PolynomialSourcePencilFactorization` and
`HomogeneousRankTwoPolynomialPencil` assemble the native structure
theorem `H_i=g h_i(p,q)` over closed characteristic-zero constants.
Both pencil generators have the same positive degree `D`; all binary
forms have the same positive degree `N`; the nonzero common factor is
homogeneous of degree `c`; and `c+D*N=d`. No parametrization, binary
factorization, common factor or nilpotence hypothesis is supplied.
The remaining general rank-two nilpotence obstruction is not proved.

`HomogeneousQuasiTranslationSpan` uses factorial closure to remove an
arbitrary nonzero common polynomial factor while preserving zero
acceleration. Its reduced direction leaves that common factor invariant.
The primitive positive span theorem and the constant-direction case
therefore give the full four-variable component-span bound of two.
`QuasiTranslationDependenceDescent` extends actual coefficients to an
algebraic closure and descends the resulting constant component relation.
Thus every positive homogeneous four-variable quasi-translation has
dependent components over every original characteristic-zero field,
including the complete `(JH)^2 X=0` branch by Euler's identity.

### Rank-two nilpotence: the original hypotheses construct the normalized prime pencil and tangent minor

`NilpotentThreeVectorOrbit` uses the last nonzero iterate of the actual
vector to prove independence of `x`, `Nx`, and `N^2 x` whenever the
second is nonzero for a nilpotent matrix. No Jordan form is supplied.
The already checked quasi-translation branch makes `(JH)^2 X` nonzero
for any remaining independent original map. Polynomial evaluation
therefore constructs an original-field point with nonzero second
iterate. `IndependentVectorCoordinateColumns` extends its independent
orbit to actual inverse matrices. `PolynomialJacobianPointEvaluation`
and `NilpotentJacobianNormalizedPointFrame` derive the exact conjugated
Jacobian at that point, with first columns `e_1` and `e_2` in zero-based
indexing. The construction works over the original characteristic-zero
field and preserves homogeneity, nilpotence and independence.

`BinaryPencilPointNormalization` uses the nonzero output to construct
inverse binary parameter coordinates whose source values are exactly
`p(e_0)=0` and `q(e_0)=1`. Every homogeneous binary polynomial retains
its original source evaluation under this change.
`HomogeneousBinaryDehomogenization` constructs bounded univariate
representatives of the transformed forms.
`NormalizedRankTwoSourcePencil` thus keeps the entire primitive original
polynomial factorization and exact homogeneous degrees in the chosen
point chart; its common scalar is nonzero at that point.

`HomogenizedParameterSourceJets` transfers actual parameter powers to
source-pencil powers and derives the first source derivative at the
normalized point. `NormalizedJacobianParameterJets` uses Euler and the
two normalized columns to derive a nonzero numerator derivative and
the double inactive divisors `g*p^2 | G_0,G_3`.

`NilpotentPencilMinorDivisibility` checks the six-minor second-trace
identity. Every inactive minor retains `g*p`, including when `g` and
`p` have common factors. The actual nilpotent traces give the active
minor divisibility. `NormalizedPencilActiveMinor` constructs the active
quotient relatively prime to `p` from its nonzero linear parameter
coefficient, and cancels `g` before using that relative primality.
Consequently `p | det J_(1,2)(G_1,p)` follows from the original hypotheses.

`SimplePointPrimePencilFactor` selects an actual prime factor `delta`
through `e_0`. The nonzero numerator derivative makes that factor simple
at the same point, and its active tangent-minor equation is inherited.
`OriginalRankTwoPrimePencilFactor` assembles the native endpoint: the
original map constructs all coordinates, generators, degrees, prime
factor, its nonzero transverse derivative and its tangent minor.
Every transformed output belongs to the actual ideal `(delta,q)`;
`delta` itself does not divide the active output `G_1`.

The remaining rank-two step must prove the active output algebraic
modulo this prime over the two fixed source-coordinate functions, then
use the finite base components and the varying normalized frames to
derive a contradiction. Those conclusions are not assumed by any
exported proof. The general rank-two and homogeneous ternary dependence
theorems, and Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 as a whole, remain incomplete.

### Rank-two projection: the original pencil constructs a linear height-two base component

`PrincipalQuotientDerivation` descends an actual tangent derivation to
its actual principal quotient. `PrimeSourcePlaneDerivation` extends it
to the actual prime quotient fraction field, proves that its nonzero
transverse partial makes it nonzero, and proves the source dimension
bound of three. `FullDimensionDerivationConstants` then excludes three
algebraically independent constants for that nonzero derivation.
`PrimePencilProjectionRelation` constructs a nonzero relation among the
active output and the two fixed source coordinates modulo the original
prime numerator factor.

`FixedPlaneProjectionPolynomial` converts the relation into a polynomial
in the active output with coefficients in the fixed coordinate plane.
Removing its exact power of the polynomial variable preserves prime
divisibility and constructs a nonzero constant coefficient. That
coefficient belongs to the literal base ideal, with no radical-membership
hypothesis. `HomogeneousPencilProjectionEquation` extracts a nonzero
positive homogeneous fixed-plane equation in the same base ideal, even
when the prime numerator factor and denominator have different degrees.

`HomogeneousFixedPlanePrimeLinearRelation` uses the actual prime fraction
field and algebraically closed original constants to produce a nonzero
constant linear form in the two fixed coordinates. The exact height-two
minimal base prime is constructed by
`PencilProjectionLinearBaseComponent`. The native endpoint in
`OriginalRankTwoLinearBaseComponent` derives all this data from the
original homogeneous independent rank-two nilpotent map. It retains the
entire primitive polynomial factorization and proves that the resulting
prime is minimal over the original coprime pencil, not merely over its
selected prime-factor subpencil.

This completes the rank-two projection step. The remaining step must
exclude those fixed-plane linear forms simultaneously using the finite
original base components and varying normalized frames. No such exclusion
or final rank-two dependence is assumed or asserted by these proofs.
Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete and the full paper goal remains active.

### Rank-two moving frames: exact Wronskians and simultaneous exclusion for every actual base component

`PolynomialWronskianIndependence` proves that two independent original
parameter polynomials have nonzero Wronskian, with no coprimality input.
`ParametricDerivationMinor` proves the exact cancellation identity: for
outputs `s*f(t)` and `s*h(t)`, their minor against the actual derivative
is `s^2*D(t)` times the original polynomial Wronskian. All derivatives
of the common scalar cancel. Nonzero `D(t)` also constructs parameter
transcendence, so evaluation cannot kill that Wronskian.
`ParametricCovectorOrbitMinor` applies the identity to every independent
constant covector pair without prescribed orders or leading coefficients.

`NormalizedPencilOrbitMinor` proves that the original source vector field
differentiates the normalized pencil ratio nontrivially, directly from
the original normalized point values and numerator partial. Every
independent covector pair consequently has a nonzero original polynomial
minor on `H` and `(JH)H`. `OriginalRankTwoOrbitMinors` derives this entire
family of nonzero minors from the original rank-two hypotheses alone,
in coordinates that also retain a genuine linear height-two base
component. No differential nonvanishing or parameter transcendence is
supplied as an additional hypothesis.

`FiniteCovectorOrbitFrame` chooses one actual original-field source point
where any finite family of those minors and the second Jacobian orbit
are simultaneously nonzero. Euler's identity identifies the exact two
moving columns with `d*H` and `d*(JH)H` at that point. The point constructs
actual inverse coordinates with both normalized Jacobian columns and
all prescribed nonzero two-column determinants.
`CovectorPlaneFrameExclusion` shows that each such determinant excludes
every nonzero original linear form from the entire corresponding
covector plane from the fixed-coordinate polynomial ring.
`OriginalRankTwoFinitePlaneAvoidance` constructs this simultaneous
exclusion from the original hypotheses for any prescribed finite family.

`PrimeConstantCovectorDimension` constructs the actual subspace of
constant covectors whose linear polynomials belong to an original source
prime. Three independent forms would give three original coordinates in
a prime of height at most two after an actual invertible change, which
is impossible. Its entire constant covector space therefore has dimension
at most two. `TwoDimensionalCovectorCover` constructs an independent
covering pair even when this space has dimension zero or one.

`RankTwoPencilBaseFrameAvoidance` constructs the finite family of all
actual minimal primes of a given source pencil and covers each entire
constant-linear-form space by such an independent pair. One actual
normalized frame simultaneously excludes every nonzero original linear
form in every component of that same pencil base. The native endpoint
constructs this frame, the original normalized map, its actual coprime
pencil, and a genuine linear height-two base component from the original
rank-two hypotheses alone.

The remaining rank-two contradiction must compare the primitive pencil
base before and after changing frames. This comparison can be expressed
intrinsically through the primitive output vector, avoiding the need to
identify a newly chosen parameter pair with a prescribed pair. The
current proofs do not assume or assert that comparison or the final
rank-two dependence theorem. Theorems 1.1 and 1.2 remain incomplete.

### The full rank-at-most-two nilpotent dependence branch is checked over the original field

`PrimitiveVectorFactorUniqueness` proves in the original polynomial ring
that two primitive common-factor presentations of the same map differ by

vector exactly when it contains the other.

`IntrinsicPrimitivePencilBase` derives both the nonzero top parameter
coefficient and absence of a common finite parameter root from the

then shows that the prime contains all primitive evaluated components
exactly when it contains both original source generators. No supplied
identification of two separately chosen parameter pairs is used.

`PrescribedNormalizedPencilProjection` performs the projection argument
in the prescribed normalized coordinates. It constructs its own

`PrimitiveBaseConjugacy` compares this freshly constructed primitive
vector with the conjugate of the original primitive output vector.
`InverseFrameFixedPlaneCovector` constructs the exact nonzero original

`HomogeneousRankTwoNilpotentDependence` assembles the entire argument

### Theorem 1.1 is fully checked over every original characteristic-zero field

`TernaryQuasiTranslationDependence` treats the zero-second-orbit case
over the original field. Removing the common factor preserves zero
acceleration. The primitive positive ternary map would have an actual
height-one principal image prime containing all its components, which
contradicts primitivity. The remaining independent map therefore has a
nonzero second Jacobian orbit. `TernaryNormalizedPointFrame` constructs
the actual source point and inverse cyclic frame with the two normalized
Jacobian columns.

`GeneralHomogeneousProjectiveField`, `GeneralRationalSourceCurveField`
and `GeneralHomogeneousRankTwoPencil` extend the actual source-line,
Luroth, Euler-weight and primitive polynomial factorization proofs to
arbitrary finite source dimension. `TernaryNormalizedParameterJets`,
`TernaryNilpotentPencilMinor` and `TernaryNormalizedPencil` derive the
entire normalized ternary pencil and active tangent minor from the
original hypothetical counterexample.

`TernaryPrimeProjectionRelation` constructs the actual quotient-field
Hamiltonian derivation and a nonzero projection relation between the
active output and the one fixed source coordinate.
`TernaryFixedAxisProjectionPolynomial` removes the exact active-variable
root multiplicity to retain a nonzero constant term. Homogeneous
extraction stays in the literal original base ideal.
`TernaryFixedAxisPrime` proves that its fixed-axis homogeneous equation
is an exact scalar power and constructs an actual height-two base prime
containing the fixed coordinate itself.

`TernaryPrimeCovectorDimension` bounds the entire actual constant-linear
form space of a height-two prime by two. A prime containing all three
independent source forms would equal the origin maximal ideal, which
has height three. `TernaryCovectorPlaneCover`, `TernaryPencilOrbitMinor`,
`TernaryFiniteOrbitFrame`, `TernaryCovectorFrameExclusion` and
`TernaryPencilBaseFrameAvoidance` construct one actual normalized frame
simultaneously excluding every nonzero original linear form in every
original pencil base component.

`TernaryPrescribedPencilProjection` constructs the new primitive base
prime in that prescribed frame. `TernaryIntrinsicFrameContradiction`
compares its intrinsic primitive base with the conjugated original one
using actual polynomial-unit uniqueness and contracts it to an actual
original minimal base prime. The inverse-frame fixed coordinate is the
contradictory nonzero original covector.
`TernaryHomogeneousNilpotentDependence` therefore proves homogeneous
ternary nilpotent dependence in all positive degrees.
`OriginalFieldTernaryDependence` descends the actual relation to every
original characteristic-zero coefficient field.

`HomogeneousDependenceTheorem` applies that proved ternary theorem to
the checked original rank-three reduction and combines it with the
completed rank-at-most-two branch. Actual coefficient extension and
relation descent then prove the exact specification
`theorem_1_1 : DependenceTheorem K`. The explicit constant coefficient
relation is also exported as `theorem_1_1_original_constant_linear_relation`.
Theorem 1.1 has no additional geometric, classification, frame, rank or

### Theorem 1.2: original-field fixed-coordinate and normalized-core reduction

`FixedCoordinateReduction` uses the proved Theorem 1.1 to construct the
nonzero relation as an actual row of an invertible original constant
matrix. The corresponding perturbation has its final component zero;
its leading Jacobian block inherits nilpotence over the original
four-variable polynomial ring.

`LastCoordinateDehomogenization` identifies the actual final-coordinate
chart with the checked projective chart and commutes it with every active
derivative. It proves exact reconstruction in the prescribed degree,
including the final-coordinate power multiplying the removed constant.
`NormalizedFixedCoordinateReduction` constructs the normalized ternary
map in its own polynomial ring and proves normalization, the degree bound
and actual nilpotence of its Jacobian.

`FixedCoordinateTranslation` constructs the target correction as a
product of three actual elementary automorphisms and proves equation
(6.7) on the entire original polynomial algebra. `LinearTameConjugacy`
places the actual inverse constant coordinate changes in the tame
subgroup and proves the full conjugacy identity. It transports an actual
tame automorphism back to the original point map.
`FixedCoordinateTamenessReduction` assembles these results: the original
hypotheses construct an explicit normalized homogeneous core, and any
actual tame automorphism for that core restores to the original map.
This theorem does not assume or assert that the remaining classification
and core factorization arguments have been proved.

### Lemma 6.2: full constructive determinant-shear tameness

`VandermondeSubmodulePolarization` reconstructs coefficient vectors
using the actual inverse of the finite Vandermonde matrix on distinct
original-field scalars. In characteristic zero the integers `0,...,N`
give the required distinct parameters. The nonzero binomial coefficients
then put each binary monomial into the span of the shifted powers.
This calculation is valid after multiplication by an arbitrary fixed
coefficient and is used for both polarizations in the paper's proof.

`QuadraticInvariantFlow` constructs the actual polynomial automorphism
`T_h=(r+2vh+alpha*h^2,v+alpha*h,u,alpha)` for every polynomial parameter
in `(alpha*r-v^2,u,alpha)`. Direct polynomial identities prove the
invariant, the full parameter addition law and the inverse with parameter
`-h`. `QuadraticFlowElementaryParameters` factors each auxiliary-only
parameter into two actual elementary maps. `QuadraticFlowParameterConjugacy`
constructs the actual elementary auxiliary shear in (6.4).
`QuadraticFlowTameness` uses the checked finite polarization and scalar-uniform
tame parameter subspace to prove tame group membership for every invariant
polynomial parameter, with no supplied factorization assumption.

`SplitQuadraticShearFlows` constructs the second flow by an actual
original-field constant involution. `SplitDeterminantShearFactorization`
proves the two-flow product, both inverse constant coordinate matrices,
and the exact quadratic determinant identity. `DeterminantShearAlphaOnly`
transports this product back to the original `(r,s,alpha,beta)` coordinates
and proves the entire `K[Delta,alpha]` parameter case of Lemma 6.2.

`DeterminantShearFamily` supplies the actual determinant-shear automorphisms
and their addition law. `DeterminantShearCoefficientIdentity` checks the
coefficient-conjugacy calculation in an arbitrary commutative algebra.
`DeterminantShearCoefficientConjugacy` instantiates it with the actual
unipotent coefficient change, a product of two elementary maps.
`DeterminantShearCoefficientPolarization` supplies the second finite
polarization and every mixed coefficient monomial.

`ConstructiveDeterminantShearTameness` assembles all these checked inputs.
`DeterminantShear.automorphism4_tame` proves actual four-variable tame
subgroup membership for every polynomial in all three parameter variables.
`DeterminantShear.lemma_6_2` exports the exact original polynomial coordinate
formula, and `DeterminantShear.lemma_6_2_affine_coordinates` proves the
paper's assertion after affine coordinate changes. No classification,
polarization, flow, coordinate frame or tameness result is supplied as an
unproved extra input to these final theorems.

Theorem 1.2 remains incomplete. Its nonhomogeneous ternary classification,
general planar-to-shear reduction, remaining independent-branch and quintic
factorizations, and full assembly still require proofs. The paper goal
remains active.


### Propositions 6.4 and 6.6, and transport of the canonical tame word

`ConstantCoefficientShearTameness` treats constant coefficients using
an elementary active linear change and an elementary translation.
`CoprimeLinearShearCoordinates`, `LinearBaseShearFrame` and
`LinearCoefficientShearTameness` construct the nonsingular original-field
matrix from the two coprime linear forms. They check the sign change in
the second active coordinate and the inverse substitution on the entire
coefficient polynomial, then apply Lemma 6.2. `Proposition64LowDegree`
assembles these proofs with the inverse, Jacobian and stable tameness
results of `Proposition64`.

`HomogenizedTernaryNormalForm` constructs the polynomial parameters in
(6.12), their exact common degrees and the identity `t^(d-1) R = t q^2`.
`TernaryNormalFormFirstFactor` and `TernaryNormalFormElementaryFactors`
construct all factors of (6.14). `TernaryNormalFormCommutatorIdentity`
checks the commutator and the final cancellation as polynomial algebra
identities. `TernaryNormalFormTameWord` gives the actual tame automorphism
with the exact original polynomial coordinates.

`TernaryNormalFormAffineChart` recovers the affine canonical family.
`TernaryNormalFormJacobian` checks its full Jacobian and its cube.
`HomogeneousMatrixBlockNilpotence` lifts vanishing of a homogeneous
matrix power from the final-coordinate chart and proves the corresponding
full-block statement. `Proposition66` proves all claims of Proposition
6.6, including equality of the actual polynomial parameters with the
paper's rational formulas on the open chart.

`TernaryNormalFormDegreeBound` derives the necessary bound `4 deg P ≤ d`
from the actual affine degree bound, by specializing the square coordinate
along the x axis. `FixedCoordinateChartLinearConjugacy` proves that the
extended constant conjugation fixing `t` commutes with dehomogenization.
`OriginalMapTamenessFromTernaryNormalForm` transports the checked tame
word back to the original map, including the removed translation. This
implication uses the canonical form as a hypothesis. Theorem 6.1 is still
needed to construct that form for every independent ternary chart.

`PlanarShearCoefficientRing` identifies the original polynomial algebra
with `K[z,t][x,y]` and checks compatibility of the active derivatives.

### Proposition 6.3 and the quintic parameter

`PlanarNilpotentDependence` proves planar dependence for normalized
maps over every characteristic-zero field. It derives a rational slope
from the active Jacobian and uses the top homogeneous part of an
invariant polynomial to show that the slope is algebraic over the
coefficient field. The resulting constant relation descends from the
algebraic closure.

The normalized active pair is then taken over `K(z,t)`. The primitive
relation over `K[z,t]` determines the coprime homogeneous coefficients
and a single polynomial multiplier. Trace zero puts the multiplier in
the kernel of the constant active derivation. Specializing along both
active axes descends each invariant coefficient to `K[z,t]`. The
homogeneous coefficient calculation proves the individual degrees
`d-r-j(r+1)` and the bound `2r+1 ≤ d`.

`Shear.proposition_6_3` assembles both cases and the actual elementary
target translations. `QuinticPlanarParameter` derives the scalar
multiplier for `d = 5` and `r = 2`.

`PolynomialAutomorphismStabilization` adjoins a fixed variable as an
actual group homomorphism. It carries affine and elementary generators
into the corresponding larger tame subgroup. This construction keeps
the stable extension valid when restoring the original frames and
target translations. A constant relation on a dependent normalized
ternary chart now constructs two fixed coordinates, and the leading
planar block retains nilpotence. The resulting original automorphism is
one-stably tame in all degrees and tame through degree four.

`TernaryNilpotentClassification` states Theorem 6.1 explicitly.
`theorem_1_2_of_ternary_classification_and_quadratic_pencils` proves
the full implication from this statement and Corollary 6.10, with the
quadratic pencil statement used only for ordinary tameness through
degree five.

For Lemma 6.8, a nonzero leading coefficient minor gives both the
degree-two and simple-root conditions at one original-field point.
Coprimality guarantees such a minor, and the selected pencil member
factors into distinct original-field linear forms. For Lemma 6.9, the
change of active coordinates preserves the invariant and multiplies
the scalar shear parameter by the determinant.

### The quintic factorization and Theorem 1.2 assembly

`QuinticPolynomialCoordinates` proves the auxiliary coordinate
identities and recovers the original coordinates by cancellation of
the fixed nonzero polynomial `t`. This allows the composition and
inverse identities to be proved directly in `K[x,y,z,t]`.
`QuinticAuxiliaryFlowIdentities` and `QuinticInvariantFlowSign` check
both flows, the change in sign of the invariant under the involution,
and its cancellation against the sign of the inverse flow in (6.21).

`QuinticAuxiliaryOnlyFlowTameness` lifts the two elementary shifts
of (6.22) by Lemma 6.2. The first grouped conjugate uses the checked
triangular frame (6.30). The second uses the explicit polynomial flow
and its parameter-shifting word (6.40), with every factor in the
original tame subgroup. `QuinticGroupedTameWord` assembles the four
factors of (6.42). `Proposition67` gives the exact original
commutator, and `QuinticOriginalFlowFormula` checks the printed
coordinate polynomials (6.43) for the same lift.

`NormalizeBinaryQuadraticPencil` completes the two constant frames
of Lemma 6.8. `BinaryPencilActiveConjugacy` checks (6.45) in the full
polynomial ring. `Corollary610` restores both frames, and
`Proposition611` proves the planar and component-span conclusions.
`theorem_1_2_of_ternary_classification` supplies the exact Theorem 1.2
conclusion assuming Theorem 6.1. The full paper goal remains active.

`TopPolynomialMatrixCoefficients` computes the top coefficient of a
polynomial matrix power. `TernaryHighestHomogeneousPart` applies this to
the Jacobian in Theorem 6.1, including the derivative coefficient
identity and the existence of a nonzero leading homogeneous part of
positive degree.

### The generic ternary curve in Theorem 6.1

The homogeneous ternary triangular frame and the finite cover of the
leading form's common zeros by constant planes are checked over the
coefficient field used in the classification proof. In a chart with two
algebraically independent outputs, the actual cross-product derivation
has the relative algebraic closure of their field as its field of
constants. Lean verifies the plane and quadratic invariants, constructs
an irreducible principal generator of degree one or two for the projected
curve's entire relation ideal, and places every direction at infinity
in one of the constant planes.

The relative closure of a rank-one output field has a common polynomial
parameter. The specialized trace forces a constant component relation,
so the normalized independent case has rank two. A simultaneous
permutation supplies its independent output pair. If the projected
curve is a line, its direction has invariant entries and lies in a
constant plane. In coordinates fixing that plane, the slope is
algebraic over the fixed source coordinate. The trace and the constant
kernel reduction then rule out the line. The quadratic invariant itself
generates the full relation ideal of the resulting conic. Two distinct
directions at infinity would give a bilinear output in a constant
frame, which the nilpotent trace and principal minors exclude. The
repeated direction yields a constant conjugacy with the third output
algebraic over the first and the first two algebraically independent.

### The ternary normal form and original-field descent

The first and third components in this chart share a common closed
polynomial parameter. The trace and principal minors give a rational
derivation with a slice. The polynomial inverse identity for the
transverse derivative makes a cubic coefficient in the slice vanish.
The middle output is consequently affine in the two outer variables.
A reduced-denominator argument puts all its coefficients in the
ordinary polynomial parameter ring.

Specializing the trace constructs two affine tangent fields whose
commutator integrates to a quadratic graph parameter. The principal
minors give the exact quadratic relation on the two outer outputs.
The rank-two quadratic case would force a product of nonconstant
univariate polynomials to be constant. The rank-one case supplies
the explicit constant normalizer over algebraically closed constants.

The unique symmetric quadratic relation descends by an original-field
linear coefficient functional. Its square and linear covectors give
an original constant frame. The three remaining affine coefficients
are unique, and the restriction of the first output to the middle
coordinate axis recovers the original ordinary parameter polynomial.
This proves Theorem 6.1 over the original characteristic-zero field.
The checked Section 6 factorizations now prove Theorem 1.2 without a
classification hypothesis.
